# Croakus verification — 2026-10-11

Book: the 10-07 generator with the 10-08 audit (byte-identical to John's magnum_croakus_generator_2026-10-07.tgz). Code: GitHub FrogNet-Living-Network e889208 + John's frognet-2026-10-11 overlay (fdf r19-a15, Android a15, fdf-tun b11). Round 1: 11 verifiers, 347 problems. Round 2 (independent, after the fixes): 6 verifiers, 72 more. All applied except what is in OPEN-FOR-JOHN-2026-10-11.md.

# Verifier A: 00_front, Part 0, Spec A, Spec B, Part I, Part II (against the 2026-10-11 tree)

The conversation tools were available. TREE = /home/claude/tree1011. The FD&F source is TREE/opt/fdf/src/fdf-source, written FDF below.
I re-checked every 10-08 lead for these Parts. All 13 are still in the text, and each is re-reported below against today's code. Spec B (03_) has no problem.

## 00_front.md

**1. Where**: 00_front.md, Before We Start (the hometown block)
**Text**: "broker     streamingfrog.com:18257  (or a local one — Part III)"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: The local broker is covered in Part VII: 10_Part_VII_Connecting_Across_the_Internet.md, "#### The local broker — short-circuiting the LAN" and "#### The intranet case". No other part file has a "local broker" section (grep -il "local broker" parts/*.md returns only 10_). The port is correct: TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet-make-gateway.sh:61 has `PORT="18257"`, and deploy_broker_v4.sh:27 has "apache:18257".
**Fix**: "broker     streamingfrog.com:18257  (or a local one — Part VII)"

**2. Where**: 00_front.md, How This Book Is Shaped
**Text**: "It lives in a repository of its own, and that repository’s Discussions are open to anyone"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: The URL given is the code repository (github.com/FawcettJohnW/FrogNet-Living-Network). The book ships inside it, at TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/broker/var/www/html/Magnum Croakus.html and docs/magnum_croakus/build_magnum.js. The guild boxes in 02_ and 04_ say "this book lives in a public repository".
**Fix**: "It lives in the project’s public repository, and that repository’s Discussions are open to anyone"

**3. Where**: 00_front.md, How This Book Is Shaped (the two-doors box)
**Text**: "the engine, under licence"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: The guild boxes (02_, 04_) say "free software under the GNU GPL v2, no licence to request and no form". The source headers say `SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-only` (for example TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/cpp/include/frogram.hpp:2). "Under licence" reads as a licence to be requested.
**Fix**: "the engine, under the GPL v2"

## 01_Part_0_The_Architecture_Contract.md

**4. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0. Three Specifications, One Croakus (the same claim is in 0.2's Connect row: "Open the machine's socket to that RAM manager")
**Text**: "every participant opens one outbound socket to the RAM manager and speaks FNWP on it"
**Verdict**: WRONG (as a statement of what the code does)
**Evidence**:
- The FD&F Client is a `frogram::Session`: FDF/rinab/src/rina_region_client.hpp:153.
- A Session dials three outbound TCP connections: TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/cpp/src/frogram.cpp:222, 228 and 231 (request; `HELLO RETURN:` for replies; `HELLO DATA:` for the data socket). The header frogram.hpp:9-14 says "Three outbound connections".
- Each librina program also opens a second, independent set for its SDUs: FDF/rinab/src/librina_region.cpp:250-253 `[CHANNEL_SETS_V1]` and `data_set()` at 316-323.
- RESULTS.md:1092 says "Each SDU a program writes is ONE posted request on its own connection to the RAM manager".
- So there is one session per program, not one socket per machine. Every connection is outbound and goes to the RAM manager, never to another member. That part of the statement is correct.
**Fix**: "every participant program opens its own outbound FNWP session to the RAM manager (a request, a return and a data connection, all outbound) and never a connection to another member". In 0.2's Connect row: "Open this participant's session to that RAM manager; FNWP runs on it. Liveness is the session."

**5. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0.3 BLDC-1 and FNWP
**Text**: "sends a FULL when the peer lacks that shape"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/cpp/include/fnwp_engine.hpp:189-212. When no template matches (`tpl_.find` misses), the request goes `raw_locked` → REQ_RAW. When a template exists but the instance has no reference yet, it goes REQ_FULL.
- FDF/rinab/experiments/RESULTS.md:1325-1328: "`raw` is a message with no template; `full` is a templated message carrying all its values. The first message of each instance must be full."
- The template is the sender's own dictionary (primed or learned). It is not something the peer lacks.
**Fix**: "It learns an instance shape. A message with no matching shape goes RAW; the first message of an instance whose shape is known goes FULL, carrying all its values; a DIFF follows when fields change, and a REPEAT/SAME form when the learned instance is unchanged."

**6. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0.3 BLDC-1 and FNWP
**Text**: "under FD&F the transport opens one per machine to the RAM manager"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: Same as entry 4: frogram.cpp:222-238 and librina_region.cpp:250-253, 316-323. A session is opened per program, and each session is three TCP connections.
**Fix**: "under FD&F each participant program opens its own session to the RAM manager"

**7. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0.3 BLDC-1 and FNWP
**Text**: "an application may open its own, as the Communicator does for media"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- The Communicator's media does not travel on FNWP. It is fnav frames, `[len u32 BE][kind u8][srclen u16 BE][src utf-8][payload]`, sent to comms-media: TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator/docs/FNAV-SPEC.md:7-8 and src/fnav/frame.*.
- No FNW1 magic appears in src/fnav.
- The Communicator's FNWP use is its own session to comms-ram (the platform client).
**Fix**: "an application may open its own, as the Communicator does to its memory server, comms-ram (its media rides a separate wire, fnav, to comms-media)"

**8. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0.5 FD&F: Farinacci, Day & Fawcett
**Text**: "LISP resolves the current RAM-manager location. The manager self-registers its mapping when it starts or moves."
**Verdict**: STALE (this is the path proven in the sandbox; the deployed region does not use it)
**Evidence**:
- `locate_afresh` takes RINA_RAM first: FDF/rinab/src/rina_region_client.hpp:134-136.
- fdf-region-join writes `export RINA_RAM=165.232.153.144:51888`: TREE/opt/fdf/bin/fdf-region-join:13 and :26.
- The droplet unit sets no RINA_MAP_AREA (TREE/etc/systemd/system/fdf-ram-frognet.service). `register_self` then returns: rina_region.cpp:1004 `[QUIET_DIRECT_V1]` "direct mode: no registration intended".
- When an area name is used, the Map-Resolver's address is derived from it: SHA-256 into 198.18.0.0/15, with no DNS (rina_region_client.hpp:42-50 and 60-63, `[MR_ADDRESS_FROM_NAME_V1]`; John's option B, 10-07).
**Fix**: "A machine is given one thing: the area's name or the RAM manager's address. Given the name, LISP resolves the current RAM-manager location: the Map-Resolver's address is derived from the name (SHA-256 into a reserved prefix), never configured or looked up in DNS, and the manager self-registers its mapping when it starts or moves. The deployed region (r19-a15) gives each node the address outright: fdf-region-join writes RINA_RAM=165.232.153.144:51888."

**9. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0.5 FD&F: Farinacci, Day & Fawcett
**Text**: "Peer names and IPs may be known, but they are not used to create a second data plane."
**Verdict**: STALE (it omits the hub, which the deployed region depends on)
**Evidence**:
- FDF/docs/PLAN-FROGNET-ON-FDF.md:7-9: "Data still rides WireGuard, hub-and-spoke through the RAM manager's host."
- DEPLOY-REGION-2026-10-09.md:16-21: fdf-hub on the droplet, wg-frognet, UDP 50889.
- HANDOFF-REGION-2026-10-09.md:72: "n1 -> n2 ping THROUGH the hub". Phone and PC bridges also reach nodes "via hub StreamingFrog": WINDOWS-BRIDGE.md:59-61.
- The FD&F data rule still holds: nothing goes member to member.
**Fix**: Add after the sentence: "When FrogNet runs on an FD&F region, its 10/8 IP traffic rides WireGuard hub-and-spoke through the hub on the RAM manager's host (fdf-hub, wg-frognet, UDP 50889). The hub's peers and routes are written from the region's tuples, and still nothing goes member to member."

**10. Where**: 01_Part_0, 0.6 What Is Demonstrated, and What Is Not
**Text**: "Region Permanent records have survived a kill -9, and programs have reattached after RAM-manager restart with a reliable 200-of-200 flow."
**Verdict**: STALE (the section stops at the sandbox and leaves out both the hardware results and the failures). I checked the numbers this section does give, and they are correct: RESULTS.md:1098-1102, 1233, 1290, 1344-1345, 1427-1428, 800, 819-822, 424 and 666.
**Evidence**:
- Kart race on hardware: RESULTS.md:1620 (10-07, "It is working").
- Communicator through the droplet: RESULTS.md:1735-1750. 850x480 at 24 fps, 400-800 kb/s, between machines that cannot reach each other.
- The region on John's Pis: chat 5bf4812e (10-09/10, Seattle5/Seattle7 GRAPH lines).
- Windows bridge on hardware: WINDOWS-BRIDGE.md:55-61 (10-11).
- Phone Communicator: ANDROID-HANDOFF.md:44, John: "was responsive and worked properly".
- Not yet shown: the phone bridge on a handset (ANDROID-HANDOFF.md:24-25; PLAN-BRIDGE.md:66). Exp 3 has no FD&F pass: the 554/554 run was withdrawn because the topology was handed to it (RESULTS.md:1029-1039). Encryption is not built; only an HMAC on map_register (rina_region_client.hpp:72-80).
**Fix**: Append: "On real hardware: the kart race and a Communicator call (850x480 at 24 fps, 400–800 kb/s) between machines that cannot reach each other ran through the droplet on 2026-10-07; since 2026-10-09 John's FrogNet nodes run as one region with the RAM manager and hub on the droplet; a Windows PC joined as a bridge on 2026-10-11, and the Communicator ran on a physical phone. Not yet shown: the phone as a bridge on a handset, FrogNet's 20 discovery topologies under FD&F's own discovery, and encryption (the only cryptography is an HMAC on the mapping registration)."

## 02_Specification_A_Ribbit.md

**11. Where**: 02_Specification_A_Ribbit, R.2 Truth Ownership
**Text**: "The LISP conversion exposed why that distinction matters when one participant's heartbeat accidentally kept another participant alive."
**Verdict**: WRONG (misattributed)
**Evidence**:
- This defect was in the Communicator. In chat 22c2753a (08-29), `refresh_calls` rewrote the whole members list, so "Dave's heartbeat rewrites the row with John's id back in"; that led to [LEAVE_MEANS_FORGET_V1].
- The same class returned in the C++ Communicator: refresh_calls rewrote the announce row every 5 s and kept withdrawn sessions offered. Fixed by `[LEAVE_WITHDRAWS_THE_OFFER_V1]` (TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator/STATUS.md, r3 section).
- The LISP conversion's finding was different: a claimer re-published another participant's registration (chat d70bdef8, v0.41).
**Fix**: "The Communicator exposed why that distinction matters: one participant's heartbeat rewrote a shared membership row and reinstated a member who had left."

## 04_Part_I_FrogNet_What_the_Sovereign_Transport_Does.md

**12. Where**: 04_Part_I, opening paragraph
**Text**: "every one of these is a pattern the system runs today"

**Evidence**:

**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: As in entry 4: frogram.cpp:222-238 and librina_region.cpp:250-253.

**Text**: "It is not a migration. It is an insertion."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with 11_Part_VII½, "What has to be proved before this is a slide")
**Evidence**: 11_*.md says: "It is not yet proved ... Until that exists, this chapter is an argument rather than a demonstration." Part I presents the insertion under "a pattern the system runs today".

**15. Where**: 04_Part_I, 1b If You Already Have a Network (last paragraph)
**Text**: "are in Plumbing It Into a Network That Already Exists, in the part on connecting across the internet"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: §22b "Plumbing It Into a Network That Already Exists" is in 11_Part_VII½ "Adopting It Where a Network Already Exists". It is not in 10_Part_VII "Connecting Across the Internet".
**Fix**: "are in Plumbing It Into a Network That Already Exists, in Part VII½"

**16. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (See live video)
**Text**: "with no call server in the middle and no account anywhere"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: Every call goes through the media server comms-media (:8994):
- STATUS.md (communicator): "John sees Donna at 1280x720 ... through comms-media".
- RESULTS.md:1749: "everything between them passing through its RAM and media servers".
- The Communicator's servers are comms-ram :8800 and comms-media :8994 (brief; fdf-comms.sh).
**Fix**: "with no account anywhere and no service outside hardware you own: the call's memory and media servers (comms-ram, comms-media) run on one of your own boxes"

**17. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (See live video)
**Text**: "full video, to reduced video, to clean audio, to text"
**Verdict**: WRONG (for the current Communicator)
**Evidence**:
- comms-app's ladder: L8..L5 are the 16:9 sizes (src/comms/call.cpp:34-37).
- Below L5 the picture shrinks through 640x360, 480x360, 320x240 and 160x120, then goes audio only (call.cpp:232-244, 304-307, `[BOTTOM_RUNG_SHRINKS_V1]`).
- There is no text rung. Chat is a separate room function (src/comms/room.*).
**Fix**: "it steps down — full video, to smaller pictures (down to 160x120), to audio only — and climbs back when the link recovers"

**18. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (See live video)
**Text**: "And the rung is chosen by queue pressure at the far end rather than by a bitrate table: if the relay cannot get keyframes out, the rung does not fit, whatever the arithmetic says."
**Verdict**: WRONG (this describes the replaced Python ladder)
**Evidence**:
- The sender's rung comes from its own send path: TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator/src/fnav/bearer.hpp:4-9 (John's rule, 2026-08-03). It goes down on audio shed, more than 3 dirty samples (a drop, or a backlog of 8 or more), more than 5 drops in a second, or a low frame rate. It goes up after 3 clean seconds at full frame rate.
- The media server picks each viewer's size from that viewer's measured link (src/media/server.cpp control_tick; STATUS.md "per-viewer rate from the MEASURED link").
- This sentence is correct and stays: "Keyframes ... are held and delivered ahead" (server.cpp:307-314 held_key, `[KEYFRAME_ANCHOR_V1]`).
**Fix**: "And the rung is chosen by what the wire does, not by a bitrate table: the sender steps down when its frames back up or are dropped or its frame rate falls, and climbs only after clean seconds at full rate; the media server sizes each viewer's picture to that viewer's measured link."

**19. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (Stretch one uplink)
**Text**: "because the wire carries only differences, that single shared uplink goes much, much further than its raw bandwidth suggests — a narrow satellite link can feed a working network of dozens"
**Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED (no run exists) and WRONG in mechanism
**Evidence**:
- BLDC-1 works only on traffic between FrogNet nodes (proxy/daemon, FNWP). A host that is not FrogNet is "just another box on the wire": TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/core/not_frognet.py:19-23.
- Ordinary traffic to the internet through the uplink is not semantically compressed.
- No chat or code has a shared-satellite-uplink run (10-08 search; today's grep finds nothing).
**Fix**: "Plug it into one FrogNet node and the mesh spreads that one uplink outward over Wi-Fi and radio to every other node in range. Traffic between the boxes themselves carries only differences, so the mesh's own coordination costs the shared pipe very little."

**20. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (Replace a stack of subscriptions)
**Text**: "Photos, family chat, a shared calendar, a map of where everyone is, video calls"
**Verdict**: STALE / UNSUPPORTED
**Evidence**:
- The family calendar was removed: TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet_prune_removed.sh:262 `gone "var/www/html/family-calendar"`; install_databasehost.sh:120 refers to the old Communicator's games/calendar.
- No photo or location-map application exists in the tree.
- Chat and video calls exist (the Communicator, examples/communicator).
**Fix**: "Family chat and video calls — the things you pay companies for every month — run on your own boxes instead, private, with no per-person fees and nobody monetizing you on the side; anything else that is shared state is one Region away."

**21. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (Keep working when the internet doesn't)
**Text**: "When the internet comes back, they catch up."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with the doctrine and Part VI §18)
**Evidence**: Memory is not migrated or reconciled across a split or merge: FDF-INVENTORY F9 (chat bc224a1a), "memory not migrated or reconciled — doctrine". Part VI §18 says the islands merge.
**Fix**: "When the internet comes back, the islands merge; each box re-asserts its own truth into the one shared space, and nothing has to be reconciled."

**22. Where**: 04_Part_I, 2. What It Lets You Do (Run real software over almost nothing)
**Text**: "the patient vitals reaching the hospital ahead of the ambulance"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: This passage refers to "the narrow-link examples above", but no example above mentions patient vitals or an ambulance (04_ lines 119-149).
**Fix**: Delete "the patient vitals reaching the hospital ahead of the ambulance, ".

**23. Where**: 04_Part_I, 3. On the Record
**Text**: "Every capability in this part has been exercised on real hardware over real links"
**Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
**Evidence**: Same as entries 12, 19 and 20.
**Fix**: "The load-bearing demonstrations below have been exercised on real hardware over real links; the other patterns above are built from the same mechanisms"

**24. Where**: 04_Part_I, 3. On the Record ("And not staged")
**Text**: "across the open internet for a year and a half"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: Chat 230e2383 (08-26). The site says "two years" on five surfaces. John: "Change the fucking croakus".
**Fix**: "across the open internet for two years"

**25. Where**: 04_Part_I, 1. And it is invisible
**Text**: "There are no discoverable application endpoints and no exposed application protocols — a scanner sweeping for something to talk to on the application layer does not find one."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with the deployed FD&F region this book describes in 0.5)
**Evidence**:
- The droplet's RAM manager listens publicly with `rina-host --listen 0.0.0.0:51888`: TREE/etc/systemd/system/fdf-ram-frognet.service.
- That port is opened to the internet (`ufw allow 51888/tcp`) and it answers plain HTTP POSTs: DEPLOY-REGION-2026-10-09.md:12-13 and 26.
- The hub listens on UDP 50889. John's stance is that he neither forces nor prevents security.
**Fix**: Add: "On a FrogNet node this holds. An FD&F region is different by design: its RAM manager is a reachable listener (the droplet's rina-host on TCP 51888), because every participant connects up to it."

## 05_Part_II_Runs_on_What_You_Have.md

**26. Where**: 05_Part_II, opening paragraph
**Text**: "a five-dollar-an-hour cloud instance"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: The droplet is priced per month. Chats 17c2ccb5 and 09c16cfd: "$4/month basic Droplet", "Starts around $5/month". 10_Part_VII says "a few dollars a month".
**Fix**: "a five-dollar-a-month cloud instance"

**27. Where**: 05_Part_II, 4. One World, Any Box
**Text**: "The whole userland is Python, PHP, and bash riding the usual Linux services"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**:
- The Ribbit platform is C++ (TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/cpp), and so are the FD&F binaries (TREE/opt/fdf/bin: rina-host, fdf-client, fdf-events, ram).
- The installer installs `build-essential gcc g++ make`: TREE/usr/local/bin/installer/frognet_install.sh:421.
**Fix**: "The userland is Python, PHP, bash and C++ (the Ribbit platform and the FD&F binaries) riding the usual Linux services"

**28. Where**: 05_Part_II, 4. One World, Any Box (i386 bullet)
**Text**: "An old i386 desktop — the fifteen-year-old tower in the closet."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- frognet_install.sh:795-797 classes every non-ARM host as x86 and compares only ARM with non-ARM. An i386 host with an x86-64 venv is kept, not rebuilt.
- No i386/i686 handling exists in the tree.

**Fix**: "An old x86-64 desktop — the tower in the closet. Slow is still a node; FrogNet does not need much."

**29. Where**: 05_Part_II, 4. One World, Any Box
**Text**: "if the box's processor does not match the architecture the world was built for, the installer rebuilds the parts that care"
**Verdict**: WRONG (overbroad)
**Evidence**: frognet_install.sh:793-797: `REBUILD_VENV=1` only when ARM-ness differs.
**Fix**: "if the box is ARM and the world was built on x86-64 (or the reverse), the installer rebuilds the parts that care"

# Report B — Parts III, IV, V (06_, 07_, 08_)

Conversation tools were available and used. Code paths are relative to TREE = /home/claude/tree1011.
The book text of these three parts is unchanged from the 10-08 pass; every 10-08 item below was re-checked against today's code and still holds unless noted. New items are marked NEW.

## Part III — 06_Part_III_Hooking_Up_the_Network.md

1. **Where**: §5 "Wired Ethernet", worked example "ShopBox joins HomeBase by cable"
   **Text**: "Nothing else is required — they share a broadcast domain, so the next discovery walk on either box sees the other as an immediate peer"
   **Verdict**: WRONG
   **Evidence**: Each node holds the .1 of its own /24 and runs dnsmasq DHCP on it (usr/local/bin/frognet-netstart:249-264, 296-298). Immediate peers come only from DHCP leases inside the node's own served /24, 10.x ARP neighbours, and the .1 of a /24 on which the node holds a non-.1 lease (opt/frognet_semantic/discovery/discovery.py:1152-1206). Two .1s of different /24s on one switch match none of these, and two DHCP servers share the wire. The simulator's two-node shape needs a separate peering /24 and calls the one-segment version "invalid" (opt/frognet_semantic/simulation/frognet_sim.py:945-957). John (b8e7ee1d, 07-12): "A FrogNetHost will only serve one network."
   **Fix**: "Run a cable from ShopBox's uplink port into the segment HomeBase serves. ShopBox takes a lease from HomeBase's DHCP, and the next walk on each box sees the other as an immediate peer through that lease."

2. **Where**: §7 "How a LAN Forms — and Splits", worked example
   **Text**: "You plug ShopBox into the switch; on its next walk HomeBase probes its wire, gets an answer from 10.84.84.1"
   **Verdict**: WRONG (same cause as item 1)
   **Evidence**: discovery/discovery.py:1152-1206, as item 1.
   **Fix**: "You cable ShopBox's uplink into HomeBase's segment; ShopBox takes a HomeBase lease, and on its next walk HomeBase finds 10.84.84.1 through that lease, proves it, and writes it into the hosts file."

3. **Where**: §5 "Onboard Wi-Fi"
   **Text**: "A link watcher fires it the instant you plug or unplug"
   **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
   **Evidence**: ifplugd is set to ARGS="-q -f -u3 -d10 -w -I": 3 s after link up, 10 s after link down (usr/local/bin/installer/frognet_install.sh:2015-2019).
   **Fix**: "A link watcher (ifplugd) runs it about 3 seconds after you plug in and 10 seconds after you unplug"

4. **Where**: §5 worked example "Turning the projected SSID off, after the fact"
   **Text**: "Set the preference with the v4 setup helper and frognet-netstart honors it on its next run"
   **Verdict**: WRONG
   **Evidence**: ssid_projection_set calls frognet-ssid-projection, which runs frognet-netstart immediately and masks hostapd on "off" (usr/local/bin/frognet-ssid-projection:69-89). Its _write_state rewrites the whole file with only SSID_PROJECTION= (54-66). That deletes AP_IFACE and WIFI_COUNTRY, and netstart then uses wlan0 and US (frognet-netstart:216-228).
   **Fix**: "Set it with the v4 setup helper; the helper applies it at once by running frognet-netstart. In this build the helper rewrites ssid_projection.conf with the on/off switch alone, so a chosen AP interface and country go back to wlan0 and US."

5. **Where**: §5 worked example, last sentence
   **Text**: "This switch governs only the node's own onboard radio."
   **Verdict**: WRONG
   **Evidence**: Projection runs on AP_IFACE, which the installer lets you set to any AP-capable device, e.g. a USB adapter (frognet_install.sh:163-164, 1536-1580, 1949-1950; frognet-netstart:222-231, 286).
   **Fix**: "This switch governs only the radio the node projects its own SSID on (AP_IFACE, wlan0 unless you chose another)."

6. **Where**: §5 "The differentiator"
   **Text**: "the topology changed under the mesh block by block and the walk re-formed it, nobody touching anything"
   **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
   **Evidence**: John's record of the Queens drive is a 900 MHz HaLow node at traffic speed with its uplink held and no observed drops, while serving its own AP to ESP32 sensors (072f3954, 07-06 doctrine; e0066f89, 05-09). No chat or log describes the walk re-forming the mesh block by block.
   **Fix**: "Dan's trunk-mounted node through Queens is this at traffic speed: a 900 MHz HaLow uplink held through the city while the same node served its own access point to sensors, with nobody touching anything."

7. **Where**: §6 "The four scopes"
   **Text**: "Chorus is a named group of nodes that belong together across the pond, wherever they physically sit — “family,” “ops,” the circle the broker actually meshes over the internet"
   **Verdict**: WRONG
   **Evidence**: John (accef35e, 05-22): "There is the pond-sized chorus of all lillypads, and there are subnet choruses controlled by the admin." A chorus is a group of lillypads, not always named and not defined by the broker.
   **Fix**: "Chorus is a group of lillypads: the pond-wide chorus of all of them, or a named chorus an administrator defines — “family,” “ops.”"

8. **Where**: §6 "Resolving names, and the default route", paragraph 1
   **Text**: "FrogNet does not run DNS for the mesh"
   **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
   **Evidence**: Every merge writes dnsmasq per-domain forwarders server=/<domain>/<ip> from the host table and SIGHUPs dnsmasq when /etc/hosts changes, so dnsmasq on the .1 serves the mesh names as DNS ([DNSMASQ_FORWARDERS_V1], [DNSMASQ_RELOAD_V1], discovery/live.py:1268-1300).
   **Fix**: "The mesh's names live in /etc/hosts. The node's dnsmasq serves that file as DNS on its .1 and forwards each remote node's domain to that node (server=/<domain>/<ip>), both rewritten on every merge."

9. **Where**: §6 "Resolving names, and the default route", paragraph 2
   **Text**: "writes the result into /etc/hosts, but only its own lines: it replaces the FrogNet-managed entries (the peers and the <role>.frognet names) and leaves everything else in the file untouched"
   **Verdict**: WRONG
   **Evidence**: build_etc_hosts returns a fixed header, "127.0.0.1 localhost", and the normalized 10.x FrogNetHost./FrogNetAdmin. lines; the role lines are appended after (discovery/hosts.py:39-44, 162-190; discovery/orchestrate.py:171-185). _commit_hosts writes that list over the whole of /etc/hosts (discovery/live.py:1248-1266). Any other line is dropped.
   **Fix**: "Discovery owns /etc/hosts. On every merge it writes the whole file from the walk's result: localhost, the FrogNetHost/FrogNetAdmin line for each node, and the .frognet role names. Do not keep hand entries there."

10. **Where**: §6 "Resolving names, and the default route", paragraph 3
    **Text**: "the default route, and FrogNet leaves it alone on purpose"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: Every merge runs FixDefaultRoute (discovery/live.py:383-400, 931). It purges dead defaults, installs off-LAN defaults at metric 601, and when the node has no uplink of its own it lays a ranked default-route ladder through attached peers that advertise a live exit; in the file's words, "the default route walks the mesh out to the WAN edge one hop at a time" (discovery/fixdefault.py:19-38, 53). John (accef35e): "managed automatically by fixDefaultRoute every merge cycle".
    **Fix**: "Discovery manages the 10/8 routes, and it also keeps a working default route. A node with its own uplink keeps that default, dead defaults are removed, and a node with no uplink takes its default through an attached peer that advertises a live exit. Non-mesh traffic and ordinary DNS leave by that default."

## Part IV — 07_Part_IV_Installing_the_Software.md

11. **Where**: §8 "The World and the Installer", paragraph 1
    **Text**: "frognet_reset.sh, which takes a node back to what the installer found"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: The reset wipes the FrogNet install, state, identity and databases and removes /usr/local/bin whole (usr/local/bin/installer/frognet_reset.sh:20-33). The installer runs it itself, at phase A0, on any box that already carries an install, unless --preserve is given (frognet_install.sh:330-381, 66-72).
    **Fix**: "…and frognet_reset.sh, which wipes the FrogNet install, state, identity and databases. The installer runs it itself on a box that already has FrogNet, so a reinstall lands on a clean machine; --preserve upgrades in place instead."

12. **Where**: §8, paragraph 1 and the worked example
    **Text**: "You run it as root and answer four things:"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: The installer asks for the pond-wide FrogUser DB password before phase A unless --db-pass is given (frognet_install.sh:290-323). It asks for --pond whenever a broker is set (1758-1770). It asks for a pond-wide WPA2 passphrase whenever SSID projection is on and /etc/frognet/wifi.env has none (1599-1625). Run as printed, the example prompts for all three.
    **Fix**: Add "--pond hometown" to the example and: "The installer also asks for the FrogUser database password, which every node in the pond shares (--db-pass), and, when it will project an SSID, the pond's one Wi-Fi passphrase (--wifi-psk)."

13. **Where**: §9 "What the Installer Does", phase A
    **Text**: "The only phase that wants the internet, and only the first time."
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: A1 runs apt-get update on every install (frognet_install.sh:382-384). C2 pip-installs the venv's packages whenever it rebuilds the venv (frognet_install.sh:787-826). A also contains A0, the reinstall reset (330-381).
    **Fix**: "A — Reset and packages. Wipes a prior install (unless --preserve), then apt and pip dependencies. It refreshes the package index every run."

14. **Where**: §9, phase C
    **Text**: "C — Extract the world. Unpacks frognet_world.tgz; if the box's architecture differs from the build's, rebuilds the Python venv in place"
    **Verdict**: STALE (NEW)
    **Evidence**: C1 lays the world from the tar or a repository checkout (--from-repo) and mints the GUID; C1b injects the DB secret; C1c consolidates config; C2 restores the venv carried across the reset and rebuilds it only on an arch mismatch; C3 runs `python -m discovery selfcheck` on the installed code and refuses to activate the node if it fails (frognet_install.sh:557-905, 649-656, 843-905).
    **Fix**: "C — Lay down the world. Unpacks frognet_world.tgz (or copies a repository checkout), mints the node GUID, injects the DB secret, rebuilds the Python venv only if its architecture does not match the box, then runs the discovery self-check on the installed code and stops the install if it fails."

15. **Where**: §9, phases D and E
    **Text**: "D — Clean and configure. Wipes stale state from a cloned box, then writes this node's real configuration — name, address, broker, identity." / "E — Provision. Brings up the services, generates local SSL, applies tuning."
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: D1-D8 are interfaces, stale state, interface overrides, MariaDB, Apache, dnsmasq skeleton, NetworkManager, schema/FrogUser (frognet_install.sh:908-1393). Name and IP are E1, hostname E2, broker/WireGuard E3, then admin site, SSL, iptables, Wi-Fi AP prerequisites, SSID projection, link watcher (E4-E8, 1397-2028). E3 runs setup_lillypad with --norestart; services come up on the reboot and are enabled in F1; tuning is F7 (1806-1809, 2036, 2192).
    **Fix**: "D — Clean and configure: interfaces, stale state, database, web server, DHCP, NetworkManager, schema. E — Identity and provision: name and address, hostname, broker and WireGuard keys, SSL, iptables, SSID projection, the link watcher. F — Finalize: enable services, logging, cron, verify, system tuning, reboot."

16. **Where**: §9 "Telling a healthy node from a sick one"
    **Text**: "the liveness check is the bidirectional ping-pong the discovery walk runs, and a candidate counts as reachable only when its daemon answers it"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: A relayed path is proved by the reflect probe on :18432, answered by the proxy, not the daemon (discovery/descend.py:196-223; proxy/proxy_main.py:770-810; proxy/constants.py:60). The :9009 ping-pong to the daemon proves direct and tunnel paths ("reflect chain for relayed (via) incumbents, :9009 pong bound to the incumbent dev for direct/tunnel ones", discovery/discovery.py:812-830).
    **Fix**: "the liveness checks are the ones the walk runs: a reflect probe for a relayed path and the bidirectional :9009 ping-pong to the daemon for a direct or tunnel path."

17. **Where**: §10 "Configuring from the Browser"
    **Text**: "Set or clear the broker — write or remove tunnel.conf (host, port, pond, password, choruses)"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: clear_broker unsets BROKER_URL, POND_NAME, GROUP_NAME, POND_PASSWORD, CHORUSES and keeps the file, because "deleting the file would take GROUP_TOKEN and NODE_GUID with it" (usr/local/bin/frognet_setup_v4_helper.bash:151-166). apply_broker always writes an https:// URL (127-129).
    **Fix**: "Set or clear the broker — write or remove the broker fields in tunnel.conf (host, port, pond, password, choruses)"

18. **Where**: §10
    **Text**: "Register the node with the broker, and generate a join QR code — point a phone at it to bring a device onto the pond without typing a thing."
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: The page's API has no register action: state, scan-wifi, auto-ip, apply-identity, connect-wifi, disconnect, broker-state, apply-broker, clear-broker, reboot, qr, ssid-projection-state, apply-ssid-projection (var/www/html/frognet_setup_v4_api.php:76-160). The QR encodes http://<ip>/setup_frognet.html so the operator can get back to the page after the reboot (var/www/html/setup_frognet.html:612-625). register is a helper CLI subcommand only (frognet_setup_v4_helper.bash:168-215).
    **Fix**: "Set the node's name and address, and join it to a Wi-Fi network as a client. After you apply, the page shows a QR code of the node's new setup address, so you can scan your way back to it after the reboot. (Broker registration is a helper command, not a page button.)"

19. **Where**: §11 "The Configuration Files", table
    **Text**: "/etc/frognet/tunnel.conf             GROUP_TOKEN (membership)      no — from redeem"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: Tunnel setup writes GROUP_TOKEN= empty (usr/local/bin/frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:264-270). The broker has no group-token check; registration is authenticated by the pond password and identified by the GUID ([NO_GROUP_TOKEN_CRITICAL_V1], opt/frognet_semantic/internet_tunnels_v3/config.py:559-568).
    **Fix**: Delete the row. If membership is wanted in the table: "/etc/frognet/tunnel.conf  NODE_GUID, pond password  no — set at install / via helper".

20. **Where**: §11 table
    **Text**: "/etc/frognet/ssid_projection.conf    SSID on/off, wifi country     via helper/page"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: The helper and page set only on/off, and their write drops AP_IFACE and WIFI_COUNTRY (frognet-ssid-projection:54-66). Country and AP interface are set at install (frognet_install.sh:1536-1597).
    **Fix**: "/etc/frognet/ssid_projection.conf  SSID on/off, AP interface, country  on/off via helper/page; interface and country at install"

21. **Where**: §11 table
    **Text**: "/etc/frognet/gateways.conf           gateway / uplink facts        rarely, by hand"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: setup_lillypad_v4.bash deletes and rewrites it with NETWORK_NAME, GATEWAY_IP (the node's own .1), ADMIN_IP (its .2), GW1, GW2 (usr/local/bin/setup_lillypad_v4.bash:212, 631-639). It holds no uplink facts. The boot fixup reads it to re-assert the .1/.2 (654-660).
    **Fix**: "/etc/frognet/gateways.conf  node name, its own .1 and .2  no — written by setup_lillypad"

22. **Where**: §11, table and paragraph
    **Text**: "The two you will actually touch are tunnel.conf and ssid_projection.conf"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (omission)
    **Evidence**: /etc/frognet/wifi.env (FROGNET_WIFI_PSK, pond-shared, mode 600) is written at install whenever projection is on (frognet_install.sh:1599-1625), and frognet-netstart dies without it (frognet-netstart:106-113).
    **Fix**: Add the row "/etc/frognet/wifi.env  AP WPA2 passphrase (pond-shared)  set at install (--wifi-psk)".

23. **Where**: §12 "Validate It in the Simulator", paragraph 1
    **Text**: "FrogNet's simulator runs the real discovery, routing, and election code over a network you describe"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: The Topology/FrogNode builder used in the example is simulation/frognet_sim.py. Its discover_routes and sync_interfaces are a model (frognet_sim.py:646, 782-800), and live_engine.py:23 says "frognet_sim.py Section 2a is a *model* of planner.py/committer.py". It runs no election. The harness that runs the real discovery engine is opt/frognet_semantic/discovery/sim (system.py, a different API), and the installer's C3 gate runs it.
    **Fix**: "The topology builder below models discovery and routing so you can check a shape in seconds. The real discovery engine runs against shapes in discovery/sim (the same self-check the installer runs before it activates a node), and the builder's shapes also run against a real kernel (below)."

24. **Where**: §12 worked example "A two-node site, modelled and converged"
    **Text**: "#  gate learns:  10.10.20.0/24 via 10.10.20.1 dev eth1"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: I ran the example as printed against today's frognet_sim.py (FROGNET_PROXY_ROOT/FROGNET_BIN_ROOT set to TREE). Output: "converged in 2 cycle(s)", "route 10.10.20.0/24 via 10.10.99.2 dev eth1 metric 22 [lan rtt=2.0ms]", "REACHABILITY: all 4/4 (production+admin) pairs OK". The next hop is shed's peering address, not its served .1.
    **Fix**: "#  gate learns:  10.10.20.0/24 via 10.10.99.2 dev eth1" and "#  every node reaches every other; converges in 2 cycles"

25. **Where**: §12 "Try a shape before you build it"
    **Text**: "More than twenty worked shapes already ship with the simulator"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: frognet_sim.py has exactly 20 topo_* builders (lines 945-1529); there are no others in the tree.
    **Fix**: "Twenty worked shapes ship with the simulator — pairs, chains, stars, rings, ponds, hub-and-spoke, mixed bearers, tunnel transits"

26. **Where**: §12 "Try a shape before you build it", last paragraph
    **Text**: "Convergence itself is cheap: a small, constant number of cycles for any connected shape, however wide."
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: Running all 20 shapes today: chain_3 3 cycles, chain_5 5, ring_4 3, asym_chain_wg_star 5; star (5 nodes) 2, pond_full_mesh 2, pond_hub_spoke 2. Cycles grow with the shape's length (diameter), not its width.
    **Fix**: "Convergence itself is cheap: a wide shape converges in two or three cycles; a long chain takes about one cycle per hop (five for a five-node chain)."

## Part V — 08_Part_V_Discovery.md

27. **Where**: Part V intro
    **Text**: "Discovery is HTTP, riding the same compressed proxy your applications ride."
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: The echo and getHosts crawl use MarkedHTTPConnection with SO_MARK=1, so the iptables rule RETURNs and the request goes straight to the neighbour's port 80, bypassing the local proxy ([ECHO_SOMARK_DIRECT_V1], [GETHOSTS_SOMARK_DIRECT_V1], discovery/real_backends.py:107-131, 161-183; etc/setup_iptables:77).
    **Fix**: "Discovery is HTTP to the neighbours' own web servers, sent past the local proxy so a walk never queues behind production traffic."

28. **Where**: §13 "We Eat Our Own Cooking", paragraph 1
    **Text**: "Both are plain HTTP, both compressed by the same engine as everything else."
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: As item 27. The one discovery request that does go through the local proxy is the RTT measure: frognet_alive.bash curls http://127.0.0.1:80/frognet_echo.php with the peer's Host header (usr/local/bin/frognet_alive.bash:31-40; RealRtt, real_backends.py:146-152).
    **Fix**: "Both are plain HTTP to the neighbour's port 80, sent direct and uncompressed (marked to bypass the local proxy). The round-trip measurement that ranks paths is the one discovery request that rides the local proxy."

29. **Where**: §13, paragraph 1 (NEW: region mode)
    **Text**: "Two ordinary web endpoints do almost all the work."
    **Verdict**: STALE (NEW)
    **Evidence**: On a node joined to an FD&F region (RINA_RAM= in /opt/fdf/var/join.env or /etc/frognet/tunnel.conf), the merge takes "who exists" from one read of the region's participant records instead of the echo/getHosts crawl, and logs "DISCOVERY_SOURCE=region … (no crawl: FD&F owns discovery)" (discovery/live.py:499-511; RegionRecords/RegionEcho/RegionGetHosts, discovery/real_backends.py:500-600). frognet_echo.php and getHosts.php answer from the region facts in that mode (var/www/html/frognet_echo.php; var/www/html/getHosts.php region branch). Path probes are unchanged. John's plan (5bf4812e, 10-10): "Discovery: region backends behind FROGNET_DISCOVERY_SOURCE=region".
    **Fix**: Add after the paragraph: "On a node joined to an FD&F region, the region already holds every member's own facts, so the merge reads the member set from the region in one call and does not crawl. The probes that measure paths — reflect, the :9009 ping-pong, the round-trip measure — run the same either way: the region says who exists, the probes say what is reachable."

30. **Where**: §13, paragraph "That pays off twice"
    **Text**: "the mesh finds itself over a 4800-baud radio link as readily as over Ethernet"
    **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
    **Evidence**: The sentence rests on discovery riding the compressed proxy, which item 27 shows it does not. No chat records a 4800-baud RF run of discovery; the 4800-baud figure in John's material is a narrowband-class benchmark (072f3954 doctrine: "the narrowband (4800-baud-class) figure is 44x").
    **Fix**: Delete the sentence, or: "Discovery needs nothing from the bearer but IP, so it runs over any carrier the node has."

31. **Where**: §13 "The proxy owns port 80", paragraph 1
    **Text**: "Every HTTP request crossing the node's interface — an application's, a browser's, and discovery's own echo and getHosts calls — arrives at the proxy first; nothing speaks HTTP on a FrogNet node without passing through it."
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: Only TCP/80 to 10.0.0.0/8 is redirected (OUTPUT, and PREROUTING on LAN_IFACE); SO_MARK=1 traffic RETURNs first (etc/setup_iptables:77-80). Discovery's echo and getHosts carry that mark (item 27).
    **Fix**: "HTTP to any 10/8 address — from an application, a browser, or a client on the lillypad — is redirected into the proxy. Discovery's own echo and getHosts are marked to go around it."

32. **Where**: §13 "The proxy owns port 80", paragraph 2
    **Text**: "A request addressed anywhere else — the public internet, a printer on the house LAN, anything outside 10/8 — is simply passed through, forwarded to the real upstream untouched. The proxy is in the path for everything"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: etc/setup_iptables:77-80 — non-10 HTTP is never redirected, so it never reaches the proxy.
    **Fix**: "HTTP to anywhere outside 10/8 is never intercepted; it leaves by the normal route. The proxy sees only FrogNet-bound HTTP."

33. **Where**: §13 "The proxy owns port 80", paragraph 3
    **Text**: "It reaches the daemon over a dedicated loopback socket."
    **Verdict**: WRONG; INCONSISTENT with the next paragraph ("a standing socket that the proxy opened")
    **Evidence**: The semantic path calls _get_worker(target_ip, 9009) (proxy/transport_semantic.py:2515-2516, 2830-2831). _get_worker refuses any host outside 10/8 (2181-2183), and _open_send_sock connects to that peer's daemon on :9009 and sends HELLO (1085-1105).
    **Fix**: "For a FrogNet-bound request, the proxy opens (and keeps) a socket to the destination node's daemon on :9009 and carries the exchange over it as FNWP frames."

34. **Where**: §14 "The Walk", paragraph after the worked example
    **Text**: "a light bidirectional liveness probe, not an application health check, and a candidate counts only if its daemon answers"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: A relayed candidate is proved by the reflect probe to its .2 on :18432, answered by the proxy's reflect handler (discovery/descend.py:196-223; proxy/proxy_main.py:770-810). The :9009 ping-pong to the daemon proves direct and tunnel paths (discovery/discovery.py:812-830; real_backends.py:332-391).
    **Fix**: "Each candidate is proved before it is trusted — a reflect probe for a path through a relay, a bidirectional :9009 ping-pong to the daemon for a direct or tunnel path — not an application health check."

35. **Where**: §14 ".2 proves, .1 carries"
    **Text**: "So discovery never probes on .1." / "and tears the .2 route down when the walk is done"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: The round-trip measure frognet_alive.bash tries the other address when the first fails: given a .2 it also tries the .1 (usr/local/bin/frognet_alive.bash:20-28, 56-60). The reflect probe's temporary /32 is deleted in a finally (descend.py:205-209), but promotion keeps a .2/32 alias route for every held or promoted winner at metric 5 (discovery.py:828-833; routes.py:50-53).
    **Fix**: "Discovery proves paths on .2. The temporary /32 for each probe is removed as soon as the probe answers; each destination that wins keeps a small .2/32 alias route beside its /24. (The round-trip helper, frognet_alive, still retries on .1 when .2 does not answer.)"
    Note for John: that .1 retry is a fallback in the code, against the no-fallback rule.

36. **Where**: §14 "Winning routes, ordered by RTT", paragraph 1
    **Text**: "As it proves each destination it measures the round-trip time to it"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
    **Evidence**: "rank by RTT to the NEXT HOP (fast), not X's far end" (discovery/descend.py:224-230).
    **Fix**: "As it proves each path it measures the round-trip time of that path's first hop"

37. **Where**: §14 "Winning routes", paragraph after "A faster path appears, and is ignored"
    **Text**: "An earlier version of the rule allowed a challenger to win if it was at least fifty per cent faster. It was replaced by the flat prohibition"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (NEW)
    **Evidence**: The hold ([ROUTE_INCUMBENCY_HOLD_V1]) runs first and leaves a healthy incumbent alone (discovery.py:797-838). The 50% bar is still in the code (WINNER_HYSTERESIS = 0.50, discovery.py:42-49) and still decides when the hold does not engage — the incumbent's health could not be checked, or it failed — and the incumbent is still among the measured candidates (discovery.py:843-860, PROMOTE_STICKY).
    **Fix**: "An earlier rule let a challenger win if it was at least fifty per cent faster. The flat prohibition now runs first; the fifty per cent bar remains only for a route whose health could not be confirmed."

38. **Where**: §14 "Routes that come back", counter table
    **Text**: "c >  32 (default hop cap)         -> LOOP among others, dropped"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (off by one)
    **Evidence**: `if c >= self._max_hops`, default 32 (proxy/proxy_main.py:747, 810).
    **Fix**: "c >= 32 (default hop cap)         -> LOOP among others, dropped"

39. **Where**: §14 "FOR THE GUILD"
    **Text**: "A box behind a box behind a box is not reached, and that is a constant in one file rather than anything the design requires"
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: One pass descends two levels (MAX_DEPTH = 2, discovery.py:58), but [DESCEND_FRONTIER_FROM_KNOWN_V1] seeds each merge from the routes already installed, so "successive runAgain-driven merges walk the chain to its end; a single pass is still bounded (2 levels)" (discovery/descend.py:479-511). In region mode there is no crawl (item 29).
    **Fix**: "Each walk descends two levels, children and grandchildren. The next merge starts from the routes already installed, so a deeper chain is walked to its end over a few passes." Drop the guild ask or point it at something open.

40. **Where**: §15 "Selecting the Service Hosts", paragraph 1
    **Text**: "A node advertises what it can do — compute, memory, storage throughput, codec support"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (minor, NEW)
    **Evidence**: The database score uses disk_class, "a property of the media. NOT measured throughput or fsync latency" ([DBHOST_STATIC_RANK_V1], core/database_handler.py:67-70, 111-135).
    **Fix**: "A node advertises what it can do — cores and clock, installed memory, disk class, codec support"

41. **Where**: §15, paragraph 2
    **Text**: "it is memory-bound, so the most RAM wins, with the highest address only as a tiebreak"
    **Verdict**: WRONG (oversimplified)
    **Evidence**: The score is installed RAM plus innodb pool, plus disk class, plus cores × MHz, gated on mysql_running; highest IP breaks ties (core/database_handler.py:59-201).
    **Fix**: "…it scores static capability — installed RAM first, then disk class and CPU — among boxes actually running MySQL, with the highest address only as a tiebreak (Part VI)."

42. **Where**: §15, paragraph 2
    **Text**: "A board-game host selects by whatever a game needs."
    **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
    **Evidence**: ROLE_HANDLERS holds only mediahost and databasehost (core/role_registry.py:30-33); no game role handler exists in core/ or discovery/.
    **Fix**: Delete, or "A future service — a game host, say — would choose by whatever it needs."

43. **Where**: §15, paragraph 2
    **Text**: "Adding a new kind of service host is implementing one virtual function, not editing a central election."
    **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
    **Evidence**: A role handler sets ROLE_NAME and implements score() and evaluate() (core/unrest_handler.py:95, 127-131), and is added to ROLE_HANDLERS (core/role_registry.py:30-33).
    **Fix**: "…is writing one handler — its score and evaluate callbacks — and adding it to the role registry, not editing a central election."

44. **Where**: §15, paragraph 3
    **Text**: "A small stability margin keeps a marginal challenger from constantly unseating a healthy incumbent."
    **Verdict**: WRONG
    **Evidence**: No margin exists. The generic election commits each role's pick every merge — "A winner that moves, moves" (core/frognet_service_hosts.py:395-404) — and commit_service_lines replaces the standing <role>.frognet line with it (440-446). The databasehost floor's incumbency hold (discovery/hosts.py:492-506) is overwritten by that line whenever the handler names a winner. Stability comes from scoring static capability only ([DBHOST_STATIC_RANK_V1], [MEDIAHOST_STATIC_RANK_V1], core/sotf_handler.py:356-364).
    **Fix**: "Stability comes from what is scored: only static capability, so a box's score does not move with load, and every node computes the same winner merge after merge."

Checked: 121 claims; 44 problems.

---

# Report C: Part VI (DatabaseHost) and Part VII (Connecting Across the Internet)

Conversation tools were available and used (newest first: 5bf4812e, a3c5615d, 304f955c, b8399587, 1f49d502, 38223476).

## A. Part VI: 09_Part_VI_The_DatabaseHost.md

### 1. FOR THE GUILD (opening): what makes a role live
- **Where**: Part VI lead, FOR THE GUILD
- **Text**: "a role is live when it has both a candidate tuple in the transient and a registered handler"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: FS/core/frognet_service_hosts.py:73-91 `_live_roles`. Every registered handler's role is appended to `live`, and the `present` tuple check is computed and never used ("even with no specialist candidate, the .1 hosts are implicit candidates"). :94-99 `[EVERY_FROGNETHOST_IS_A_CANDIDATE_V1]`.
- **Fix**: "a role is live when a handler for it is registered; every live .1 FrogNetHost is a candidate for it, and the handler decides what “best” means."

### 2. §16: the score is a weighted sum, not RAM then disk
- **Where**: §16 One Name, A Moving Target, para 1
- **Text**: "past it the box with the most RAM, then the faster disk, takes the role; the highest address only breaks a tie"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: FS/core/database_handler.py:59-130 `score()` adds installed RAM GB, 0.5 × the innodb pool, a disk-class weight and a CPU term (cores × MHz). It is a sum, not an order. Free RAM and load are not scored (`[DBHOST_STATIC_RANK_V1]`, `[NO_MEM_AVAILABLE_V1]`).
- **Fix**: "past it, a weighted sum of installed RAM (the dominant term), disk class and CPU decides; the highest address only breaks a tie."

### 3. §16 "Names from other ponds": resolv.conf does not list tunnel peers
- **Where**: §16, Names from other ponds, paras 2-3
- **Text**: "So each node lists, as a nameserver, the machine at the far end of every tunnel it holds."
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**: FS/discovery/resolv.py:20-21,34-53 `[RESOLV_LOCAL_UPSTREAM_GOOGLE_V1]`: "127.0.0.1, the upstream (if any), 8.8.8.8 -- nothing else ... no tunnel peers". FS/discovery/live.py:1020-1060 `_write_forwarders` writes `server=/<dom>/<ip>` and `server=/.<dom>/<ip>` per FrogNetHost into /etc/dnsmasq.d/frognet_forwarders_auto.conf. John's ruling of 2026-09-26 (e86ac371): "only use 127.0.0.1, the upstream (if any), and 8.8.8.8".
- **Fix**: Replace paras 2-3: "resolv.conf names only 127.0.0.1, the upstream (if any) and 8.8.8.8. A name from another pond reaches the right primary through dnsmasq. Each merge writes a `server=/<domain>/<its .1>` line for every FrogNetHost in the converged hosts block into /etc/dnsmasq.d/frognet_forwarders_auto.conf. It is written from the hosts block, the one place that names which .1 serves which domain." Keep para 4 (the kernel cannot answer it) as the reason the routing table cannot supply that join.

### 4. §16: how --local-service is lifted
- **Where**: §16, Names from other ponds, last two paras
- **Text**: "So a FrogNet node disables it, by configuration rather than by editing the distro’s own service file."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: No file in TREE/etc or TREE/usr/local/bin sets DNSMASQ_OPTS or bind-dynamic (grep). FS/discovery/live.py:1025,1059 writes `listen-address=127.0.0.1,<self .1>` and `bind-interfaces`. TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet_dhcp_scope.sh renders the `interface=` lines. dnsmasq drops --local-service whenever interface= or listen-address= is set.
- **Fix**: "dnsmasq drops --local-service whenever interface= or listen-address= is configured, and a FrogNet node has both: interface= lines rendered from /etc/dnsmasq_conf_template, and listen-address=127.0.0.1,<self .1> plus bind-interfaces, which the merge writes. No distro file is edited." Delete the next paragraph ("It is not replaced with a list of interfaces ... open resolver ..."), because the node does list its interfaces.

### 5. §16: an opt-in History entity exists
- **Where**: §16, One value, and how old it is, para 1
- **Text**: "There is no history, no log, no revision."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (minor; it is right about the tuple)
- **Evidence**: TREE/var/www/html/api.php:941-958 `entity=history&action=append` (`[HISTORY_APPEND_V1]`, append-only rows) and :960-1000 `write_all`.
- **Fix**: "The tuple keeps no history, no log, no revision. An application that wants a time series appends to the separate History entity (`entity=history&action=append`); that is its choice, not the Region's default."

### 6. §17 intro: what the control plane holds
- **Where**: §17 Two Names, Two Planes, para 2
- **Text**: "It holds the capability records the election reads, and nothing else. No application ever addresses it, nothing you build should touch it"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: FS/core/frognet_tuples.py:336-339 `DEFAULT_DBHOST = "databasehost_control.frognet"`: "capability, presence, beacons and the Services table resolve here". FS/discovery/live.py:777-787 writes `discovery/reach_plane` there (not in region mode).
- **Fix**: "It holds the coordination plane: the SD: tuples, meaning capability records, reach_plane, presence, beacons and the Services table. It holds no application data of yours."

### 7. §17 worked example: order of operations
- **Where**: §17, WORKED EXAMPLE The order of operations in a single merge
- **Text**: "2  FLOOR      databasehost.frognet = highest .1"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: FS/discovery/orchestrate.py:178-185. Control is computed first (`control_host_ip`). Then `select_database_host` (FS/discovery/hosts.py:404-551) picks the best mysql-running .1 from the control's capability rows and holds a healthy incumbent (`[DBHOST_INCUMBENCY_HOLD_V1]`). It uses the highest .1 only when no capability data exists. `set_control_host` then writes the control line. "written FIRST" (step 3) is therefore wrong as well.
- **Fix**: "1 build the host block · 2 CONTROL: compute the highest .1, with no election · 3 FLOOR: databasehost.frognet = the best mysql-running .1 per control's capability rows, holding a healthy incumbent; the highest .1 if there is no capability data · 4 write the control line · 5 publish (broker mode only) · 6 ELECT: every role's handler over control's rows; if no databasehost is named, it pins to the control (FS/core/frognet_service_hosts.py:445-450)."

### 8. §17 "Waiting for everyone": the barrier is gone
- **Where**: §17, Waiting for everyone (both paras)
- **Text**: "It waits until control holds a fresh record for every live machine it can see."
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**: FS/discovery/live.py:888-897 `[ABSENT_IS_A_DECIDABLE_STATE_V1] role_barrier_ready is no longer called`, `_ready_roles = set(_roles)`. FS/core/frognet_service_hosts.py:379-411 `[ABSENT_IS_A_DECIDABLE_STATE_V1 - John 2026-09-12] The barrier is gone`, with `ready_roles` "accepted and IGNORED".
- **Fix**: Retitle "Absent is an answer" and replace both paras: "The election does not wait. It runs on every converged merge. A live .1 that has not published is still a candidate; its capability is unknown, so it ranks at the floor, and the highest .1 breaks the tie. Every node reads the same store and the same converged hosts block, so every node reaches the same answer from the same absence. If the election names no database host, the name pins to the control host. A lone box elects itself for every role."

### 9. §18: the write-then-read detector is not wired up
- **Where**: §18 Split, Merge, and Why It Is Safe, paras 3-6
- **Text**: "And detection is where FrogNet’s involvement ends. It fires the callbacks and stops."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (as shipped)
- **Evidence**: FS/proxy/proxy_metrics.py:1518,1530 and FS/daemon/daemon_metrics.py:768,779 call `_T.on_database_change()` and `_T.heartbeat()`. Neither is defined in FS/core/frognet_tuples.py. The only definitions are under ribbit/cpp/tests/reference/, so registration fails and is logged. The trigger that runs is `HostResetWatcher` (FS/core/frognet_host_reset.py:88-), which fires on a change in the resolved databasehost(_control) IP; FS/core/role_publish.py:123-129 uses it. In region mode the daemon also writes /var/lib/frognet/view.json (`dbhost`, `gen`, `phase`; FS/internet_tunnels_v3/poll.py:2283-2326).
- **Fix**: Keep write-then-read as the recommended application pattern. Replace "It fires the callbacks" with: "What FrogNet runs is a per-process watcher on the name: when databasehost.frognet resolves somewhere new, it calls each module's hostReset() (core/frognet_host_reset.py). On a region the daemon also writes the new databasehost into /var/lib/frognet/view.json within a second of the change. The node-heartbeat hooks the proxy and daemon reference (NODE_HEARTBEAT_TS_V1) are not in this tree."

## B. Part VII: 10_Part_VII_Connecting_Across_the_Internet.md

### 10. §19 lead: the broker decrypts
- **Where**: §19 The Broker, para 2
- **Text**: "which relays the encrypted bytes without holding or reading anything"
- **Verdict**: WRONG / INCONSISTENT (with the Part VII lead and §21 para 2)
- **Evidence**: FS/internet_tunnels_v3/peer.py:807-809: every peer block is `droplet_pubkey`/`droplet_endpoint`, so each tunnel terminates at the broker. Chat 1f49d502: the broker is the inter-site data path.
- **Fix**: "the tunnels it hands out terminate at the broker, which decrypts each one and forwards between them in the kernel, so inter-site traffic is in the clear inside the broker."

### 11. §19 and §20: a wiped broker does not rebuild ponds
- **Where**: §19 para 2; §19 Where the broker can live, para 3; §20 Clearing and resetting the database, last para
- **Text**: "Wipe the broker clean and the next round of polls rebuilds its whole picture." / "nothing in it the network cannot rebuild by checking in again" / "That is also why db reset is a recoverable operation rather than a catastrophe"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: bundle frognet_broker_v4.py:688-695 (`_get_pond` 404 "Pond not found") and :1788 `/register`. Ponds come only from the admin `POST /api/v4/ponds` (:1683). `db_reset` deletes every table in `_ALL_TABLES`, ponds included (:3336-3360, :3702).
- **Fix**: "Nodes, tunnels and choruses are rebuilt by nodes checking in. Ponds are not: after a wipe or `db reset` the operator recreates each pond with the same base, step and password, and the nodes re-register on their next poll."

### 12. §19 "One egress per node": a LAN-only node keeps its broker config
- **Where**: §19, One egress per node
- **Text**: "it is LAN-only: it carries no broker configuration at all"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:64-104: "We do NOT remove broker config"; "Not registering. Broker config left intact".
- **Fix**: "it is LAN-only: it keeps its broker configuration but does not register, and it reaches the wider world through that one peer."

### 13. §19 worked example: the backup line
- **Where**: §19, WORKED EXAMPLE hometown's broker, on a droplet
- **Text**: "backup   copy that one file. that's the whole pond's membership."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with §20) / WRONG
- **Evidence**: bundle frognet_broker_v4.py:84,91 `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL`. §20 itself says a copy of the main file is not a backup.
- **Fix**: "backup   frognet-pond db backup  (SQLite's backup API; a bare copy misses the WAL)"

### 14. §19 tunnel.conf examples: CHORUSES format and role
- **Where**: §19 Pointing a node at a broker; §19 local broker example; §20 Ten hops become two
- **Text**: "CHORUSES=family,workshop"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
- **Evidence**: TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet-make-gateway.sh:138 and TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet_setup_v4_helper.bash:140 store CHORUSES space-separated. Registration does not send it: frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:201 posts to /api/v4/register, which auto-joins entire_pond, and :222 reads the broker's chorus list back. Joins go through `/api/v4/choruses/join` (bundle :2368).
- **Fix**: `CHORUSES="family workshop"`, and in the prose: "the choruses it means to join (recorded here; the join itself is a call to the broker's chorus API)".

### 15. §19: pond-bootstrap is no longer a timer
- **Where**: §19, after HomeBase's tunnel.conf
- **Text**: "A timer, frognet-pond-bootstrap, reads it and tries the broker every couple of minutes until it answers a health check"
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**: TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet-pond-bootstrap.sh:21-28 `[DEFERRED_BROKER_SETUP_V1] Event-driven, not polled`. TREE/usr/local/bin/runMerge.bash:387-396 runs it at the tail of every merge until /etc/frognet/pond_bootstrap_done exists. TREE/usr/local/bin/installer/frognet_install.sh:1804-1811: "No timer ... The timer unit is left disabled." The check is `GET /api/v4/ponds` → 200 (bootstrap.sh:67-81). In region mode it is not run (runMerge.bash:391 `[NO_BROKER_ENROLMENT_IN_REGION_V1]`).
- **Fix**: "Each merge runs frognet-pond-bootstrap until enrolment is done. A merge is what a network change already produces (a DHCP lease, an interface up or down, a connectivity change), so the broker is tried at the moment it might have become reachable. When GET /api/v4/ponds answers 200, the node registers, starts the tunnel daemon and writes /etc/frognet/pond_bootstrap_done, and the merge stops calling it. The node is a complete network the whole time."

### 16. §19 "Bootstrapping and handing out keys": commands, passcodes and GROUP_TOKEN are wrong
- **Where**: §19, Bootstrapping and handing out keys, its worked example and the paragraph after it
- **Text**: "frognet-broker-admin.sh passcode new --group hometown   ->  ribbit-9F3K" / "What lands on MomBox is a GROUP_TOKEN"
- **Verdict**: REWRITE
- **Evidence**: frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:41-48 accepts only `--pond-password` and reads BROKER_URL/POND_NAME from tunnel.conf. It writes `GROUP_TOKEN=` empty (:268). FS/internet_tunnels_v3/config.py:559 `[NO_GROUP_TOKEN_CRITICAL_V1 - John 2026-09-12]`. frognet-broker-admin.sh has no `passcode new`; its `create-passcode` POSTs /api/v1/passcodes (:132), which v4 does not serve (bundle route list). The admin token is minted by the broker's installer through `POST /api/v4/bootstrap` (bundle :1645). The broker checks `pond_password` at register (bundle :1812). Chat 38223476 already flagged this CLI.
- **Fix** (replacement section):
  > #### Joining a pond
  > The broker's installer bootstraps it once and keeps the admin token on the broker machine. The operator creates a pond: `frognet-pond create hometown --node-base 10.101.100.1 --increment 10 --max-users 50 [--password PW]`. A node joins with three lines in /etc/frognet/tunnel.conf (BROKER_URL, POND_NAME and, if the pond has one, POND_PASSWORD) and `frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh [--pond-password PW]`. That script generates the node's WireGuard keypair and posts its GUID (/etc/fnid), public key and /24 to /api/v4/register. The broker identifies the node by its GUID. There is no passcode and no group token. “Distributing broker knowledge” is that one file.
  >
  > WORKED EXAMPLE — Standing up hometown, then adding Mom
  > ```
  > # on the broker
  > frognet-pond create hometown --node-base 10.101.100.1 --increment 10 --password hop-2026
  > # on MomBox: /etc/frognet/tunnel.conf
  > BROKER_URL=http://streamingfrog.com:18257
  > POND_NAME=hometown
  > POND_PASSWORD=hop-2026
  > # then
  > frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh --pond-password hop-2026
  > ```

### 17. §19 and §20 local broker: only nodes with their own uplink register
- **Where**: §19 The local broker; §20 The intranet case, para 2
- **Text**: "Point tunnel.conf at one running on the LAN"
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED (a caveat is missing)
- **Evidence**: frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:82-104 `has_direct_upstream`: registration requires a default route with a non-10.x next hop, whatever the broker address. No chat lifts this for a LAN broker (searched "local broker").
- **Fix**: Add: "Only a node with its own non-FrogNet default route registers (frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh), so both ends of a shortcut must be such nodes."

### 18. §20 installer, console, gate and day-to-day commands describe software that is not shipped
- **Where**: §20, What you will be asked; Running the installer; What the installer builds — and the wall in the middle of it; Running it day to day
- **Text**: "BROKER GATE: PASS 10/10" (and the question list, `--mode upgrade-keep`, `--verify-only`, the spool/admin wall, `journalctl -u frognet-gatekeeper-broker -f`, `frognet-pond ping`, `members`, `result`)
- **Verdict**: REWRITE
- **Evidence**: bundle install_frognet_broker.sh:8,21 ("It does not interrogate you"), flags `--port --webroot --no-console --no-packages --dry-run` (:59-75), port defaults to 18257 (:237-246), and :462 `uvicorn frognet_broker_v4:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port $PORT` with no TLS. It has no modes, no --verify-only, no pond creation and no BROKER GATE line. There is no gatekeeper in TREE. The bundle's frognet-pond calls `/api/v4/gate/ping` and `?gate_result=` (:123,:219), which the broker does not serve (route list :1645-3702). Its subcommand is `nodes`, not `members` (:156). Chats: b8399587 (07-27, John: "Pull that bullshit out"); 304f955c (08-29, John: "THERE IS NO WEBSITE ASSOCIATED WITH 18428 OR 18257 ... The broker is uvicorn on 18257").
- **Fix** (replacement for those four subsections):
  > #### Running the installer
  > `sudo ./install_frognet_broker.sh [--port 18257] [--webroot DIR] [--no-console] [--no-packages] [--dry-run]`. It asks no questions. It installs the Python environment, writes frognet-broker-v4.service (uvicorn on 0.0.0.0:<port>, plain HTTP), backs up an existing database with SQLite's backup API, mints the admin token through POST /api/v4/bootstrap and keeps it in /etc/frognet/admin_token_v4.conf. It finishes with a postflight: the unit is active and GET /api/v4/ponds answers 200. It is idempotent, so running it again on an existing broker is the upgrade.
  >
  > #### Ponds
  > Create ponds afterwards: `frognet-pond create <name> --node-base <10.x.y.1> --increment <n> --max-users <n> [--password PW]`. The broker refuses a node base that does not end in .1.
  >
  > #### Running it day to day
  > `frognet-pond` talks to the broker's port with the bearer token: `list`, `status <pond>`, `nodes [pond]`, `forget <pubkey>`, `block <pubkey>`, `unblock <pubkey>`, `blocklist`, `remove`, and the `db` commands below. Logs: `journalctl -u frognet-broker-v4 -f`.
  >
  > Drop the console/air-gap/gatekeeper material, `ping` and `result`, and the "one request at a time" paragraph.

### 19. §20: the broker service does not terminate TLS
- **Where**: §20, What the installer builds, para 2
- **Text**: "terminates its own encryption"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (and INCONSISTENT with "The broker service speaks HTTP")
- **Evidence**: bundle install_frognet_broker.sh:462 has no TLS options.
- **Fix**: "It opens and manages its own port, speaks plain HTTP, allocates the WireGuard ports, and programs the namespaces."

### 20. §20: "holds no credential", "cannot route to the 10/8 plane", "attack surface remains zero"
- **Where**: §20, the last two paras before "None of this touches your nodes"
- **Text**: "What answers holds no credential. It cannot route to the 10/8 plane." / "The attack surface remains zero."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: bundle frognet_broker_v4.py:1645-1670: the admin token is held in `admin_config`. The pond namespaces terminate every tunnel and route 10.0.0.0/8 between them (:3303 `add_wg_peer(... ["10.0.0.0/8"])`). The security stance ruling: "The tunnel is wg, which is basic."
- **Fix**: "There is one port on the internet, and it answers plain HTTP. Admin routes require the bearer token. The same machine terminates the pond's tunnels, so whoever owns it owns the inter-site seam (Part XII). FrogNet does not force more on it and does not prevent it."

### 21. §20: one namespace per pond, not per tunnel
- **Where**: §20, after the three installer modes
- **Text**: "behind every tunnel record are two WireGuard interfaces, two port forwards, and a network namespace"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: bundle frognet_broker_v4.py:1705 `ns_name = f"pondv4_{name}"`; `_destroy_tunnel` at :1187 removes two forwards and two wg interfaces inside it.
- **Fix**: "behind every tunnel record are two WireGuard interfaces and two port forwards inside the pond's network namespace"

### 22. §20: the WireGuard port range
- **Where**: §20, What you will be asked
- **Text**: "the WireGuard port range        (default 51901-51999)"
- **Verdict**: WRONG / INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**: bundle frognet_broker_v4.py:53-54 sets the code default to 51901-51999. FS/broker/etc/systemd/system/frognet-broker-v4.service.d/wgports.conf sets 51000-59999. The installer asks nothing. bundle broker_namespace_v4.py:128-131 hard-codes FORWARD `--dport 51820:51900`, which covers neither range. This is a code issue for John and is left out of the book.
- **Fix**: "WireGuard ports come from FROGNET_WG_PORT_BASE/MAX (code default 51901-51999; the shipped drop-in sets 51000-59999)."

### 23. §20: what the broker checks on a node base
- **Where**: §20, paragraph after the question list
- **Text**: "the installer will refuse you if you fight them"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
- **Evidence**: The installer takes no node base. bundle `POST /api/v4/ponds` (:1683-1715) checks only that the base is an IP ending in .1. The 10.253/10.254 refusal applies to the node subnet at /register (:1800).
- **Fix**: "The broker refuses a node base that does not end in .1, and refuses to register a node subnet inside 10.253 or 10.254."

### 24. §20: ~40 ms and ~3 ms
- **Where**: §20, WORKED EXAMPLE Ten hops become two
- **Text**: "~40 ms, 8 ways to fail." / "~3 ms, and the eight relays keep their own traffic."
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
- **Evidence**: No measurement in FS/simulation baselines, FS/tests or the chats (searched "local broker", "ten hops").
- **Fix**: "every packet crosses eight machines, and each is one more way to fail." / "one hop, and the eight relays keep their own traffic." (Or mark the numbers illustrative.)

### 25. §20 intranet case: "drops to a fallback"
- **Where**: §20, after Ten hops become two
- **Text**: "The chain does not disappear; it drops to a fallback and takes over unchanged the moment the tunnel stops answering."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (wording against the no-fallbacks ruling)
- **Evidence**: Settled ruling: no fallbacks. The mechanism is that the next merge re-ranks by measured RTT (FS/discovery/descend.py rank/commit), not a standby path held in reserve.
- **Fix**: "The chain does not disappear. It is still in the walk, and the next merge, when the tunnel stops answering, ranks it best again."

### 26. §21 "What keeps it private": hop-by-hop, no passcode, no group token
- **Where**: §21, What keeps it private
- **Text**: "a node redeems a passcode with the broker for a group token it then carries as its credential" / "so traffic between sites is encrypted end to end"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: As #10 and #16; config.py:559; bundle :1812 pond_password.
- **Fix**: "Joining a pond is registration: the node presents its GUID, its WireGuard public key and the pond password, if the pond has one. ..." / "every cross-site tunnel is WireGuard, so traffic is encrypted on each leg between a node and the broker, and is in the clear inside the broker."

### 27. §21: the tunnel daemon polls; it does not react to broker events
- **Where**: §21, The tunnel daemon owns the lifecycle
- **Text**: "on a broker event it brings the interfaces up and installs their routes; a periodic reconciliation rebuilds any interface that has vanished, within tens of seconds"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: FS/internet_tunnels_v3/config.py:78 `POLL_INTERVAL_SEC = 300`; poll.py:2672-2714 `run_poll_loop` (poll_once, then a runMerge fork if anything changed). FS/discovery/live.py:565-570: bring-up runs at the start of a merge (not in region mode). In region mode the daemon acts on region events instead (poll.py:2341-, "never a timer"; see #33).
- **Fix**: "With a broker, the daemon reconciles kernel wg interfaces to the broker's channel list on each poll (every 5 minutes) and at the start of every merge, bringing up what is missing and tearing down what the broker dropped. On an FD&F region it does the same on each region event."

### 28. §21 worked example: endpoint port
- **Where**: §21, WORKED EXAMPLE A channel becomes an interface and a route
- **Text**: "endpoint    203.0.113.7:51820   (the broker, reachable)"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
- **Evidence**: Ports are allocated from WG_PORT_BASE (bundle :53, :772): 51901 by default, 51000 with the drop-in.
- **Fix**: "endpoint    203.0.113.7:51907   (a per-tunnel port the broker allocated)"

### 29. §19 lead (minor): "stateless"
- **Where**: §19 The Broker, para 2
- **Text**: "The broker is stateless — a rendezvous, not a record."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (follows from #11)
- **Evidence**: The pond rows, the node IP allocation (`node_ip_alloc`) and the admin token live only in broker.db (bundle `_ALL_TABLES` :3340-3360).
- **Fix**: "The broker holds little: the ponds and the addresses it handed out are its own records, and everything else is re-derived from nodes registering and polling."
