## C. Region mode (FD&F): where the book needs to know it

A FrogNet node can now run in region mode. `fdf-region-join SITE_KEY [RAM] [AREA]` (FDF/fdf/bin/fdf-region-join;
defaults 165.232.153.144:51888, area frognet) writes /opt/fdf/var/site.env and join.env and starts fdf-client. It
disables frognet-dbhost-advertise.timer, frognet-mediahost-advertise.timer, frognet-dbhost-elect.timer,
frognet-merge-watcher.service and frognet-pond-bootstrap.timer, and restarts frognet-tunnel-daemon-v3. `RINA_RAM=` in
join.env or tunnel.conf is the one switch (FS/internet_tunnels_v3/config.py:520-530 `[REGION_SWITCH_V2]`;
TREE/usr/local/bin/runMerge.bash:117). The FrogNet region patch (FDF/frognet/..., the same files as
FS/internet_tunnels_v3/region.py, discovery/elect.py, core/region_capability.py) changes Parts VI and VII as follows.
The book describes only broker mode.

### 30. Part VI lead / §16: name the two modes
- **Where**: Part VI lead para (after "...Ribbit itself invents none of those semantics.")
- **Text**: "FrogNet elected one reachable database host and resolved it through one stable name, databasehost.frognet."
- **Verdict**: STALE (incomplete)
- **Evidence**: FS/discovery/live.py:499-519 (`FROGNET_DISCOVERY_SOURCE=region`, `no_crawl`); :661-685 (peers from /var/lib/frognet/region_hosts, "The walk does not run"; candidates from the region's facts); :772-819 (in region mode reach_plane and capability tuples are not written).
- **Fix**: Add after the lead: "A FrogNet node runs in one of two modes. With a broker, which is what this Part describes, the walk finds the members, and capability is published to the control plane. On an FD&F region (fdf-region-join), FD&F owns membership: the members come from the region, each member's capability is a fact the tunnel daemon probes and announces (at start and every 600 s), and the elections read those facts, not the store. databasehost.frognet is the same name in both modes, chosen by the same scoring code."

### 31. §16 "recalculates ... on every pass" and §17 "Publishing to both" in region mode
- **Where**: §16 para after the first worked example; §17 Publishing to both
- **Text**: "The merge — the same machinery that runs the walk — recalculates the database host and rewrites it into every node's hosts file on every pass." / "A node writes its capability to both planes, every time."
- **Verdict**: STALE (broker mode only)
- **Evidence**: FS/internet_tunnels_v3/poll.py:2372-2400 `[CAPABILITY_IS_A_FACT_V1]` (facts.capability, FROGNET_CAP_REFRESH_SEC 600). :2462-2470 and :2544-2545 `[ELECT_IN_PROCESS_V1]`: the daemon runs `discovery.elect.elect_now` on a member loss or return or a capability change, with "no runMerge for a region event". FS/core/role_publish.py:59-66 `[CAPABILITY_TUPLES_OFF_IN_REGION_V1]`. FS/core/region_capability.py:1-14 states why: the store lived on the host that died (Seattle5, 2026-10-09).
- **Fix**: Add to §16: "On a region the tunnel daemon runs the same election in-process on the region event that changed its input: a member leaving or returning, or a capability fact changing. A dead database host is elected around within a second, and the new answer is in /var/lib/frognet/view.json." Add to §17 Publishing to both: "This is broker mode. On a region nothing is published to either plane for the election; capability is a fact in the region, and the election reads the members' facts, so it never depends on a store on the host that just left."

### 32. §17 control host on a region
- **Where**: §17 Why one is elected and the other is not, para 1
- **Text**: "it is simply the highest gateway address in the network — the highest address ending in .1, excluding the reserved 10.253 transit and 10.254 chorus ranges."
- **Verdict**: STALE (one more exclusion on a region)
- **Evidence**: FS/discovery/elect.py:100-110 `[ELECT_NODES_ONLY_V1]`: on a region, leaves (phones, PCs) get /32s in 10.252/16, and the first is 10.252.0.1, which ends in .1. It was elected control on 2026-10-11, and members with role leaf/participant are now excluded from the pool. They are named in /etc/hosts and never chosen (:55-59).
- **Fix**: Append: "On a region the pool is the region's nodes only. A leaf's address (10.252.x, the first of which ends in .1) is named in /etc/hosts but is never a candidate."

### 33. Part VII lead / §19: the region replaces the broker for its members
- **Where**: Part VII lead para; §19 para 1
- **Text**: "Two pieces do it — a broker that introduces frogs that cannot find each other, and the encrypted tunnels it hands out to carry the 10-net between them."
- **Verdict**: STALE (incomplete)
- **Evidence**: FS/internet_tunnels_v3/region.py:5-27: in region mode the broker's four calls (register, my-channels, update-subnets, transit-map) are answered from the region. my-channels returns one channel per live hub (edge 10.253.A.B/16 for 10.A.B.0/24, AllowedIPs 10.0.0.0/8; :206, :280-281). poll.py:2341-2356: run_region_loop is driven by fdf-events, "never a timer"; when the region is gone, routes and names are withdrawn and the tunnels torn down (`[GONE_IS_GONE_V1]`, :2652). FDF/docs/DEPLOY-REGION-2026-10-09.md:44-66: the droplet StreamingFrog runs the RAM manager (rina-host, TCP 51888) and fdf-hub (UDP 50889, wg-frognet, 10.253.253.254/16) next to broker v4, which keeps running for broker-mode nodes.
- **Fix**: Add to the lead: "A node can instead be a member of an FD&F region (Specification C). There the region replaces the broker: membership is the node's session with the RAM manager, and its one tunnel goes to the region's hub (fdf-hub, beside the RAM manager on StreamingFrog), which forwards between members. AllowedIPs is still 10.0.0.0/8. Nothing polls; the daemon acts on region events, and when the region is gone its routes, names and tunnel go with it. The broker keeps serving nodes that are not on a region." Add a one-line pointer in §21 and §22 that the walk-ranking text is broker mode: on a region the routes are FD&F's plan (fdf_graph), applied by the daemon, which admits LAN candidates with one probe each and gates hub routes on the handshake (poll.py:2344-2348).

### 34. §22 "ranks a tunnelled path by its round-trip time" on a region
- **Where**: §22, para after Two sites, one overlay
- **Text**: "The discovery walk of Part V ranks a tunnelled path by its round-trip time exactly as it ranks a wired one"
- **Verdict**: STALE (broker mode only)
- **Evidence**: FS/discovery/live.py:519 `disc.no_crawl` and :661-675 (region: "The walk does not run").
- **Fix**: Append: "That is broker mode. On a region the walk does not run; the routes are the region's plan, as #33 describes."

### 35. §22 Choruses on a region
- **Where**: §22, Choruses — groups that span the pond
- **Text**: "the CHORUSES=family line from the broker file"
- **Verdict**: STALE (note only)
- **Evidence**: Chat 5bf4812e (10-09 plan, 0.4): "Chorus subnets (10.254) stay as they are for now." A pond on a region is an FD&F area with its own RAM manager and site key (FDF/docs/PLAN-PONDS.md §1-2), not a broker pond.
- **Fix**: Add: "On a region a pond is the region's area. Choruses and 10.254 are unchanged for now."

Checked: 131 claims; 35 problems.

# Report D — Part VII½ (11_), Part VIII (12_), Part IX (13_)

Conversation tools were available and used. Paths are relative to TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ unless stated otherwise.
None of the 17 problems found on 10-08 has been fixed in the book text, and the code has not moved to meet any of them. Each is re-checked below against the 10-11 code, and new problems are added.

---

## 11_Part_VII_Adopting_It_Where_a_Network_Already_Exist.md

**1. Where**: §22b, "Agentless is the whole difference"
- **Text**: "No per-host mesh can make that claim, and it is the claim worth leading with."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (external fact; carried over from 10-08)
- **Evidence**: Tailscale subnet routers, NetBird network routes and ZeroTier managed routes all reach unmodified hosts through one agent box.
- **Fix**: "Per-host meshes can reach a device that cannot take an agent through a subnet router, as an add-on. For FrogNet, insertion below egress is the default, not the exception, and that is the claim worth leading with."

**2. Where**: §22b, "But we already use 10-space"
- **Text**: "You also have to answer the phone when discovery tries to contact the proxy."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new)
- **Evidence**: The gate is the daemon's :9009 ping-pong, not the proxy. See discovery/discovery.py:400-410 `[PINGPONG_GATE_V1]` ("The daemon binds 0.0.0.0:9009") and `_alive_measure`.
- **Fix**: "You also have to answer the phone when discovery calls the daemon on :9009."

**3. Where**: §22b, worked example "Why only a refusal marks a host as not-FrogNet" (the title, the first paragraph and the last paragraph)
- **Text**: "a timeout must never mark a host as not-FrogNet. Only a definitive refusal does."
- **Verdict**: STALE / WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**: core/not_frognet.py:
  - Definitive errors are ECONNREFUSED, EHOSTUNREACH and ENETUNREACH (lines 106-110).
  - `STRIKES_TO_MARK`=3 `[THREE_STRIKES_V1]` (line 380).
  - `NF_STRIKE_WINDOW_S`=15 `[STRIKES_MUST_SPAN_TIME_V1]` (line 402).
  - `NF_RETRY_AFTER_S`=60 `[MARK_EXPIRES_ON_ITS_OWN_V1]` (line 262).
  - A timeout to a client-range address (.3-.254) does mark (`_is_client_range`, lines 131-145, and `mark_if_definitive`, line 443).
  - The Seattle3 incident was itself one refusal, so "a refusal is evidence" cannot be the rule that fixed it.
- **Fix**: Retitle the example "Why one failure never marks a host as not-FrogNet". Replace the first paragraph with: "The negative cache has a rule that looks over-careful until you know where it came from: one failure never marks a host. A timeout to a node address (.1 or .2) never marks it at all." Replace the last paragraph with: "One legitimate refusal, during a two-second restart, cost the network its memory. What followed: a mark now needs three consecutive definitive failures (refused, or no route) spread over at least 15 seconds, and every mark expires after 60 seconds so the host gets a fresh probe."

## 13_Part_IX_Ribbit_Wire_BLDC_1_and_FNWP.md

**7. Where**: §25 From Messages to Memory, paragraph 3
- **Text**: "Ribbit is the programming model and Region One boundary is worth drawing here"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (text corruption; carried over)
- **Evidence**: A separate paragraph has been spliced into the middle of the sentence "Ribbit is the programming model and Region contract; …".
- **Fix**: "…the ownership is precise: Ribbit is the programming model and Region contract; BLDC-1 is semantic representation; FNWP is the wire; FrogNet or FD&F supplies transport. Two participants that share state need not re-send what both already hold. The wire can carry the difference and nothing else." Then put "One boundary is worth drawing here: … Borrow the model, own the convention." as its own paragraph after it.

**8. Where**: §26, paragraph 1
- **Text**: "The first exchange of a structure goes whole — FULL — and both sides learn its template"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - The first exchange of an unknown shape goes as REQ_RAW / RESP_RAW: proxy/transport_semantic.py:2445-2471 `[BOOTSTRAP_ON_MISSING_TEMPLATE_V1]` → `_handle_raw_and_learn` (line 2811).
  - REQ_FULL is sent only when a template exists and there is no reference yet (lines 2574-2577).
  - C++ agrees: ribbit/cpp/include/fnwp_engine.hpp:25 "the server LEARNS from the RAW exchange".
  - Settled ruling: a primed dictionary does not make the first message compact.
- **Fix**: "The first exchange of a structure goes raw — the whole HTTP request and answer — and both sides learn its template from it. The next exchange of that shape is the first FULL: every value, with no reference yet to diff against. After that…"

**9. Where**: §26, paragraph 1
- **Text**: "and falls back to FULL when the structure no longer matches the retained representation."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new)
- **Evidence**:
  - The body's field set is part of the template key: proxy/templates.py:245-273 `_with_body_shape`, `[BODY_FIELD_SET_IS_PART_OF_THE_IDENTITY_V1]`.
  - So a changed structure finds no template and goes RAW again (transport_semantic.py:2464).
  - A template miss at the daemon re-bootstraps RAW (daemon/engine/session.py:1120-1126).
  - In C++, a changed answer shape goes RESP_RAW (fnwp_engine.hpp:27-28).
- **Fix**: "…and when the structure changes, the exchange goes raw again and a new template is learned from it."

**10. Where**: §26, worked example "A sensor reading on the wire"
- **Text**: "exchange 1   FULL" … "exchange 2   DIFF   value->54.6  ts->...                    ~12  B"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over, and extended)
- **Evidence**:
  - The first exchange is RAW, not FULL (item 8).
  - A REQ_DIFF frame is 4 magic + 1 op + 16 hash + 4 len = 25 B (core/semcache_wire.py:140-144).
  - The codec header is 8 B (`<BBIH`, core/codec.py:197).
  - Each changed number costs 3 B index/type + 8 B value (codec.py:185-187, 438-447).
  - So two changed numbers cost at least 25+8+22 = 55 B, before the location fields and the 4-byte length prefix. A DIFF can never be smaller than the 21 B SAME.
- **Fix**: "exchange 1   RAW    the whole request and answer   both sides learn the template / exchange 2   FULL   the values, no reference yet / exchange 3   DIFF   value->54.6 ts->...   ~55 B / exchange 4   SAME   (nothing changed)   21 B"

**11. Where**: §26 "How a template is learned", paragraph 1
- **Text**: "That template is the FULL from a moment ago; learning it and sending it are the same act."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**: Same as item 8. The template is learned from the RAW exchange (proxy/templates.py:251-252 "both sides learn from RAW exchanges").
- **Fix**: "That template is learned from the raw exchange a moment ago; nobody sends a template."

**12. Where**: §26 "How a template is learned", paragraph 2
- **Text**: "A temperature reading and a humidity reading from the same kind of sensor share one template"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - proxy/templates.py:276-305 `canonical_semantic_key`: a sensor upsert is keyed `…&SensorType=<t>&MetricName=<last segment of SensorName>`, so temperature and humidity get two templates.
  - Every other call also folds in the set of body field names (`_with_body_shape`).
- **Fix**: "It is keyed by the stable parts of the exchange — the operation, the fixed query keys and the set of body field names; a sensor write is keyed by its sensor type and metric — never by the values. Two barns' temperature readings share one template; a humidity reading has its own. A brand-new temperature sensor rides the existing template the instant it reports."

**13. Where**: §26 "The cache — what each side remembers", paragraph 1
- **Text**: "for each peer and each kind of exchange, the proxy keeps the last canonical values it sent or received."
- **Verdict**: STALE (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - References are keyed (target_ip, opcode, instance): proxy/transport_semantic.py:314-319 `[INSTANCE_REFERENCES_V1]`.
  - The instance is the location in the message (core/tuple_key.py; transport_semantic.py:2525-2528).
- **Fix**: "for each peer, each kind of exchange and each instance of it — the location the message names — the proxy keeps the last canonical values it sent or received."

**14. Where**: §26 "The cache", paragraph 1
- **Text**: "so a SAME never touches the disk on either side."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - A REQ_REPEAT is answered by re-executing the request against the origin (Apache → MySQL) and comparing hashes: daemon/engine/session.py:946-1007 and `_reexecute_semantic` (line 1078), lines 1134-1146.
  - Only the reply body is served from the proxy's LRU (transport_semantic.py:3185-3192).
- **Fix**: "so a SAME answer is never re-sent or re-stored; the far side still runs the request to know that nothing changed."

**15. Where**: §26 "The cache", paragraph 2
- **Text**: "And underneath, the references are written through to durable storage — the node's own database — so they survive a restart."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - References are in-memory dicts (transport_semantic.py:318-319).
  - `upsert_ref` / `load_all_refs` (proxy/cache/semcache_db.py, daemon/cache/semcache_db.py) have no callers.
  - The SAME caches on both sides are LRU only, `[SAME_CACHE_IS_MEMORY_ONLY_V1]` (transport_semantic.py:3108; session.py:1167, 1254).
  - Only templates persist, in each node's local MySQL (core/store.py:160, 216 "the template store is LOCAL to every node").
- **Fix**: "Underneath, the learned templates are written to the node's own database and survive a restart. References and SAME answers live in memory only: after a reboot the first exchange of each kind goes FULL and rebuilds its reference." Then remove "The cache is therefore both hot and durable … learned templates." or change it to "templates durable, references hot."
- **Same fix needed**: §27 table row "Large, fast disks": change "write-through reference caches that survive a restart" to "learned templates that survive a restart".

**16. Where**: §26 FOR THE GUILD
- **Text**: "A list is held whole up to sixty-four items and then handled differently; why sixty-four?"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**: `DEFAULT_MAX_ITEMS = 64` and `_max_items()` are defined (core/json_handler.py:87, 406), but nothing calls `_max_items()` (grep of core, proxy, daemon). Arrays are atomic, always sent whole.
- **Fix**: "A list is carried whole as one value however long it is, so one changed item moves the whole list; is that right?"

**17. Where**: §26 FOR THE GUILD
- **Text**: "but the crossover was measured on one machine against one codec."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new)
- **Evidence**: There is no crossover threshold. `_lz4_smart` compresses every payload with LZ4 and keeps the result only if it is smaller (core/codec.py:54-64). The float tolerance is a fixed 1e-9 (codec.py:200-211).
- **Fix**: "A payload is compressed with LZ4 and the compressed form is kept only when it is smaller, which costs a compression on every payload; one codec, never compared against another."

**18. Where**: §27 FNWP-1, intro
- **Text**: "It is what rides the established sockets between node proxies introduced in Part V."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**: The proxy's `_DaemonWorker` dials the peer's daemon on :9009 (`_DAEMON_PORT`, transport_semantic.py:182; connect at line 1101).
- **Fix**: "It is what rides the established sockets from a node's proxy to each peer's daemon on :9009."

**19. Where**: §27 "The pathway, end to end", diagram steps 4 and 8 and the paragraph after it; "Fan-in"; "Fan-out"; "The break from request-reply REST"
- **Text**: "FNWP-1 frame  -->  back over the same socket"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - Each connection is two TCP sockets, both opened by the proxy to daemon :9009.
  - The send socket opens with HELLO `<gw>#<n>` (`[SESSION_TOKEN_V1]`, transport_semantic.py:1103-1107).
  - The return socket opens with HELLO `RETURN:<token>` (`_open_return_channel`, lines 1151-1182, `[RETURN_ONLY_V1]`).
  - The daemon writes replies only on the RETURN socket (daemon/engine/session.py:600-656, 777-793).
  - On the far side, the reader is the daemon, not a proxy handler.
- **Fix**: Change step 4 to "daemon :9009 reader --> handler (BLDC-1)" and step 8 to "FNWP-1 frame --> back over the return socket". Change "the reply home along the very same path" to "the reply home over the return socket of the same pair". Change "one established socket" in Fan-in, Fan-out and the REST diagram to "one established socket pair — requests out on one, replies home on the other". Change "Owning a persistent socket to the peer" to "Owning a persistent socket pair to the peer".

**20. Where**: §27 "The pathway, end to end", paragraph 2
- **Text**: "A short request hash on the frame tells the far side which reference this is measured against"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new)
- **Evidence**:
  - The request hash is sha256 of the destination, path, opcode and the full request values after this message, truncated to 16 B (`_compute_req_hash`, transport_semantic.py:662-668). It names the request, which is how a REPEAT is looked up.
  - The reference a DIFF is applied to is picked by the instance's location fields, which every non-FULL request carries (transport_semantic.py:2546-2558; fnwp_engine.hpp:37-42).
- **Fix**: "A 16-byte request hash on the frame names the request, and the location fields every difference carries tell the far side which instance's reference to apply it to, so the two ends never disagree about what 'last time' was."

**21. Where**: §27 "The frame", paragraph 1
- **Text**: "Every FNWP-1 message is a length-prefixed frame: four bytes of length, then a four-byte magic, an opcode, and the body."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete; new)
- **Evidence**:
  - Requests are [len:4]["FNW1"][op:1][body] (transport_semantic.py:691-697; semcache_wire.py:42-58).
  - Replies carry a 4-byte sequence between the length and the magic: `tagged = struct.pack("!I", seq) + reply` (session.py:792).
- **Fix**: "Every FNWP-1 message is a length-prefixed frame: four bytes of length, then the four-byte magic FNW1, a one-byte opcode, and the body. A reply puts a four-byte sequence number between the length and the magic."

**22. Where**: §27 "The frame", opcode list
- **Text**: "a MISS meaning the hash you sent means nothing to me; a HELLO to open a channel; an ERROR; a SEQ_RESET; and the round-trip probes."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (incomplete; carried over)
- **Evidence**: The C++ wire adds REQ_SAME_MISS 0x22 (ribbit/cpp/include/semwire.hpp:61, 172-175; `[SAME_MISS_FROM_CACHE_V1]`). The live Python wire does not have it (core/semcache_wire.py:68-90). RTT_LOOP 0x62 is in both.
- **Fix**: Append: "In the Ribbit C++ wire, a SAME-MISS: the client no longer holds the answer a SAME named, and asks the far side for it from its cache; the request is never run again. The probes are PING, PONG and LOOP — LOOP being the daemon's verdict that the connection came back to itself."

**23. Where**: §27 "The frame", paragraph 2; and the FOR THE GUILD at the end of Part IX
- **Text**: "That is why there is a SEQ_RESET opcode: two independent counters can drift apart, which would be impossible if the number rode the request, and SEQ_RESET is how they are put back in step."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - `OP_SEQ_RESET` 0x40 is defined (semcache_wire.py:82; semwire.hpp:62), but no live proxy or daemon code sends or handles it (grep: only the wire codecs and tests).
  - The counters align because both restart at zero on a new connection: the proxy sets `_seq = 0` (transport_semantic.py:1228, 1702), and each daemon session sets `_request_seq = 0` (session.py:560).
  - The docstring at semcache_wire.py:200-205 claims the proxy sends it. It does not.
- **Fix**: "The two agree because they count the same events in order on the same connection, and both restart from zero when a connection is made. A SEQ_RESET opcode is defined for putting them back in step without reconnecting; nothing sends it today." In FOR THE GUILD, change "SEQ_RESET exists precisely because the two sides count independently" to "the two sides count independently and are put back in step only by reconnecting (SEQ_RESET is defined and unused)".

**24. Where**: §27 "Independent channel sets", and the Part IX intro
- **Text**: "So FNWP-1 lets a node open independent channel sets to a peer — up to three, each with its own sockets"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT with the settled ruling, and WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - **Ruling**: FNWP frames onto a socket it does not open, so channel sets cannot be an FNWP feature. They are the proxy driver's allocator (proxy/channel_sets.py:29-31, "the set is an artifact of the driver").
  - **Default set is not kept apart**: `_next_free_set_id` hands out set 0, the default set, first (channel_sets.py:65-68, 147-150; test_channel_sets.py:89 expects sets [0,1,2]).
  - **Not wired up**: no production code calls `open_channel_set` (grep: only tests and simulation/run_all.py).
  - **What does exist**: the Ribbit C++ wire keeps large payloads off the semantic socket with a separate data socket (ribbit/cpp/include/dataplane.hpp:18-24, `[HIGH_SPEED_DATA_SOCKET_V1]`).
- **Fix**: "FrogNet's proxy driver — not FNWP, which never opens a socket — has an allocator that can give a peer up to three independent socket sets, each with its own sequence space and threads; set 0 is the default every ordinary call uses, and a fourth protocol overlays the least latency-sensitive set. The allocator is unit-tested; nothing opens a set yet. The Ribbit C++ wire takes a different route to the same end: a payload too large to diff goes, cut into segments, over a separate always-open data socket, so the semantic socket keeps moving."

**25. Where**: §27 "The break from request-reply REST", paragraph 4
- **Text**: "the figure measured on a narrowband, 4800-baud-class link is 44×"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (carried over)
- **Evidence**:
  - Chat 22881970 (March): 44× = 100 concurrent requests in 3.3 s against a 0.7 req/s serial baseline, over a WireGuard tunnel Seattle→NYC→Seattle (~1,500 ms RTT, 1 Gb fiber) between two Raspberry Pis, with a static echo that was all SAME and coalesced.
  - John, chat 627a21cd (04-20): "We have run FrogNet over 4800 baud links. They were ethernet-to-ethernet shaped with delay and jitter". That is a separate run, not the 44× figure.
- **Fix**: "…the figure measured — 100 concurrent requests over a 1,500 ms WireGuard round trip, an echo that answered SAME every time — is 44× the serial baseline. FrogNet has also run over Ethernet shaped to 4800 baud."

**26. Where**: §27 "The break from request-reply REST", paragraph 5
- **Text**: "a single narrow satellite link at the command post feeds a working mesh of dozens instead of buckling under the first few."
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
- **Evidence**: No shared-uplink run is in the code or the chats. The 10-08 report A searched for Starlink and shared uplink and found only marketing copy. No simulator topology tests it.
- **Fix**: "…a single narrow satellite link at the command post should feed a mesh of dozens instead of the first few; that is argued from the byte counts, not yet measured."

**27. Where**: §27, "Threading" paragraph
- **Text**: "The permanent socket is driven by a pool of worker threads, each carrying its own exchange"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new; mechanism)
- **Evidence**:
  - Proxy: each application request has its own thread (ThreadingMixIn, proxy/proxy_main.py:866). Per peer and set, one writer thread puts their frames on the send socket and one reader takes replies off the return socket (transport_semantic.py:869, 1278, 1825-1840).
  - Daemon: execution pools (`fast_exec`, `db_exec`, session.py:740-765) run the requests concurrently, and one writer sends replies (session.py:777).
- **Fix**: "Each application request runs on its own thread; one writer per peer puts their frames on the send socket and one reader takes replies off the return socket, while on the far side a pool of threads executes the requests at once. Requests from many senders and replies to many receivers cross the wire together, not taken in turns."

Checked: 78 claims; 27 problems.

# Report E — 14_Part_X_Ribbit_Handlers_and_Domain_Protocols.md (against the 2026-10-11 tree)

I had the conversation tools and used them. Paths are relative to TREE/opt/frognet_semantic (written as FN/) or
FN/ribbit/examples/communicator (written as COMMS/) unless stated otherwise.

What the code still matches, so it needs no entry:
- UnRESTHandler: ROLE_NAME, learn_request_template, extract_request_dynamic, rebuild_reply; the score default of -1.0; advertise returns at once when ROLE_NAME is None; hostReset() calls advertise() (FN/core/unrest_handler.py:90-202).
- Registries: FORMAT_HANDLERS holds json/xml/html/text/raw plus sotf_media; ROLE_HANDLERS holds mediahost and databasehost (FN/core/format_registry.py:30-58, FN/core/role_registry.py:27-30).
- learn_json_schema is the "older schema learner nothing calls". It has no callers (FN/core/template_utils.py:26).
- HostResetWatcher primes on its first observation and then reconciles on each change (FN/core/frognet_host_reset.py).
- The keyframe hold is real: the newest keyframe is held and a newer one replaces it (COMMS/src/media/server.cpp:306-325). There is one encoder per wanted size, and it is dropped when no viewer wants that size (COMMS/src/media/transcode.cpp:128-175). The frame rate defaults to 24 (COMMS/src/app/main.cpp:366). The video bitrates are 3M/1.2M/600k/300k (COMMS/src/media/transcode.cpp:17-20).

Most of the 10-08 entries are still wrong in today's code. They are re-confirmed below with current line numbers, and new items are added.

---

**1. Where**: Part X intro, FOR THE GUILD
**Text**: "Fifteen opcodes are assigned. Two hundred and forty-one are free"
**Verdict**: WRONG (for Ribbit)
**Evidence**: FN/ribbit/cpp/include/semwire.hpp:60-62 assigns 16 opcodes, including OP_REQ_SAME_MISS 0x22. The Python core (FN/core/semcache_wire.py:73-98) has 15.
**Fix**: "Sixteen opcodes are assigned in Ribbit's wire (fifteen in the original Python core). Two hundred and forty are free"

**2. Where**: §28 worked example "Which slots a handler fills"
**Text**: "boardgame       —  (no-op)      implemented     implemented"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**:
- FN/core/role_registry.py:27-30: ROLE_HANDLERS = mediahost, databasehost only.
- FN/proxy/proxy_main.py:272-273: "[GAME_ORIGIN_REMOVED_V1] John, 2026-09-25: core/game_origin.py is outdated and removed".
- TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet_prune_removed.sh:249-261 deletes core/game_role.py and install_boardgamehost.sh ("retired by af0aa9b (bundles and games)").
- Chats 7fdcda81 and 24e6c9e1 record the removal of the game origin, the boardgame role, game_role.py and the game simulations.
**Fix**: Delete the boardgame row.

**3. Where**: §28 "The flagship extensions — SotF and the game protocol", the tic-tac-toe worked example, and the backgammon lead-in
**Text**: "the same _game machinery runs FrogNet's real games — connect four, reversi, backgammon, hearts, liar's dice — each just a different rulebook over the one shared-memory protocol."; "X moves — an ordinary POST carrying _game:"; "Take backgammon — a real game in the set above"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: The same as item 2. No _game handling or game origin remains anywhere in FN/core, FN/proxy, FN/daemon or FN/discovery: "/game requests take the normal path" (proxy_main.py:273). The only game_role.py left is a frozen test reference copy (FN/ribbit/cpp/tests/reference/core/game_role.py).
**Fix**: Recast the section as a design example, in the conditional: "A turn-based game would be the opposite extreme ... A game handler would be the authority: a move arrives, it checks the turn, marks the cell, writes the board back." Then add: "An early /game origin and a boardgame role were built and were removed on 25 September 2026; FrogNet ships no games." Delete "the same _game machinery runs FrogNet's real games — connect four, reversi, backgammon, hearts, liar's dice — each just a different rulebook over the one shared-memory protocol." Change "a real game in the set above" to "a game".

**4. Where**: §28, the paragraph after "Backgammon on the wire"
**Text**: "an unchanged turn collapses to a four-byte SAME"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT. The table directly above says 21 bytes, and the code agrees with the table.
**Evidence**: FN/core/semcache_wire.py:160-164 builds a SAME from MAGIC "FNW1" (4 bytes), the opcode (1 byte) and a 16-byte same_id, which is 21 bytes.
**Fix**: "an unchanged turn collapses to a 21-byte SAME"

**5. Where**: §28 "The lifecycle slot"
**Text**: "each handler folds any service-specific facts its own probe gathers into that picture — the media handler, for instance, adds whether OpenCV2 is present"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- FN/core/role_publish.py:60-72 probes once and passes the same blob to every handler's advertise(). No handler adds fields.
- Nothing checks for OpenCV: there is no OpenCV or cv2 check in TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet_capability_probe.sh or in FN/core.
- The media gate is ffmpeg plus libvpx (FN/core/sotf_handler.py:331-338).
**Fix**: "The node runs its capability probe once and every role handler publishes that same blob as its <role>/capability tuple; the media election gates on what the probe found (ffmpeg with libvpx)."

**6. Where**: §28 "The lifecycle slot"
**Text**: "Advertising is a dual write — onto the deterministic control name where the election reads its inputs, and onto the floating data name where ordinary consumers see it"
**Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
**Evidence**: FN/core/unrest_handler.py:160-181 [MIRROR_ONLY_WHEN_IT_IS_A_DIFFERENT_MACHINE_V1] skips the data write when both names resolve, through /etc/hosts, to the same address.
**Fix**: Append "— the second write is skipped while both names resolve to the same machine."

**7. Where**: §28 "The lifecycle slot", §29 opening, §30 opening (new)
**Text**: "so the very fact that a node can host a role is itself a tuple in shared memory, put there by the handler, scored by everyone"; "Every role-bearing handler on every node in the island runs its lifecycle reconcile — hostReset — and each re-advertises its capability into the new space."
**Verdict**: STALE (it applies only to FrogNet mode)
**Evidence**:
- FN/core/role_publish.py:56-63 [CAPABILITY_TUPLES_OFF_IN_REGION_V1]: on an FD&F region (RINA_RAM set in /opt/fdf/var/join.env), publish_all() returns without probing or writing. Capability there is a fact the tunnel daemon announces ([CAPABILITY_IS_A_FACT_V1]).
- FN/discovery/live.py:815-823 likewise skips the publish in region mode.
**Fix**: Add one sentence after the first quote: "That is FrogNet's own mode. On an FD&F region the <role>/capability tuples are not written; the election reads the node's capability from the facts the tunnel daemon announces."

**8. Where**: §29 worked example "Island A repopulates its own scratchpad"
**Text**: "its transient DB is empty.  the merge fires hostReset() everywhere:"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT. It contradicts §29 ("What fires it is not the merge reaching out to them") and §30 ("Nothing calls it on a node's behalf").
**Evidence**: Each node's own merge calls publish_all() for that node only (FN/discovery/live.py:823-830). The daemon's watcher detects that the control address moved (FN/core/role_publish.py:115-138).
**Fix**: "its transient DB is empty.  each node notices the move and re-advertises for itself:"

**9. Where**: §30 "The trigger is an observation, not a message"
**Text**: "every process that holds live modules — the daemon, the proxy, the Communicator shell, each bundle application — already has a loop, and in that loop it does one trivial thing: it resolves the control name"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- Only the daemon starts the watcher (FN/daemon/daemon_main.py:86-87 start_publish_watcher), with a 15 s interval (FN/core/role_publish.py:115).
- The proxy publishes once at boot and runs no watcher (FN/proxy/proxy_main.py:1019-1020).
- Bundles are retired (frognet_prune_removed.sh:65-66, 249).
- The current Communicator (COMMS/src) has no hostReset or watcher.
- The watcher resolves through /etc/hosts, not DNS (role_publish.py:93-107 [HOSTS_ONLY_V1]).
**Fix**: "The daemon runs a watcher that, every 15 seconds, resolves databasehost_control.frognet from /etc/hosts and compares the answer to the one it got last time. If the address is different, it reconciles. If it is the same, it does nothing at all. The proxy publishes once at boot; the discovery merge publishes for its own node on each merge."

**10. Where**: §30 opening
**Text**: "There is one implementation of advertise in the entire system"
**Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
**Evidence**: There are two: FN/core/unrest_handler.py:134 and the C++ port FN/ribbit/cpp/include/unrest_handler.hpp (UnRESTHandler::advertise).
**Fix**: "There is one implementation of advertise per platform, on the base class, and every role gets it identically."

**11. Where**: §30 "It is not confined to handlers"
**Text**: "Nothing registers it, nothing is wired, no interface is declared. It answers the verb, so it gets called."
**Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
**Evidence**: The watcher calls only the modules its get_modules callable returns. In the daemon that is a fixed list, [RolePublishReconcile(logger)] (FN/core/role_publish.py:128-131). A module must be put in that list to be called. The getattr(m, "hostReset") duck-typing is real (FN/core/frognet_host_reset.py:38-40).
**Fix**: "No interface is declared: any module the process hands its watcher is called if it has a hostReset(); one that has none is skipped."

**12. Where**: §29 "Re-establishing more than capability"
**Text**: "The media handler leans on this on purpose, nulling its control reference so the next frame re-FULLs the control plane against the new host."
**Verdict**: STALE (it lives only in the simulator)
**Evidence**: SotFMediaCodex.hostReset sets _ctl_ref = None (FN/core/sotf_handler.py:549-553). SotFMediaCodex is constructed only in FN/simulation (sim_sotf_media_codex.py, run_movies.py, run_hardware_loop.py). The deployed Communicator (comms-ram, comms-media, comms-app) does not use it.
**Fix**: "The Python media codex in the simulator leans on this on purpose, nulling its control reference so the next frame re-FULLs the control plane against the new host."

**13. Where**: Part X FOR THE GUILD (the classifier)
**Text**: "Content-Type is a weak hint and the body decides"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: FN/core/format_registry.py:136-151, detect_request_handler. A Content-Type containing "json" or "xml" returns that handler before the body is examined. The body decides only for HTML, and when there is no hint.
**Fix**: "Content-Type decides for JSON and XML, HTML must also look like HTML, and otherwise the body decides, resolving to json, xml, html, text or raw."

**14. Where**: same paragraph
**Text**: "a body that falls through to raw learns nothing and diffs against nothing, so every send of it is a FULL"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: RawFormatHandler learns a one-field template whose baseline is the whole body, and it extracts that field (FN/core/text_handler.py RawFormatHandler.learn_request_template and extract_request_dynamic). An identical repeat can be SAME. Any change resends the whole body as that one value.
**Fix**: "a body that falls through to raw is learned as a single field, so a repeat is SAME and any change resends the whole body"

**15. Where**: §31 "What we are doing instead" (Server) and its table row, §32 "What was held to be impossible", §33 "A role is which planes you hold" (new for the current Communicator)
**Text**: "SotF's mediahost is an elected role, not a box. It floats to a capable node, one per LAN, and exists because a call needs it."; "A media server no one installed, that elects itself onto whatever node is fit and dissolves when the call ends."; "It exists because a call needs it and dissolves when the call ends; nobody installs it, nobody points at it"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**:
- The current Communicator's media server is comms-media on port 8994 (COMMS/src/media/main.cpp:20). On a node, TREE/opt/fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh starts it beside comms-ram :8800, and on the droplet it runs as a service.
- It is found through the pond's where cell (fdf-where comms ram=self:8800 media=self:8994, fdf-comms.sh:35-37), and it keeps running between calls.
- The elected mediahost role (SotFMediaHandler, FN/core/role_registry.py) belongs to the FrogNet Python side and does not place comms-media.
**Fix**: "In the FrogNet implementation the media host was an elected role, mediahost, gated on ffmpeg with libvpx. The current Communicator's media server, comms-media on port 8994, is started on a node by fdf-comms.sh (or runs on the droplet) and says where it is in the pond's where cell; every Communicator finds it there, with nothing typed. Nobody points at it by address; it is not elected and it does not dissolve between calls."

**16. Where**: §31 "What we are doing instead" (Server) and its table row, §33 "A role is which planes you hold"
**Text**: "It mixes audio as the sum of the others and video as a grid of the others, minus your own image, composed per recipient."; "The host composes, for every recipient individually, a personalized mix: audio summed from all the other participants, video laid out as a grid of the other participants"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**:
- fan_audio forwards each block to every other participant (COMMS/src/media/server.cpp:105-114).
- Video is passed through, or transcoded once per wanted size per source (server.cpp:226-246; transcode.cpp:128-175).
- No grid compositor exists. Each receiver mixes the voices it receives (FNAV-SPEC.md, "Mixer").
- Minus-self holds (peers_of).
**Fix**: "It fans each participant's audio and video to every other participant, never back to the sender, transcoding a source once for each smaller size a viewer needs; each receiver mixes the voices it gets." In the table, change "mixes minus-self" to "fans minus-self".

**17. Where**: §31 "Starting from the answer everyone already has"; §33 "Two planes, one handler" (new)
**Text**: "The media channels are raised the same way the proxy and daemon raise theirs — the same channel bring-up FrogNet already uses everywhere else."; "The data plane is the channel pair, raised the same way the proxy and daemon raise their connections, so the media inherits the network's existing transport"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: The media planes are plain TCP sockets that comms-app opens to comms-media (COMMS/src/fnav/net.cpp, plane.cpp:67). They carry the fnav frame layer (COMMS/src/fnav/frame.hpp:6). Neither the proxy's channel_sets nor FNWP is involved.
**Fix**: "The media planes are two ordinary TCP connections from the Communicator to its media server, each carrying length-prefixed media frames. No new transport was invented; it is TCP, made never to wait."

**18. Where**: §33 "Two planes, one handler"
**Text**: "leave the socket able to block on receive, and gate only the send on writability"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: Every socket is non-blocking, receive included: [ALL_NONBLOCKING_V1] COMMS/src/fnav/plane.cpp:67 and COMMS/python/frogcomms/fnav.py:172.
**Fix**: "Every socket is non-blocking, receive included; both directions ask the socket first (writable? readable?), and a frame that will not fit is dropped whole."

**19. Where**: §33 "The ladder — from bullfrog to pulse", the table
**Text**: "L4 SOLO        mono audio (video shed)                  mic"; "L3 VOICE       mono audio                               mic"; "L2 WHISPER     plaintext text                           --"; "L1 BEACON      token vocabulary                         --"; "L0 PULSE       presence                                 --"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**:
- The rung names, and L4 through L0 as distinct behaviours, came from sotf_ladder.py. That file is not in the tree. It is referenced only by TREE/usr/local/bin/install_mediahost.sh:122 and by tests, and FNAV-SPEC.md documents it as the old module.
- In current code every rung below L5 means "no video": size_for_rung returns -1 for rung < 5 (COMMS/src/comms/call.cpp:36, COMMS/python/frogcomms/media.py:28-30).
- Below L5 the code has only the shrinking gray sizes, still reported as L5, and then audio only (call.cpp:302-318).
- Nothing sends a text, token or presence rung. The rung names appear nowhere in COMMS/src or COMMS/python.
**Fix**: Replace the table with the code's ladder:
```
rung      carries                                   needs
L8        1920x1080 colour video + audio  3 Mb/s     camera
L7        1280x720  colour video + audio  1.2 Mb/s   camera
L6        854x480   colour video + audio  600 kb/s   camera
L5        640x360 gray, then 480x360 / 320x240 /
          160x120 gray (4:3)  + audio   300/250/150/60 kb/s
below L5  audio only                                  mic
audio: Opus 24 kb/s, 16 kHz mono
```
Keep the names only as history: "The rungs once carried names, BULLFROG at L8 down to PULSE at L0, and a text floor; the running code has the video sizes and audio only."

**20. Where**: §33 "The ladder", the table footer
**Text**: "audio on every rung that carries it: 16 kHz mono s16le"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: Audio is Opus at 24 kb/s. The 16 kHz mono s16le is the PCM before encoding, and PCM goes on the wire only as an operator choice (COMMS/src/comms/audio.hpp:4-6; FNAV-SPEC.md "Audio: Opus, 24 kb/s ... [AUDIO_IS_OPUS_V1]").
**Fix**: "audio on every rung that carries it: Opus, 24 kb/s, 16 kHz mono"

**21. Where**: §33 "The ladder", the ceiling paragraph
**Text**: "A camera and a mic ceiling at BULLFROG."; "A microphone alone, or a deliberate no-video, ceils at SOLO, the top audio-only rung. Neither, and the ceiling is WHISPER."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- ceiling_for picks the largest ladder size the source fills, with a minimum of L5 (COMMS/src/comms/call.cpp:41-44). A 720p camera ceilings at L7.
- With no camera there is no video thread at all (call.cpp:81, "if (src_)"), so no SOLO or WHISPER ceiling is computed.
**Fix**: "A camera ceilings at the largest rung its picture fills: L8 for a 1080p camera, L7 for 720p. With no camera the call sends audio only."

**22. Where**: §33 "How the ladder moves — backpressure"; §33 "What the ladder became", last paragraph
**Text**: "The descent is framerate-first. Hold the resolution and thin the motion — 24 to 15 to 7 frames a second"; "The framerate-first descent described above still holds within a rung."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- The frame rate is fixed at the target: the deadline is 1000000/fps_ (COMMS/src/comms/call.cpp:247).
- The Bearer moves the rung index, and the frame rate is only a trigger for stepping down: below max(10, 75% of target) for more than 3 samples (COMMS/src/fnav/bearer.hpp:6-9, bearer.cpp:16-18).
- No code steps through 24, 15 and 7 fps.
**Fix**: "The frame rate is held at its target, 24 by default; falling below it is itself a reason to step down. Each step down is a smaller picture: 1080p, 720p, 480p, 360p gray, then 480x360, 320x240 and 160x120 gray, then audio only." Delete "The framerate-first descent described above still holds within a rung."

**23. Where**: §33 "How the ladder moves — backpressure"
**Text**: "Drops accumulating means step down; the link going quiet and accepting frames again means there is room to step back up."
**Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
**Evidence**:
- Up requires 3 clean seconds AND 98% of the target frame rate. A rung that failed is held off 15 s, doubling to 300 s (COMMS/src/fnav/bearer.hpp:8-9, 20-21).
- That governs only the sender's upload. Each viewer's size is set by the media server (see item 25).
**Fix**: "Drops accumulating means step down. Up comes only after three clean seconds at full frame rate, and a rung that just failed is held off for 15 seconds, doubling to five minutes."

**24. Where**: §31 "Adaptation"; §31 "Failure"; §32 "What was held to be impossible"; §33 "Why it never dies"
**Text**: "then shedding video for a mono voice bed, then text, then a single presence bit"; "down to text, down to a one-byte \"I am still here,\""; "that thins to a single byte of presence"; "to voice to text to a beacon to the bare pulse of still here"
**Verdict**: WRONG. It is also INCONSISTENT with this Part's own "the ladder floors at a voice rather than at nothing".
**Evidence**: Congestion ends at audio only: "160x120 refused too: audio only" (COMMS/src/comms/call.cpp:310-312) and target SIZES.size() = audio only (server.cpp:116-121). FNAV-SPEC.md: "Protected floor: congestion steps down to the audio rung and stops there". There is no text, beacon or presence rung in code (item 19).
**Fix**: "...then shedding video for the voice, and stopping there." In "Failure": "walks itself from high-definition video down to a gray thumbnail and then to voice alone, and back up". In §33: "rides its link down — 1080p to 720p to 480p to a 360p gray picture, smaller still, then voice — and rides it back up". In §32: "that thins to voice alone and thickens back to high definition".

**25. Where**: §33 "What the ladder became"
**Text**: "Adaptation is driven by keyframe queue pressure. Not by a bitrate table and not by an estimate of the link."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**:
- The media server's control_tick steps each viewer by frames delivered against frames aimed, over 2 s windows: HAPPY_FRACTION 0.80, BAD_WINDOWS 2, HAPPY_WINDOWS 3, SETTLE_S 45 (COMMS/src/media/server.cpp:141-192; server.hpp:105-107).
- A viewer's MediaSpeed cap is mapped through the SIZES bitrate table (target_for_cap, server.cpp:116-121).
- Keyframe holding only orders delivery; it is not an input to the ladder.
**Fix**: "Adaptation is driven by what actually reached the viewer: frames delivered against frames aimed, per two-second window, stepping down under 80% for two windows and up after three good windows and 45 settled seconds, plus any cap the viewer set."

**26. Where**: §33 "What the ladder became"
**Text**: "two persistent sockets, each carrying FNWP, declared at connect"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: The planes carry the fnav frame layer [len u32][kind u8][srclen u16][src][payload]. Kind 3 is PLANE and declares 'A' or 'V' (COMMS/src/fnav/frame.hpp:6-31), not FNW1-framed FNWP opcodes. The settled ruling is that FNWP is the framed BLDC-1 exchange.
**Fix**: "two persistent sockets, each carrying length-prefixed media frames, the first frame on each declaring audio or video"

**27. Where**: §31 "Song of the Frogs — The Same Problem"
**Text**: "and it rides its own channel sets so it never blocks, or is blocked by, anything else"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: Channel sets exist only in the proxy (FN/proxy/channel_sets.py). The Communicator's planes do not use them (item 17); its isolation comes from separate audio and video sockets.
**Fix**: "and it rides its own sockets, audio apart from video, so it never blocks, or is blocked by, anything else"

**28. Where**: §34 "REST and Ribbit, Side by Side", Security row
**Text**: "Proximity + mesh membership; no per-service TLS on internal traffic."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT with John's settled security stance. Report F (Part XII) also found node services bound to 0.0.0.0, so "proximity" is not the mechanism.
**Evidence**: John's ruling: "I do not force security on the system, but I do not prevent it. The tunnel is wg, which is basic."
**Fix**: "None forced: the inter-site tunnel is WireGuard; access control and further layers are the builder's to add."

# Report F — 15_Part_XI, 16_Part_XII_Security, 17_Part_XII (XII½)

Conversation tools: available. Checked against TREE = /home/claude/tree1011 (2026-10-11). All 19 problems from the 10-08 report are still in the text; each was re-checked against today's code below. Items 20-25 are new.

## 15_Part_XI_Ribbit_The_Programming_Surface.md

**1.**
- **Where**: 35f, "Concurrency does not go away"
- **Text**: "section 54 carries it with the other honest edges"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**: 22_Part_XV:19 is "### 54. Collectives Without a Schedule" (PyTorch). The Region-policy honest edge is in 23_Part_XVI:126, "### 61. Smaller Things, Named Honestly" (line 140, "Region policy is not one universal consistency rule").
- **Fix**: "section 61 carries it with the other honest edges"

**2.**
- **Where**: 35f, "Concurrency does not go away"
- **Text**: "What FrogNet’s put() gives you today is last-write-wins against the three-coordinate key"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with the settled ruling: the default Region holds current state, not history, as a Region policy)
- **Evidence**: TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/defaults/api/frognet_tuples.py:139-145 `put()` stamps ts and calls `tuples_put`, which replaces the cell. The C++ client has the same contract: ribbit/cpp/include/ram_client.hpp:52-56 says "A write replaces the cell". The own=True part of the sentence is correct (frognet_tuples.py:103-110,141: removed at process exit).
- **Fix**: "What FrogNet’s put() gives you today is the default Region's policy: one current value per three-coordinate key, with no history"

**3.**
- **Where**: 35f, "Libraries and bundles"
- **Text**: "the FrogNet deployment packaging for a complete application, conventionally under /etc/frognet_bundles/"
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**: TREE/usr/local/bin/frognet_prune_removed.sh:66 `gone "etc/frognet_bundles"`. installer/frognet_reset.sh:161 `nuke /etc/frognet_bundles`. MANIFEST-2026-10-11.md: the Communicator is `opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator/` (comms-app r3).
- **Fix**: "A bundle is a complete application packaged to run on FrogNet. The Communicator ships as a self-contained example under /opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator. That is an implementation convention, not a Ribbit requirement." Also change "the worked FrogNet bundle" to "the worked example".

## 16_Part_XII_Security.md

**4.**
- **Where**: Part intro, paragraph 1
- **Text**: "No discoverable application endpoints. No exposed application protocols. A scanner sweeping the address does not find an application to talk to, because the applications never needed a door."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new)
- **Evidence**: A Ribbit application is a TCP listener with an HTTP path.
  - ribbit/cpp/include/ram_host.hpp:46-47: `default_listen()` is `"0.0.0.0:8800"`, "every address". api_path is `/<vendor_id>-api` (:44).
  - fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh:5,22,26: comms-ram listens on `0.0.0.0:8800`, comms-media on `0.0.0.0:8994`, with the instruction "Open TCP 8800 and 8994 to the players".
  - The FD&F region's RAM manager is `rina-host --listen 0.0.0.0:51888` on the droplet's public address: etc/systemd/system/fdf-ram-frognet.service:9; DEPLOY-REGION-2026-10-09.md:25 `ufw allow 51888/tcp`; fdf-region-join:13 defaults to `165.232.153.144:51888`; Android Identity.kt:36.
- **Fix**: "Ribbit does not publish one endpoint per operation. Each vendor's memory is one listener, its RAM host, on one path (/<vendor>-api), and only participants speak to it. Where that listener sits decides who can reach it: on a node behind its uplink, nobody off the mesh can; on a public host, such as the FD&F region's RAM manager on StreamingFrog:51888, anyone on the internet can."

**5.**
- **Where**: §36, paragraphs 1-2
- **Text**: "every service the node runs — Apache, the proxy, the daemon, discovery, both database planes — listens on the 10-net and only there. None binds a public address"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - etc/systemd/system/frognet-proxy.service:45 binds `0.0.0.0:80`. frognet-daemon.service:48 binds `0.0.0.0:9009`. frognet-dashboard.service:36 binds `0.0.0.0:9100`. frognet-mediahost.service:33 binds `0.0.0.0:9000`.
  - etc/setup_iptables:49-56 sets `-P INPUT ACCEPT` and accepts on every interface in $FROGNET_INTERFACES. That list is every up physical interface, the uplink included (usr/local/bin/mapInterfaces:171-172).
  - A node has no WireGuard listen port. frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:503-515 writes no ListenPort, and the node dials `droplet_endpoint`.
- **Fix**: "Node services bind all interfaces. What keeps the internet off them is where the node sits, not what they bind to. A node is behind its uplink's NAT, its 10.x addresses are not routable from outside, and it opens no tunnel port: it dials the broker. Put a node's uplink on a public address and those ports can be reached unless you filter them." In paragraph 2, drop "the absence of any listening service on the public side".

**6.**
- **Where**: §36, "What a scan actually returns" (node block)
- **Text**: "all scanned ports: filtered      nothing answers"
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
- **Evidence**: Chat 1f49d502 (08-17) records only the broker scan, and no node scan transcript has been found. The block's second line ("the wg bearer drops unauthenticated packets") does not apply, because a node has no wg listener (item 5).
- **Fix**: Replace the block with: "A node normally has no public address of its own. Behind its router there is nothing for a scan to reach."

**7.**
- **Where**: §36, "The one surface, named honestly"
- **Text**: "One piece of the pond does face the internet, and the book will not pretend otherwise: the broker."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new; FD&F, 10-09 to 10-11)
- **Evidence**: Next to broker v4, the same droplet now runs the FD&F region:
  - `rina-host` on TCP 51888 (public, ufw-allowed) and `fdf-hub` WireGuard on UDP 50889 (DEPLOY-REGION-2026-10-09.md:50,63).
  - The broker's own API is reached on 8443 as well as 18257 (same table, :63). The broker uvicorn unit itself binds `127.0.0.1:18428` (etc/systemd/system/frognet-broker-v4.service:27).
  - FD&F members reach the RAM manager directly at its public address over FNW1. The bootstrap is in the clear by ruling (PLAN-FROGNET-ON-FDF.md:284 "Bootstrap in the clear ... deferred, ruling stands"; HANDOFF-FDF-2026-10-08.md:27 "security is out of scope for the demo").
- **Fix**: "Two things on the droplet face the internet. One is the FrogNet broker (its service port and its WireGuard ports). The other is the FD&F region: its RAM manager on TCP 51888, reached directly over FNW1 and unencrypted, and its hub's WireGuard on UDP 50889."

**8.**
- **Where**: §36, worked example and "Two enrollment shapes"
- **Text**: "the only things that talk to it are setup_lillypad and frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh" / "A node is enrolled by a script that redeems a passcode against that port, and the operator who minted the passcode did that from the broker's own command line." / "operator, on the broker      mint a passcode" / "There is no step a stranger can start."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - Callers of /api/v4/register also include frognet-make-gateway.sh, frognet-config-v4, change_pond.bash, frognet-tunnel-register.sh and frognet_setup_v4_helper.bash (grep in usr/local/bin). The tunnel daemon polls `/api/v4/my-channels` every 30 s (frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:86,735-747).
  - Registration checks a pond password, not a passcode (frognet_broker_v4.py:1827-1828).
  - `_verify_password` returns True when the pond has no password (:665-667). frognet-make-gateway.sh:49 says "omit for open".
  - `/api/v4/my-channels` needs only a pubkey (:2206-2209). `/api/v4/bootstrap` is unauthenticated and claims the admin token once (:1661-1690).
  - The scheme defaults to http (frognet-make-gateway.sh:45-46: "the broker port is plain HTTP unless the broker has a TLS cert"). Clients set `CERT_NONE` (frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:744,758), and `group_token` travels in the query string (:740).
- **Fix**: "A node enrolls by running a setup script (frognet-make-gateway.sh, setup_lillypad_v4.bash, frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh) that registers it in a pond with the pond's password. Its tunnel daemon then polls my-channels. There is no web form. The traffic is plain HTTP to the broker's port unless you give the broker TLS. A pond created without a password admits any node that knows its name, so the pond password and TLS on the broker port are the operator's to set." Change the diagram's left column to "operator, on the broker: create the pond (optional password)" and "node -> broker service: register (pond password)". Delete "There is no step a stranger can start."

**9.**
- **Where**: §36, "Return to the broker service on 18257" and "The reason that is true of the console too"
- **Text**: "FNWP-1 carries it, a first contact costs a FULL" / "A capture yields length-prefixed codec frames" / "the broker's web server is itself a FrogNet semantic server"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The broker is FastAPI under uvicorn (frognet_broker_v4.py `@app.post(...)`). The installer binds it `--host 0.0.0.0 --port $PORT` (full_broker.tgz install_frognet_broker.sh:454-462).
  - Clients use urllib (frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:735-763) and curl, not FNW1.
  - Node port-80 interception redirects only `-d 10.0.0.0/8 --dport 80`: etc/setup_iptables:77-80 (OUTPUT and PREROUTING on the LAN interface). Traffic to a public address on 18257 never reaches the proxy or the daemon.
  - The book's own scan shows plain `HTTP/1.1 404` and `{"detail":"Not Found"}`.
- **Fix**: Replace both paragraphs with: "The broker service is plain HTTP, FastAPI under uvicorn, on its own port. A capture of it is ordinary HTTP. FrogNet's port-80 interception applies only to traffic to 10/8, so traffic to the broker never enters the semantic wire. What protects the broker is that it is one small API behind a pond password and an admin token, not that its wire is hard to read."

**10.**
- **Where**: §36, "What we removed after running this"
- **Text**: "a systemd drop-in redefining the listen address had silently discarded the --no-server-header flag the main unit carried"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `grep -r no-server-header TREE` finds nothing. Neither the v4 unit (etc/systemd/system/frognet-broker-v4.service:26-27) nor the installer's listen.conf (install_frognet_broker.sh:462) passes it. A fresh install therefore sends `server: uvicorn`, and "the header is gone" holds only for the hand-fixed droplet.
- **Fix**: "The first run returned server: uvicorn on every response, because the listen drop-in that sets the real bind never passed --no-server-header. The droplet was fixed by hand, and the shipped installer still omits the flag."

**11.**
- **Where**: §36, "What a scan actually returns"
- **Text**: "nmap fingerprinted it against eight different probes" / "Here is everything those eight probes returned."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: The book's own table lists 9 probes (GET, OPTIONS, RTSP + RPC, DNS x2, Help, SSL, terminal-server cookie). Chat 1f49d502 turn 278 shows the same 9.
- **Fix**: "nine different probes" / "those nine probes"

**12.**
- **Where**: §36, "The one surface" and "What the scan does not prove"
- **Text**: "the broker sits outside the mesh, so even this surface is not a path inward" / "holding no node identity and no route inward — so taking it gives you the transport path, not membership"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The broker stores each node's guid, which is the identity key (frognet_broker_v4.py:172,1880-1887). It routes the node subnets inside each pond namespace (broker_namespace_v4.py).
  - Every node accepts `AllowedIPs = 10.0.0.0/8` from the broker (frognet_broker_v4.py:74,1491 → frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:513).
  - Nodes accept all INPUT and FORWARD on wg* (etc/setup_iptables:49-56,66-70). Root on the broker can therefore originate packets to any node's :80 or :9009.
- **Fix**: "The broker routes the pond's whole 10/8, and every node accepts 10/8 from it, so root on the broker can reach node services. What it lacks is the semantic state those services hold, not a route to them." Restate Oracle 1 to match.

**13.**
- **Where**: §36, "Take the broker. Now show me the pivot."
- **Text**: "so inter-site traffic is in the clear at that seam -- it is inside the encryption boundary. It holds no node identity and no pond secret."
- **Verdict**: WRONG / INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**:
  - The paragraph says "in the clear at that seam" twice. "Inside the encryption boundary" contradicts the ruling: WireGuard ends at the broker, so the traffic there is outside the boundary.
  - The broker DB holds `password_hash` per pond and chorus (frognet_broker_v4.py:118,151) and `guid` per node (:172).
  - Tunnel private keys are not persisted ([IN_MEMORY_KEYS], :1357-1364). They are written empty but live in the broker's kernel wg interfaces.
  - "Unreconstructable" contradicts §38 "The honest boundary". The count also drifts: "Two things" becomes "a fourth property", then "the four".
- **Fix**: "The broker terminates a separate tunnel to each site and forwards between them in the kernel, so inter-site traffic is in the clear at that seam: it is outside the WireGuard boundary. The broker holds pond password hashes and node GUIDs. Its tunnel private keys live only in its kernel interfaces and are never written to disk. It runs none of the semantic stack, but a capture taken there from the start of a conversation learns the templates, exactly as §38 says." Delete the repeated clause and "unreconstructable rather than merely unread". Make the count consistent ("Three things hold").

**14.**
- **Where**: Part intro paragraph 3, and §39 paragraph 2
- **Text**: "FrogNet implements no cryptography of its own. The bearer crypto is WireGuard’s" / "FrogNet implements no cryptography of its own. The encryption in the system is WireGuard"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - ribbit/cpp/include/fnwp_server.hpp:21,32,110-111 calls OpenSSL `HMAC(EVP_sha256(), ...)` to make the SAME id. SHA via openssl/sha.h is used in fnwp_client.hpp:29 and semtpl.hpp:48.
  - The broker may run TLS (frognet-make-gateway.sh:45).
  - The FD&F hub is WireGuard too (fdf/hub/apply_once.py:5,27).
  - Ruling: "The tunnel is wg, which is basic."
- **Fix**: "FrogNet encrypts nothing of its own. It calls OpenSSL for SHA-256 and HMAC-SHA256 to name cache entries, which is integrity, not confidentiality. Confidentiality comes from WireGuard, which is the tunnel and is basic, or from TLS where you configure it."

**15.**
- **Where**: §39, paragraph 3, and §38 "Which is exactly why…"
- **Text**: "Put a WireGuard bearer under a link and that link is encrypted end to end" / "Between sites it is already there, because the cross-internet tunnels are WireGuard by default." / "it crosses a WireGuard bearer, and then the listener never gets the capture at all"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (ruling: inter-site traffic is in the clear at the broker seam)
- **Evidence**: Nodes peer only with the broker (frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:511-512 `droplet_pubkey`/`droplet_endpoint`). The encryption covers each node-to-broker hop, not the path end to end, and the broker is a listener that does see the traffic. FD&F's bootstrap and member-to-RAM-manager traffic is in the clear (PLAN-FROGNET-ON-FDF.md:284).
- **Fix**: "Put a WireGuard bearer under a link and that hop is encrypted. Between sites, each site's hop to the broker is WireGuard. The broker decrypts, forwards in the kernel and re-encrypts, so the traffic is encrypted on the wire but not end to end. If you do not trust the broker host, layer your own cipher on the payload." In §38, change "and then the listener never gets the capture at all" to "and then a listener on the wire gets nothing; the broker still does".

**16.**
- **Where**: §37, last paragraph (and §39 paragraph 1, "the inside is reached only by someone physically near the wire and holding a token")
- **Text**: "nothing routes onto that wire without its say. Being near is necessary; it is not sufficient. A stranger who reaches the wire still meets the identity and membership gates — the GUID in /etc/fnid, the redeemed group token"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - dnsmasq leases `.2-.254` to any client (usr/local/bin/frognet_fixup.sh:356).
  - The proxy (0.0.0.0:80) and daemon (0.0.0.0:9009) take any client. The daemon rejects only non-FNW1 frames (daemon/engine/session.py:870-871).
  - /etc/fnid and GROUP_TOKEN are used only toward the broker (frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:195; frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:740).
  - The only gate on the segment is WPA2-PSK on the node's own AP (internet_tunnels_v3/hostapd.conf.template:38-42). A wired port has none.
  - Ruling: "A disaster area needs to be wide open."
- **Fix**: "On a wired segment, being there is enough: the node hands you an address. On its Wi-Fi, the gate is the WPA2 passphrase. The GUID and group token gate the broker, not the segment. That is deliberate. FrogNet does not force access security, and it does not prevent you from adding it." In §39, replace "and holding a token" with "or holding the Wi-Fi passphrase".

**17.**
- **Where**: §38, paragraph 2
- **Text**: "It also matches no familiar signature." / "there is no envelope announcing what it carries"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - Every frame begins with the 4-byte magic "FNW1" followed by an opcode byte (ribbit/cpp/include/semwire.hpp:24,56,88). It is a fixed signature.
  - The first contact on a new path is REQ_RAW/RESP_RAW, which carries the original HTTP request and response (semwire.hpp:26,28). The sequence number is in the record header ([len][seq][total][offset], dataplane.hpp:26).
- **Fix**: "Each frame carries a 4-byte FNW1 marker and an opcode, so it is recognisable as FrogNet. What it does not carry is the structure: after the first exchange, which on a new path is a RAW frame holding the original HTTP, the frames are references and changed values."

**18.**
- **Where**: Opening FOR THE GUILD panel
- **Text**: "Everything you have just read about FNWP-1 and BLDC-1 is a description"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**: The panel opens Part XII. The preceding part is XI (the programming surface). FNWP-1 and BLDC-1 are covered in 13_Part_IX.
- **Fix**: "Everything this book says about FNWP-1 and BLDC-1 (Part IX) is a description" (or move the panel to the end of Part IX).

**19.**
- **Where**: §36, "What we removed after running this"
- **Text**: "the same failure shape as the docstring in Part VIII that described a flush nothing ever called"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (new)
- **Evidence**: The docstring and flush story is in 11_Part_VII_Adopting_It_Where_a_Network_Already_Exist.md:67. 12_Part_VIII has no "flush".
- **Fix**: "the docstring in Part VII½ that described a flush nothing ever called"

**20.**
- **Where**: All three FOR THE GUILD "Two doors" panels (same text in 15/16/17: 16 lines 13 and 173, 17 lines 63 and 94)
- **Text**: "free software under the GNU GPL v2, no licence to request and no form"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (imprecise licence; new)
- **Evidence**: TREE/COPYRIGHT:6,17,22: "version 2 of the License, and no other version", "SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-only", "This is GPL version 2 ONLY. It is not 'version 2 or later'". Every source header carries GPL-2.0-only (e.g. etc/setup_iptables:5).
- **Fix**: "free software under the GNU GPL, version 2 only (GPL-2.0-only), no licence to request and no form. What you build on it and distribute is GPL-2.0-only too; that is the deal."

**21.**
- **Where**: §36, "Take the broker. Now show me the pivot." (recovery paragraph)
- **Text**: "the whole recovery being a config line, because there was never any state on it worth recovering"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (new)
- **Evidence**:
  - The broker keeps a sqlite DB in /var/lib/frognet_broker_v4 with ponds, password hashes, node guids, subnets and tunnels (frognet_broker_v4.py:110-205; DEPLOY-REGION-2026-10-09.md:67).
  - Nodes rejoin by re-registering, which needs each pond recreated with its password, and the admin token must be bootstrapped again (:1661).
  - The droplet now also hosts the FD&F region's RAM manager. Its region file /opt/fdf/var/area-frognet.perm is the FD&F region's memory.
- **Fix**: "The answer to a compromised broker is a new droplet. Recreate the ponds and their passwords on it and point the nodes at it, and they re-register. The broker's database is a convenience, not a store of anything the network needs. If the same host carries an FD&F RAM manager, its region file goes with it."

**22.**
- **Where**: "The rules, as one table" (intro), 39a "A derivation must not read its own previous output", and 39b
- **Text**: "Every rule below is a doctrine tag that appears verbatim in the source and is checked mechanically" / "The rate at which every participant in a call sends is one pure function — derive_rate(state, ladder) -> geometry." / "one oracle now checks that every self.X(...) resolves to something the class defines"

The rules are kept here as lessons, not as current source." In 39a, put derive_rate in the past tense and add: "Today each viewer's rate is set by the media server from what that viewer's link drains and its download slider, and the server transcodes the one upload down to it." In 39b, write "one oracle checked".

**23.**
- **Where**: "Say why the answer is empty", and the table row DIAGNOSTICS_GO_WHERE_THEY_CAN_BE_READ_V1
- **Text**: "two hours went into reading the absence of printed output"

The examples are communicator, redis, lispers.net and chat.
- **Fix**: "The Ribbit C++ platform (a vendor's RamHost and RamClient) and the examples built on it are one implementation of that model, not its definition."

Programming-surface check (Part XI 35f pseudocode): consistent with code. ram_client.hpp:52-58 gives `write(service, variable, instance, bag_json)` and `read(service, variable, instance, after, wait_s)`, and a read with after>=0 and wait_s>0 is held. The book calls its lines pseudocode and says "ordinary reads and writes plus held reads". No problem.
Port-80 interception: only 10/8:80 (setup_iptables:77-80). It bears on item 9.

Checked: 152 claims; 25 problems.

**4.**

Anything else raises `ValueError("config must contain either 'networks:' or 'generate:'")`. The netdef.py docstring (lines 30-41) nests `net1:` under `networks:`. The file as printed does not load.

The tree has only `netdef.py`, `hardware.py`, `machine.py`, `harness_topo.py`, and the early FrogSim scripts in `tests/frogsim/` and `frogsim/` (1,606 lines in all). Its network definition (netdef.py) ships in simulation/; its report, coverage registry, negative controls, tuple store and namespace app plane are not yet in the published tree." Alternatively, ship FrogSim.

But `validate()` reports 299 pairs unreachable, because `_reaches(..., max_hops=12)` (system.py:510) is still 12. With 64 hops the count is 0. The 0 came from the FrogSim copy, where the limit was raised (96bb1e7e).
- **Fix**: Raise `max_hops` in `discovery/sim/system.py:510`. Or print "0 of 2,256 ordered pairs unreachable (with the hop limit raised; the shipped validator, still at twelve, reports 299 false failures)".

**8.**
- **Where**: 18, "What it found", "Four more, briefly"

But the shipped `discovery/sim/system.py:510` still uses 12, and so does `tests/frogsim/fabric.py`. The limit was raised only in FrogSim. Also, `System.remove_node` (system.py:356-360) still does not detach a removed AP's guests, so the "helper that removed an access point" bug is also unfixed in the tree.

The current Communicator is comms-app (C++/Qt) against comms-ram :8800 and comms-media :8994, and no simulator tier runs it.
- **Fix**: "The earlier Python A/V phone placed a call between two nodes and the far relay registered the peer; the current Communicator (comms-app, comms-ram, comms-media) has not been run in the simulator."

**10.**
- **Where**: 18, "Where it stops", NOT DEMONSTRATED paragraph
- **Text**: "And runs are checked against golden baselines captured from real hardware — the three-node baseline and the kernel-FIB chain, confirmed end to end — which is the difference between a good harness and a qualified simulator."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The sentence sits under NOT DEMONSTRATED but states a positive.
  - It narrows the baselines to two, but all 20 `simulation/baselines/*.json` carry `"source": "hardware"`.
  - "hardware" means a run on the real-kernel netns backend: `live_engine.py:344` sets `source = "hardware" if mode == "real"`.
  - `simulation/AI_README.md`: "No run has yet predicted something a real box then confirmed. State the first, never the second."
- **Fix**: "And while all twenty built-in topologies have baselines recorded on the real-kernel tier that the offline engine reproduces byte for byte, no run has yet predicted something a real box then confirmed — which is the difference between a good harness and a qualified simulator."

**11.**
- **Where**: 18, ch.42 "The monitor — what the network looks like from here"
- **Text**: "FrogNet_monitor runs on a node"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: The command is `usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor`, lowercase. It runs `cd /usr/local/bin; python3 -m frognet_monitor_py`.
- **Fix**: "frognet_monitor runs on a node"

**12.**
- **Where**: 18, same section
- **Text**: "A host reads as online or degraded — degraded meaning its round-trip has gone past 250 milliseconds — or as idle or down."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: Neither monitor matches this sentence.
  - Python, `usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor_py/draw_dashboard.py:141-177`, decides in this order: DOWN (echo failed), ONLINE (no LinkQuality entry), IDLE (sample_count 0), FLAKY (success_rate < 0.8), SLOW (saturation_ratio_p95 ≥ 4.0), DEGRADED (saturation_ratio_p95 ≥ 1.5, or no baseline and p95 ≥ 1000 ms), else ONLINE. Nothing uses 250 ms.
  - C++, `usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor_cpp/src/dashboard.cpp:71-89`: DOWN, SAT (sat ≥ 0.90), BUSY (≥ 0.70), SLOW (> 1000 ms), FAIR (> 250 ms), OK.
- **Fix**: "A host reads as ONLINE, IDLE, FLAKY, SLOW, DEGRADED or DOWN. DEGRADED means its p95 round-trip is at least 1.5 times what that link has measured it should achieve, or over a second when no baseline exists yet; SLOW is 4 times or more; FLAKY is under 80% success. (The C++ monitor uses its own words: OK, FAIR past 250 ms, SLOW past a second, BUSY, SAT, DOWN.)"

**13.**
- **Where**: 18, "pipe_workload — the same call, four ways"
- **Text**: "chat shapes the traffic the way real conversation does, with roughly a fifth of it non-message noise"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `var/www/html/semantic_test_chat.php:22-34` serves a 50-message window with `$NEW_PROB = 20`. That is a 20% chance per poll of a new message, and otherwise the window is unchanged. `usr/local/bin/pipe_workload.py:84` describes it as "50-msg chat window, ~20% new-msg rate".
- **Fix**: "chat polls a 50-message rolling window in which roughly one poll in five carries a new message, so you get SAME and DIFF mixed…"

**14.**
- **Where**: 18, "A polled endpoint, which is the ordinary case"
- **Text**: "A hundred and thirty-five bytes per request. Steady state 99.7%, a factor of three hundred and twenty-nine."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (two runs mixed)
- **Evidence**: Chat 22c2753a gives the 1 MB numbers:
  - Serial phase: 25 requests, 94,447 bytes actual (about 3,778 B/request), 99.7%, 329x.
  - Burst: 269 concurrent, 36,302 bytes actual, which is the "135 bytes per request".

  So 135 B/request and 329x come from different phases. The table's own rows show this: 23.5 Mbps / 71 kbps is about 330x, but 43.8 Mbps / 4.7 kbps is about 9,300x. Also, "269 concurrent" is the burst, so "polled, not burst" does not describe the table.
- **Fix**: "Serial, it is about 3.8 KB per request — 99.7%, a factor of 329. At 269 concurrent it is 135 bytes per request."

**15.**
- **Where**: 18, same section, cross-checks paragraph
- **Text**: "Per-request cost is identical at 25 serial and at 269 concurrent" … "And the 1.24x by which wire bytes exceed body bytes"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (misattributed)
- **Evidence**: 22c2753a:
  - Identical per-request cost holds only for would-bytes: 1,244,31x per request in every 1 MB phase. Actual bytes per request differ by about 28x between serial and burst (item 14).
  - 1.24x was measured on the `kb` run (13.1 KB body). For the 1 MB run the same chat says "1.13× the 1077 KB body".
- **Fix**: "Would-have-been cost per request is identical at 25 serial and at 269 concurrent, so the denominator is not moving with concurrency. … And the 1.13x by which would-have-been bytes exceed the body is packet headers and WireGuard encapsulation — named, rather than quietly absorbed."
