- **Fix**: "Flags: --name, --ip (10.X.Y.1), --broker-host, --broker-port, --broker-scheme, --pond, --db-pass, --wifi-psk, --no-ssid-projection / --ssid-projection, --ap-iface, --country, --preserve, --from-repo, --rebuild-venv."

**C2. Where**: 28, Networking & radio, ssid_projection.conf (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "SSID-on/off and Wi-Fi country, seeded from --no-ssid-projection / --country."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
- **Evidence**: frognet_install.sh:1937-1958 writes SSID_PROJECTION, AP_IFACE and WIFI_COUNTRY.
- **Fix**: "SSID_PROJECTION, AP_IFACE and WIFI_COUNTRY, seeded from --no-ssid-projection, --ap-iface and --country. Read by frognet-netstart."

**C3. Where**: 28, Networking & radio, gateways.conf
- **Text**: "Gateway / uplink facts for a node with a non-FrogNet way out."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: setup_lillypad_v4.bash writes NETWORK_NAME, GATEWAY_IP, ADMIN_IP, GW1 and GW2 on every node (the 10-08 lead, still true). frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:179 reads NETWORK_NAME from it.
- **Fix**: "Every node's identity facts: NETWORK_NAME, GATEWAY_IP (.1), ADMIN_IP (.2), GW1/GW2."

**C4. Where**: 28, Broker, frognet-pond-bootstrap
- **Text**: "Timer that retries the broker every couple of minutes until it answers a health check, then proceeds and disables itself."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - frognet-pond-bootstrap.sh:21-29 (`[DEFERRED_BROKER_SETUP_V1]`): it is event-driven, run from the tail of runMerge.bash (387-395) while /etc/frognet/pond_bootstrap_done is absent.
  - frognet_install.sh:2096 disables the timer.
  - fdf-region-join:36 also disables it on a region.
- **Fix**: "frognet-pond-bootstrap.sh: run from the end of each merge while broker enrolment is pending (network events cause merges). On success it starts the tunnel daemon and writes /etc/frognet/pond_bootstrap_done. Not used on an FD&F region."

**C5. Where**: 28, Broker, frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh
- **Text**: "Bootstraps a fresh broker (--bootstrap <group> <name>) or redeems a passcode to join an existing pond — writing the GROUP_TOKEN membership card."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:42-48 accepts only `--pond-password` and exits on anything else.
  - It reads BROKER_URL and POND_NAME/GROUP_NAME from tunnel.conf (79, 177).
  - It registers only when a non-10.x default route exists, POSTing /api/v4/register (201).
  - It writes `GROUP_TOKEN=` empty (268).
- **Fix**: "frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh [--pond-password PW]: registers this node with the broker named in tunnel.conf (POST /api/v4/register: pond, WireGuard pubkey, /24, /etc/fnid GUID), then starts the tunnel daemon. Only a node with a direct, non-10.x upstream registers."

**C6. Where**: 28, Broker, frognet-broker-admin.sh
- **Text**: "frognet-broker-admin.sh passcode new --group <g>"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - frognet-broker-admin.sh:36-55 has no `passcode new`. It has create-group, create-passcode, group-status and similar commands against the v1 API.
  - The v4 broker has no passcode or group-token routes (0 hits in broker/opt/frognet_broker_v4/frognet_broker_v4.py).
  - The v4 admin tool is `frognet-pond` (frognet-pond-v4:26-30; installed as /usr/local/bin/frognet-pond in broker/full_broker.tgz).
- **Fix**: Replace the row with: "frognet-pond create <pond> [--max-users N] [--node-base IP] [--increment N] [--password PW]: on the broker, creates a pond for nodes to register into. Also list, status, members, allocations, remove, bootstrap."

**C7. Where**: 28, Broker, the two tunnel.conf rows
- **Text**: "The broker URL, pond name, choruses to join, and the GROUP_TOKEN redeemed at join."; "Holds the GROUP_TOKEN written when a node redeems a passcode. Not edited by hand."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (the same file is listed twice) and STALE
- **Evidence**:
  - setup_lillypad_v4.bash:745-750 seeds BROKER_URL, PASSCODE=, GROUP_TOKEN=, GROUP_NAME= and MAX_TUNNELS=5000. It sets BROKER_URL and the pond through conf.sh (`[ONE_CONF_V1]`, 772-776).
  - The setup page writes CHORUSES and POND_PASSWORD (frognet_setup_v4_helper.bash:140, 158).
  - Nothing on the v4 path fills GROUP_TOKEN.
- **Fix**: One row: "/etc/frognet/tunnel.conf: the one broker config file. BROKER_URL, POND_NAME, optional POND_PASSWORD, CHORUSES, MAX_TUNNELS. On an FD&F region it may also carry RINA_RAM (the region join writes /opt/fdf/var/join.env and keeps a copy of this file as tunnel.conf.broker)."

**C8. Where**: 28, Discovery, the FrogNet_monitor row
- **Text**: "FrogNet_monitor"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: The command is usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor, all lower case.
- **Fix**: "frognet_monitor"

**C9. Where**: 28, Discovery, frognet_setup_v4_api.php (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "set/clear the broker, register the node, generate a join QR code"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: var/www/html/frognet_setup_v4_api.php:72-166. The actions are state, scan-wifi, auto-ip, apply-identity, connect-wifi, disconnect, broker-state, apply-broker (host, port, pond, pond_password, choruses), clear-broker, reboot, qr, ssid-projection-state and apply-ssid-projection. There is no "register" action.
- **Fix**: "set the node's identity, join Wi-Fi, set or clear the broker (host, port, pond, password, choruses), generate a join QR code, toggle SSID projection, reboot."

**C10. Where**: 28, Broker, broker.db
- **Text**: "a cache the nodes refill, not a system of record. Back it up by copying the file."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - frognet_broker_v4.py:97-107 runs SQLite in WAL mode, so a bare copy can miss the WAL.
  - Ponds are admin-created (frognet-pond create). /register answers 404 "Pond not found" unless the pond row exists (frognet_broker_v4.py:711). Nodes do not recreate ponds.
- **Fix**: "broker.db: the broker's state in one SQLite file (WAL mode). Nodes, tunnels and choruses are rebuilt by nodes checking in. Ponds are not: the operator creates them. Back it up with SQLite's backup, not a bare copy."

**C11. Where**: 28, whole table and closing paragraph (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "The two files you will actually edit are tunnel.conf and ssid_projection.conf"
- **Verdict**: STALE (the table has no FD&F region commands; the brief puts the current deployment at r19-a15)
- **Evidence**:
  - opt/fdf/bin/fdf-region-join:1-12 (`fdf-region-join SITE_KEY [RAM_HOST:PORT] [AREA]`, defaults 165.232.153.144:51888 and area frognet) writes /opt/fdf/var/site.env and join.env, stops the store-era timers, and restarts frognet-tunnel-daemon-v3.
  - fdf-leave reverses the join. fdf-ls lists members.
  - fdf-comms.sh starts comms-ram :8800 and comms-media :8994 and writes the pond's `where` cell.
- **Fix**: Add a "Region (FD&F)" group:
  - "fdf-region-join SITE_KEY [RAM:PORT] [AREA]: joins this FrogNet node to the region (site.env, join.env, tunnel daemon in region mode)."
  - "fdf-leave: leaves the region and turns the broker-mode units back on."
  - "fdf-ls: the region's members."
  - "fdf-comms.sh: runs the Communicator's servers (comms-ram :8800, comms-media :8994) on this node and says where they are in the pond."
  
  Then change the closing sentence to: "Off a region, the two files you edit are tunnel.conf and ssid_projection.conf. On a region, fdf-region-join writes what it needs."

## 29 Appendix D — REST, BLDC-1, and Ribbit

**D1. Where**: 29, Scenario 3 table (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "~40 KB (9× less)"; "~1.1× — marginal"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (the arithmetic does not follow from the stated assumptions)
- **Evidence**: The scenario's own assumptions give 1,000 sensors × 15 B × 3 viewers = 45 KB/min. That is 360/45 = 8× less, and 45/36 = 1.25× the link capacity.
- **Fix**: "~45 KB (8× less)" and "~1.25× — marginal". Or change the diff size to ~13 B so that 40 KB holds.

**D2. Where**: 29, Scenario 4 header
- **Text**: "plus presence, chat, and a game table"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The demonstrated run was a call. John, chat ec30d1cc (07-16): "held both audio and video solidly through about 750 Kbps … down to about 300Kbps … Audio was consistent."
  - The current Communicator has no games. A grep for game or backgammon in ribbit/examples/communicator/src and frognet-android finds nothing.
- **Fix**: "A small-group HD video call, plus presence and chat"

**D3. Where**: 29, Scenario 4 table, Ribbit column
- **Text**: "adaptive: 1 Mbit → ~300 Kbps → a 1-byte heartbeat, and back — no re-dial"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - ribbit/examples/communicator/src/comms/call.cpp:34-37: below rung L5 the upload carries no video. Audio continues. Nothing in the code or the chats supports "1-byte heartbeat".
  - The ~300 Kbps figure is John's own (ec30d1cc).
- **Fix**: "adaptive: 1 Mbit → ~300 Kbps → audio only, and back — no re-dial"

**D4. Where**: 29, Scenario 4 table, Complexity row
- **Text**: "1 interface; call, chat, games and presence as handlers over one space; the media host is an elected role"
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**:
  - There are no games (D2).
  - The Communicator's servers are comms-ram :8800 and comms-media :8994. fdf-comms.sh starts them on a node and writes the pond's `where` cell (opt/fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh:1-12). Members find the servers through that cell; no election picks them.
  - The desktop comms-app is given `--ram` and `--media` (src/app/main.cpp:7, 822).
- **Fix**: "1 shared memory (comms-ram) and 1 media server (comms-media); call, chat and presence are rows in it; a node's own servers are found through the pond's where cell"

**D5. Where**: 29, "What the numbers say"
- **Text**: "so a tightening link degrades it to a heartbeat instead of dropping it"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (same evidence as D3)
- **Fix**: "so a tightening link sheds the picture to keep the voice instead of dropping the call"

## 30 Appendix E — Advanced Simulation, The Disaster Area

**E1. Where**: 30, "What the investigation asserts"
- **Text**: "and every one fails loudly if the real code regresses"

- **Verdict**: STALE (it describes broker mode only)

- **Fix**: Add after the sentence: "This is broker mode, where every node walks and merges. On an FD&F region the walk does not run; the region's records and plan replace it."

## 31 Appendix F — Inside the Compression Engine

**F1. Where**: 31, "Taking port 80" (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "where only the local machinery talks to it"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: frognet_install.sh:1035-1042 writes `Listen 8080` and `Listen 8443` on every address. etc/setup_iptables has no rule for 8080 or 8443; it only REDIRECTs 10/8:80 (lines 77-80) and RETURNs mark-1 traffic.
- **Fix**: "Apache moves to port 8080 (8443 for TLS); port 80 belongs to the proxy. Apache still listens on every address; the local machinery is what uses it."

**F2. Where**: 31, "Deciding whether to compress at all" (the whole section)
- **Text**: "and the default is the fast path everywhere."; "Semantic mode engages when the next hop measures slow, or when policy names it explicitly."
- **Verdict**: REWRITE
- **Evidence**:
  - proxy/proxy_main.py:502-513: "FAST and SEMANTIC both go through daemon with SAME/DIFF caching". `is_fast_path` is used only in a log line (proxy/transport_semantic.py:2415, 2462).
  - proxy_main.py:329-341 (`[TEN_NET_IS_SEMANTIC_V1]`) sends any 10. target that lands on HAM to the semantic transport.
  - proxy/decision.py:280 sets the hop to the destination (`nh = target_ip`), and line 332 calls `_update_rtt_state(nh=nh, dev="")`. `_daemon_rtt_ms` returns None when dev is empty (125-126), so no RTT sample is ever taken.
  - The EWMA (0.25), enter/exit thresholds (250/200 ms) and 30 s hold-down (77-87) are therefore unreachable. The "rtt unknown -> FAST" branch (348-360) always returns first.
  - /etc/frognet/semantic_hosts (proxy/policy.py:50; installed empty, frognet_install.sh:959) only changes the label.
- **Fix** (replacement section):
  > **Every FrogNet request rides the codec.** The proxy sends every request for a remote FrogNet address (10/8) through the daemon with SAME/DIFF: templates, references, REPEAT and SAME. Only a request for the node itself goes straight to the local Apache, and only addresses outside 10/8 go out raw. The proxy still labels each hop FAST or SEMANTIC, and /etc/frognet/semantic_hosts can force the label. The label does not change the codec path. The smoothing, hysteresis and hold-down code exists in proxy/decision.py, but no RTT sample reaches it today (the probe is called without a device), so it never switches a hop.
  
  John's call: either fix decision.py:332 so the RTT path runs, or delete it.

**F3. Where**: 31, "What makes two requests the same request", worked example
- **Text**: "template: /api.php?entity=sensors&action=values"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete identity)
- **Evidence**:
  - proxy/templates.py:205-243 (`[DYNAMIC_KEY_SET_IS_PART_OF_THE_IDENTITY_V1]`) folds the sorted names of the varying keys into the key as `__dyn=`.
  - templates.py:246-276 (`[BODY_FIELD_SET_IS_PART_OF_THE_IDENTITY_V1]`) folds a JSON body's field set in as `__body=<crc32>`.
  - A tuple call's location keys (SensorName on a read) are values (templates.py:152-165; core/tuple_key.py:135-145).
  - The identities are /propogateNotification.php?__dyn=event, /api.php?entity=sensors&action=values&__dyn=SensorName,parse, and /frognet_echo.php.
- **Fix**: Show the three identities as above. Add: "The names of the varying keys, and a JSON body's field set, are part of the identity, because they fix the wire layout. Their values are not."

**F4. Where**: 31, "The first exchange, and why it must not fail"
- **Text**: "That is the FULL of Part IX, and learning it and sending it are the same act."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (it contradicts the appendix's own table and the code)
- **Evidence**: The bootstrap is REQ_RAW 0x03 and RESP_RAW 0x14: plain HTTP, after which both ends learn the template (transport_semantic.py:2443-2470; core/semcache_wire.py:73-80). REQ_FULL 0x01 is the first templated request, sent while the reference is unset (transport_semantic.py:2575-2578). The table further down says the same thing.
- **Fix**: "It goes over raw, once, and both ends learn the shape from it. The next request is the first FULL: every value, in the learned template."

**F5. Where**: 31, "Three states, and a memory with three values"
- **Text**: "reference is EMPTY     sent before, no values, still none"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: core/codec.py:173-183 and transport_semantic.py:2569-2582. An unset reference (None) sends FULL. Identical values, whether the field set is empty or not, send REPEAT, the hash only. Changed values send DIFF. SAME is the reply.
- **Fix**:
  ```
  reference UNSET       never sent to this peer        ->  FULL
  reference SET, equal  nothing changed (or no values) ->  REPEAT
  reference SET, moved  some values changed            ->  DIFF
  ```

**F6. Where**: 31, "Twenty-one bytes"
- **Text**: "A frame is a four-byte marker, a one-byte opcode, and a payload."; "A repeat of an unchanged request for an unchanged resource is twenty-one bytes out and twenty-one bytes back"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (the on-wire count)
- **Evidence**:
  - The FNW1 message is 4 + 1 + 16 = 21 bytes (semcache_wire.py:139-164).
  - `_send_frame` adds a 4-byte length (transport_semantic.py:691-697).
  - The daemon prefixes every reply with a 4-byte seq (daemon/engine/session.py:792).
  - Requests carry no seq; the daemon counts them (session.py:758-760).
- **Fix**: "A message is a four-byte marker, a one-byte opcode and a payload, carried in a frame with a four-byte length. Replies also carry a four-byte sequence tag. A repeat is a 21-byte message each way: 25 bytes on the wire out, 29 back."

**F7. Where**: 31, opcode table
- **Text**: "ERROReithera status and a message"; "SEQ_RESETeitherput the two sequence counters back in step"; "RTT_PING / PONG / LOOPeitherround-trip probes on the same wire"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - RESP_RAW 0x14 (same_id, status, headers, body) is missing from the table (semcache_wire.py:79; proxy handles it at transport_semantic.py:2788).
  - Only the daemon sends ERROR (session.py:844-1350).
  - Neither the proxy nor the daemon sends or handles SEQ_RESET. It appears only in simulation/transport_sim_tier.py, and the daemon answers it with 400 "unknown FNW1 op" (session.py:937).
  - RTT_PING goes to the peer; PONG and LOOP come back from it (semcache_wire.py:84-91).
- **Fix**: Add the row "RAW | from peer | same_id, status, headers, body — the bootstrap reply". Mark ERROR "from peer". Mark RTT_PING "to peer" and PONG/LOOP "from peer". Mark SEQ_RESET "defined, not used by the proxy or daemon".

**F8. Where**: 31, "Asking once", receiving side
- **Text**: "On the receiving side, the collapse is keyed on the request alone — not on who asked."; "Three different nodes asking the same question in the same instant cost the answering node one database execution among them, not three."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The wait is off by default. session.py:163 sets `_COALESCE_WAIT_SEC` from FROGNET_DAEMON_COALESCE_WAIT (default "0.0"). `event.wait(timeout=0.0)` returns at once (896-902), so every concurrent duplicate executes independently.
  - The key is req_hash (881-893). That is sha256 over the destination, the path, the opcode and the sender's reference values (transport_semantic.py:662-668), so two senders coalesce only if their references are identical.
- **Fix**: "On the receiving side, a request whose hash is already being answered can wait for that answer. The hash covers the destination, the template and the sender's reference values, so callers holding the same references share one execution. The wait is FROGNET_DAEMON_COALESCE_WAIT seconds, and it is 0 by default, which turns this off."

**F9. Where**: 31, "Asking once", the two probe passages
- **Text**: "Health probes are excluded, because a probe handed somebody else's result has measured nothing."; "Probes were once exempted from the collapse on the theory that they were cheap."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (the two read as contradicting each other)
- **Evidence**:
  - The daemon excludes only HEALTH_CHECK_HASH (session.py:881-885), and answers it SAME without executing anything (946-950).
  - The 400+ queue incident was echo and discovery probes on the sending side, which now coalesce (transport_semantic.py:40-44).
- **Fix**: "Only the synthetic health check is excluded…" and "Echo and discovery probes were once exempted from the sending-side collapse…"

**F10. Where**: 31, "Telling everyone" (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "a node notifies only its DIRECT neighbours"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete) and STALE
- **Evidence**:
  - usr/local/bin/propogateNotificationInternal: when `python3 -m discovery.neighbors` fails, the node notifies every 10.x.y.1 in /etc/hosts (`source=etc_hosts_fallback`).
  - On an FD&F region it exits without notifying anyone ("region_mode_propagation_is_the_facts_write").
  - fdf-region-join:34-38 stops frognet-merge-watcher.
- **Fix**: Add: "Off a region only. On an FD&F region a change is a facts write, the region tells the members, and no notification is sent." Delete the /etc/hosts path from the code, or say that it is there. It is a fallback.

**F11. Where**: 31, "How SAME is proven", identifier
- **Text**: "The identifier it replies with is derived from the node’s own identity — the GUID in /etc/fnid from Part IV — together with the request and the content of the response."; "No cipher is involved and none is claimed; this is identity and derivation, not cryptography."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (against the code)
- **Evidence**:
  - core/semcache_id.py:43-61: `same_id = HMAC-SHA256(secret, req_hash ‖ sha256(body))[:16]`.
  - The secret is read from /etc/frognet/sem_cache_secret (core/frognet_secret.py:35-63), which the installer mints with `openssl rand -hex 32` (frognet_install.sh:961).
  - The C++ engine uses the same derivation (ribbit/cpp/include/fnwp_server.hpp:106-111).
  - /etc/fnid is not used.
  - Conflict: John ruled on 07-30 (chat 45427cf8): "FrogNet does not implement cryptography. The shared secret is a GUID … It's not a MAC either … It is the fnid." The code does not match that ruling. John decides which one changes. The text below follows the code.
- **Fix**: "The identifier is sixteen bytes of HMAC-SHA256, keyed by the node's semantic-cache secret (/etc/frognet/sem_cache_secret, made at install), over the request hash and a hash of the response body. It names an answer; it does not hide one, and it is not a confidentiality claim. Change the data and the identifier changes with it."

**F12. Where**: 31, "How SAME is proven", the reference invariant (**NEW**)
- **Text**: "And the reference is advanced only after the far side has accepted the exchange."; "If the answering node failed — its database down, its origin returning an error — neither side moves, and the two references stay in step."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The request reference is committed as the frame leaves (transport_semantic.py:2590, "committed as it leaves").
  - A failed RPC, an OP_ERROR or an undecodable reply clears it (2607, 2681, 2686), so the next request is REQ_FULL and both ends restart from a full value set.
  - The daemon does not cache a response with status 400 or above (session.py:1056-1060).
- **Fix**: "The request reference moves as the frame leaves. If the far side cannot decode or answer it, the reference is cleared, and the next request goes FULL, which resets the far side too. An error is never stored as an answer."

**F13. Where**: 31, "When it goes wrong", the "SAME, no copy" row
- **Text**: "drop both references, fetch it whole, prime both caches — one extra round trip, no failure"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with the Ribbit C++ FNWP engine). It is correct for the Python proxy.
- **Evidence**:
  - The Python proxy matches the row: transport_semantic.py:2722-2745 `[SAME_MISS_AUTORETRY_V1]` re-fetches over REQ_RAW, which runs the request at the origin again.
  - The C++ engine instead sends REQ_SAME_MISS 0x22 with the same_id. The host answers RESP_RAW from its cache without running anything, or returns 410 if the answer is gone (ribbit/cpp/include/fnwp_engine.hpp:249-252, 279, 390-392).
- **Fix**: Keep the row for the port-80 implementation and add one sentence: "The Ribbit C++ engine asks the host for the answer the SAME named (REQ_SAME_MISS 0x22). The host serves it from its cache and does not run the request again; if the answer is gone, it says so (410)."

## 32 Appendix G — Inside a Handler

**1. Where**: 32 Appendix G, "A JSON request, from the client's call to the wire"
- **Text**: "The content type is treated as a hint, not an instruction: the body is sniffed and the sniff wins."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `core/format_registry.py` `detect_request_handler`: a Content-Type containing "json" returns JSON_HANDLER and one containing "xml" returns XML_HANDLER at once, with no sniff. Only a declared HTML type is checked against the body (`<html`, `<!doctype html`, or a leading `<`). The sniff (`sniff_body_mode`) runs only when no hint matched. Replies (`detect_reply_handler`) trust `application/json` or a leading `{`/`[`.
- **Fix**: "A declared JSON or XML type is taken as given. A declared HTML type is used only when the body starts like HTML. With no usable declared type, the body is sniffed: JSON, XML, HTML, text, else raw."

**2. Where**: 32 Appendix G, worked example "The same reading, before and after"
- **Text**: "types:  host,       enum,       float"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: The JSON handler has no `enum` type (`core/json_handler.py:_leaf_type`, the type list at `:348`). `SensorType` is forced to `string` by `_force_string_for_path` (`p == "sensortype"`). `SensorName` is forced to `host` by `_force_host_for_path`.
- **Fix**: "types:  host,       string,     float"

---

## 33 Appendix H — The Liveness Gate

**3. Where**: 33 Appendix H, "Three answers, not two" (still wrong from 10-08 #21)
- **Text**: "The probe returns one of three things, and the third is the one worth the appendix."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `discovery/real_backends.py:332-391` `_ping_pong` returns a float rtt, `None`, `"LOOP"`, or `"REFUSED"`. `REFUSED` covers ECONNREFUSED, EHOSTUNREACH and ENETUNREACH (`:371-380`). `discovery/discovery.py:323-331` `_alive_measure` treats float, LOOP and REFUSED as decisive. It re-asks only a `None`, up to `FROGNET_ALIVE_9009_TRIES` (default 3, `:279`).
- **Fix**: "The probe returns one of four things. A number: PONG came back, alive, this is the rtt. Nothing: no answer, asked again up to three times this pass. REFUSED: refused, or no route; something answered no, and that is definitive absence. LOOP: the far daemon says this route comes back to me." Update the code block to four rows.

**4. Where**: 33 Appendix H, "Ping-pong, and nothing else"
- **Text**: "When the pin cannot be applied — an unprivileged process, a simulation — the code says so loudly rather than measuring the wrong thing quietly."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `real_backends.py:343-350`: when SO_BINDTODEVICE fails, the code logs `[PINGPONG] BINDTODEVICE FAILED … probe rides installed route, not the candidate` and goes on to connect and measure over the installed route.
- **Fix**: "When the pin cannot be applied (an unprivileged process, a simulation), the code logs that the probe rides the installed route, not the candidate, and measures that route. It does it loudly, never quietly."

**5. Where**: 33 Appendix H, "Ping-pong, and nothing else"
- **Text**: "Open a connection, say who you are and where you want the answer sent back"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (minor)
- **Evidence**: `real_backends.py:353` sends `wrap_hello(_get_alive_return_ip(dev))`. The HELLO body is only the return IP. `daemon/engine/server.py:194` reads `return_ip = body`.
- **Fix**: "Open a connection, send a greeting that names the address the answer should go back to, send a ping stamped with the current clock, and wait."

**6. Where**: 33 Appendix H, "Whose address goes in the greeting"
- **Text**: "It has to be the address that actually leaves by the interface under test — not the node's identity."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (the code has a fallback the text denies)
- **Evidence**: `real_backends.py:413-458` `_get_alive_return_ip`. When no dev is given, or the dev has no 10.x address, it uses the hostname identity from /etc/hosts (`FrogNetHost`). Failing that it returns `0.0.0.0`. Its docstring says "Fall back to the hostname identity only when no dev is given or the dev has no 10.x". This conflicts with the no-fallbacks ruling.
- **Fix**: State what the code does, and flag the fallback to John: "It is the 10.x address on the interface under test. If that interface has none, the code uses the node's identity from /etc/hosts." Or remove the fallback in code, then keep the sentence.

**7. Where**: 33 Appendix H, "A miss is not an absence"
- **Text**: "The probe is stateless. It remembers nothing between merges and carries no history forward. Every pass asks the question again and believes only this pass's answer."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (within a merge)
- **Evidence**: A REFUSED verdict marks the host in the not-FrogNet negative cache: `discovery.py:419-421` `_nf_mark(ip, "on_segment_client_no_pong")`. Later passes in the same merge skip it (`discovery.py:383-389` `SKIP_NOT_FROGNET … cached_non_frognet_this_session`). The cache is shared across processes through a sentinel file and is flushed at the top of each runMerge (`core/not_frognet.py:19-50`).
- **Fix**: "The probe itself keeps nothing. One thing is remembered, and only until the next merge: a host that refused is marked not-FrogNet and not re-probed in later passes of the same merge. The mark is flushed at the top of every merge. A timeout is never marked."

**8. Where**: 33 Appendix H, "A miss is not an absence"
- **Text**: "A refused connection is different — something answered, and it said no — and that is a fact you may act on."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
- **Evidence**: `real_backends.py:375-380`: EHOSTUNREACH and ENETUNREACH also return `"REFUSED"` (definitive). `not_frognet.py` "Only DEFINITIVE non-FrogNet signals: connection refused, no route / network unreachable".
- **Fix**: "A refused connection, or no route to the host, is different. That is a fact you may act on."

---

## 34 Appendix I — Machine Identity

**9. Where**: 34 Appendix I, opening
- **Text**: "Its encryption keys are replaced whenever a tunnel is rebuilt."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - `usr/local/bin/frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:135-143` generates the node WireGuard keypair only if `/var/lib/frognet-tunnel/node_private.key` is absent. Otherwise it logs "WireGuard keypair exists".
  - `frognet_nuke_tunnels:28-36,101-110` keeps the keys on purpose, because "deleting this makes tunnel-setup mint a NEW keypair".
  - The keys are removed only by `frognet_reset.sh` without `--keep-identity` (`installer/frognet_reset.sh:172-177`).
- **Fix**: "Its WireGuard key is kept across tunnel rebuilds, but it is replaced whenever the key files are removed, as a full reset does."

**10. Where**: 34 Appendix I, "One file, written once"
- **Text**: "so that resetting a node's configuration, rebuilding its tunnels, renaming it or re-addressing it all leave the identity untouched."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (reset)
- **Evidence**: `installer/frognet_reset.sh:40-43,172-177`: by default, phase "4. Node identity" runs `nuke /etc/fnid` and `nuke /etc/wireguard`. Only `--keep-identity` keeps them. `frognet_install.sh:649` and `frognet-node-guid.sh` place `/etc/fnid` outside `/etc/frognet` so that a reinstall wiping `/etc/frognet` keeps it.
- **Fix**: "…so that reinstalling over /etc/frognet, rebuilding its tunnels, renaming it or re-addressing it all leave the identity untouched. A full reset (frognet_reset.sh) removes it unless given --keep-identity."

**11. Where**: 34 Appendix I, "One file, written once"
- **Text**: "There is exactly one path that creates it, and it runs at install."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - `frognet-node-guid.sh --ensure` is called by the installer (`frognet_install.sh:654`).
  - It is also called by `frognet-make-gateway.sh:116-127` when `/etc/fnid` is missing ("no /etc/fnid - creating node GUID").
  - `frognet-node-guid.sh regenerate` writes a new one.
  - Readers (`frognet-node-guid.sh` with no argument or `--read`, and `frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:195`) refuse.
- **Fix**: "One command creates it: frognet-node-guid.sh --ensure. It runs at install, and frognet-make-gateway.sh runs it if the file is missing. A deliberate rotation is frognet-node-guid.sh regenerate. Everything else reads, and a reader that finds the file missing refuses."

**12. Where**: 34 Appendix I, "How the broker uses it"
- **Text**: "A new machine arriving under a name that is already taken is refused, and it should be."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (how it is refused)
- **Evidence**: `frognet_broker_v4.py:1945-1960`: an unknown GUID makes a plain `INSERT INTO nodes`. The partial unique index `idx_nodes_pond_name_active ON nodes(pond_id, name) WHERE active=1` (`:362-364`) makes it raise `sqlite3.IntegrityError`. The register handler does not catch it, so the node gets HTTP 500, not a named refusal.
- **Fix**: "…is refused: the broker's unique (pond, name) index rejects the row, and today the node sees a server error rather than a message naming the conflict." (Or have the code return a 409 that names the name and the GUID, then keep the original sentence.)

**13. Where**: 34 Appendix I, "Retiring an identity" (still wrong from 10-08 #22)
- **Text**: "the record blocking its name is keyed by an identifier that no longer exists anywhere — and the one clean way to clear it is out of reach."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - `DELETE /api/v4/admin/nodes/{pubkey}` (`frognet_broker_v4.py:2777-2810`) destroys the node's tunnels, sets `active=0` and frees its IP allocation, which frees the (pond, name) index.
  - It ships as `frognet-pond forget <pubkey>` (`full_broker.tgz` `payload/usr/local/bin/frognet-pond:27,170`).
  - `frognet-node-guid.sh regenerate` with no `/etc/fnid` skips the broker retire ("no broker retire … rotating locally").
- **Fix**: "…keyed by an identifier that no longer exists anywhere, so the GUID retire (POST /api/v4/retire-guid) cannot reach it. The operator clears it by key on the broker: frognet-pond forget <pubkey>. Keep a copy of /etc/fnid."

The appendix claims the FrogNet-only path: two nodes and a broker, no FD&F. Steps 3–6 describe the retired broker CLI and the retired Python Communicator. Items 14–20 are corrections. Item 21 gives replacement text for Steps 3–6.

**21. Where**: 35 Appendix J, Steps 3, 4, 5 (and the Step 6 ladder caption)
- **Text**: "it asks for: the public name, the port, the first pond and its"; "BROKER GATE: PASS 10/10"; "--bootstrap hop-2026-spring hometown"; "frognet-broker-admin.sh passcode new --group hometown"; "A GROUP_TOKEN written into /etc/frognet/tunnel.conf"; "The frognet-pond-bootstrap timer retries the broker"; "frognet-pond members hometown"; "sudo ./install_mediahost.sh"; "fnav.py --list"; "frognet-communicator --name Gorp --cam 0"

Everything is a flag"; its flags are `--port --webroot --no-console --no-packages --dry-run`, and the port defaults to 18257 (`:246`). Its postflight (`:548-580`) prints `service active`, `listening`, `api GET /api/v4/ponds -> 200`, `data N pond(s) visible`, `installed: <BUNDLE_ID>`. There is no BROKER GATE and no URL banner. It creates no pond.
  - **Ponds.** `frognet-pond` has `ping list status create nodes remove forget block unblock db result`, and no `members` (`payload/usr/local/bin/frognet-pond:15-36`). `create` makes the `entire_pond` chorus (`frognet_broker_v4.py:1735-1741`).
  - **Registration.** `frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:42-48` accepts only `--pond-password`; it takes no URL or passcode. The CLI path is `frognet-make-gateway.sh --host --port --scheme --pond --password --chorus` (`:37-52`), which writes tunnel.conf, ensures `/etc/fnid`, and runs `frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh` (POST /api/v4/register). Registration needs a real non-10 uplink.
  - **Enrolment retry.** The pond-bootstrap timer is disabled by the installer (`frognet_install.sh:2091-2096`); enrolment is retried from runMerge (`runMerge.bash:387-395`).
  - **Old Communicator.** `install_mediahost.sh:55-59` needs `frognet_communicator.py`, which is absent from the tree, so it exits. `frognet-mediahost.service:33` runs `ribbit/examples/communicator/fnav.py`, which does not exist (fnav.py is at `python/frogcomms/fnav.py`, a library).
  - **Current Communicator.** `comms-ram --listen`, `comms-media --listen --ram`, `comms-app --ram --media --name [--camera DEV]` (`TESTING.md` §2; `src/app/main.cpp:7-9,822-834`).
  - **Device list.** `comms-app --list-devices` enumerates FFmpeg's v4l2/dshow sources and prints each as `--camera "<name>"`. It does not open a camera or test for a frame (`src/comms/devices.cpp:160-185`).
  - **fdf-comms.sh** is the FD&F path. It needs `/opt/fdf/var/join.env` RINA_RAM, otherwise "it is not in a pond" and exit (`fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh`).
- **Fix** — replacement text for Steps 3–5, grounded in the files above (marked REPLACEMENT):

> REPLACEMENT — Step 3 — The broker
> On the third machine, from the extracted broker bundle:
> ```
> sudo ./install_frognet_broker.sh            # --port 18257 is the default
> ```
> It does not ask questions; everything is a flag. What you should see is the postflight:
> ```
> service       active, <pid> pid
> listening     0.0.0.0:18257
> api           GET /api/v4/ponds -> 200
> data          0 pond(s) visible; database preserved at /var/lib/frognet_broker_v4/broker.db
> installed: frognet_broker_<stamp>
> ```
> Then make the pond the nodes will join:
> ```
> frognet-pond create hometown [--password PW]   # on the broker
> frognet-pond list                              # hometown, with its entire_pond chorus
> ```
> The broker URL you carry to the nodes is http://<broker host>:18257.
>
> REPLACEMENT — Step 4 — A node across the internet
> On each node (it needs a real non-10 uplink: a WAN lease, or Wi-Fi client upstream):
> ```
> sudo frognet-make-gateway.sh --host your.broker.example --port 18257 --pond hometown [--password PW]
> ```
> It writes BROKER_URL and POND_NAME into /etc/frognet/tunnel.conf, checks /etc/fnid, and registers (POST /api/v4/register). A node whose uplink is not up yet stays LAN-only; enrolment is retried from runMerge on the next network event.
> ```
> frognet-pond nodes hometown   # on the broker: both, registered
> frognet_monitor               # on either node: the other one, with RTT
> ping 10.80.90.1               # from HomeBase, across the internet
> ```
> If it does not work: check frognet-pond nodes first. A node missing there did not register; its tunnel-setup log names why.
>
> REPLACEMENT — Step 5 — The call
> Build the Communicator on each machine from ribbit/examples/communicator:
> ```
> ./build.sh            # installs the FFmpeg, libvpx, Opus, PortAudio and Qt 6 it needs
> ```
> On the machine that will hold the call's memory and media server (open TCP 8800 and 8994 to the others):
> ```
> build/comms-ram   --listen 0.0.0.0:8800
> build/comms-media --listen 0.0.0.0:8994 --ram 127.0.0.1:8800
> ```
> Find your devices:
> ```
> build/comms-app --list-devices      # cameras, each as --camera "<name>"; audio as --mic N / --speaker N
> ```
> On each machine a person uses (RAM and MEDIA are that machine's 10.x address):
> ```
> build/comms-app --ram RAM:8800 --media MEDIA:8994 --name Gorp --camera /dev/video0
> ```
> With no camera, comms-app sends a synthetic picture (--synthetic); --no-audio runs without sound.
> (On an FD&F pond node, /opt/fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh runs the same two servers and writes the pond's where cell, so the phone's Communicator finds them with nothing typed.)

  Step 6 ladder: label it "recorded on the earlier Python Communicator" or replace it with the current C++ walk. In `TESTING.md` §3 and `STATUS.md` [BOTTOM_RUNG_SHRINKS_V1], at 128 kb/s the sender walks L7 1280x720 → … → 640x360 → 320x240 → 160x120, and it goes audio only only after 160x120 is refused.

## 36 Appendix K — Known Limits

The arithmetic matches the code:
- `frognet_broker_v4.py:729-746`: 31 tunnels per block, third octet 200 + 4·pond_index + block (up to 253), two /30s per tunnel (8 addresses).
- 54 × 31 = 1,674 → 58 nodes; 4 × 31 = 124 → 16 nodes.
- `TUNNEL_ALLOWED_IPS = "10.0.0.0/8"` (`:74`); two wg ports per tunnel (`host_wg_port`, `join_wg_port`).
- `MAX_TUNNELS` = 4 in `frognet-tunnel-daemon.py:113` and `frognet-create-passcode.sh:37`, and 5000 in `setup_lillypad_v4.bash:750`; the v3 daemon and the broker never read it.
- 256 chorus /24s (`:554-568`).

**23. Where**: 36 Appendix K, "What is not a FrogNet limit" table, row "Discovery depth"
- **Text**: "children and grandchildren. Deeper tiers are not walked"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `discovery/descend.py:475-490,509-527` ([DESCEND_FRONTIER_FROM_KNOWN_V1]). Each pass crawls two levels (`max_levels=2`), but the frontier is seeded from every installed /24 route. Each merge therefore advances two hops deeper, and runAgain keeps merging until a pass installs no new /24.
- **Fix**: "two levels per pass, from every node already routed; depth comes from re-merging, so a chain of any length is walked to its end."

**24. Where**: 36 Appendix K, table row "Open files per process"
- **Text**: "unlimited. Set it in the unit files and it stops being a number anyone thinks about"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (not done in the shipped units)
- **Evidence**: No unit in `TREE/etc/systemd/system` and no broker unit sets `LimitNOFILE` (grep: 0 hits). The systemd default applies. John's policy (chat 99aebaff summary) is `LimitNOFILE=infinity`.
- **Fix**: Add `LimitNOFILE=infinity` to the units, or write "the operator's. The shipped units do not set it yet; set LimitNOFILE=infinity in them."

**25. Where**: 36 Appendix K, opening and "The honest summary"
- **Text**: "a replacement that removes the limit is in development"; "and it is being worked now."
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED (as of 10-11)
- **Evidence**: The allocator in the 10-11 tree is unchanged (`_compute_tunnel_ips`, `frognet_broker_v4.py:729`). No per-node allocator or single-interface-per-pond code exists in `broker/` or `internet_tunnels_v3/`. The latest statement of the plan is chat 99aebaff (08-01). No later chat records work on it.
- **Fix**: "a replacement that removes the limit is designed and not yet built", unless John says otherwise.

## 39 Appendix M — Glossary

**29. Where**: 39 Glossary, "Monitor" (still from 10-08 #10)
- **Text**: "FrogNet_monitor — the terminal program every node carries."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor`.
- **Fix**: "frognet_monitor — the terminal program…"

**30. Where**: 39 Glossary, "broker" (still from 10-08 #36)
- **Text**: "holds no authoritative state (its picture is rebuilt from node polls)"; "It holds no node identity and no pond secret, runs none of the semantic stack"; "itself a FrogNet semantic server"
- **Verdict**: WRONG / INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**:
  - The broker keeps a SQLite DB (`/var/lib/frognet_broker_v4/broker.db`) of ponds, nodes (with `guid`, the sole identity key, `frognet_broker_v4.py:1866-1887`), tunnels, port and IP allocations, and pond `password_hash`. Tunnel allocation is authoritative there; a wiped DB does not rebuild from polls (10-08 #11).
  - The console is `pond_admin.html` + `frognet_broker_admin_v4.php` + `frognet_gate_lib.php` (bundle `payload/www/`), PHP, not a FrogNet semantic server.
- **Fix**: "It holds the pond's registry: each node's install GUID (its matching key), public key and subnet, the tunnels and their address and port allocations, and any pond password hash. It holds no private keys, runs none of the semantic stack, and is not a member of any FrogNet. The optional console is a PHP page in the broker host's web root, with frognet-pond as its command-line equivalent."

**31. Where**: 39 Glossary, "capability probe" (10-08 #4)
- **Text**: "each service folds in its own checks before publishing the result as a tuple"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `core/unrest_handler.py` `advertise()` and `core/role_publish.py` publish the base blob unchanged. The only per-role addition is `av_port`, stamped by `frognet_register_candidate.sh`.
- **Fix**: "…as a JSON blob; each role's advertiser publishes it as its <role>/capability tuple, which the election scores (Appendix A)."

**32. Where**: 39 Glossary, "daemon" (still from 10-08 #37)
- **Text**: "is reached over a loopback socket"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: The daemon listens on `FROGNET_DAEMON_PORT` 9009 on 0.0.0.0. Peer proxies and the liveness probe connect to it across the network (`real_backends.py:304-305,352`; `measure_or_loop` docstring "the daemon binds 0.0.0.0").
- **Fix**: "…reached by peer proxies, and by the liveness probe, on TCP 9009."

**33. Where**: 39 Glossary, "databasehost_control" (10-08 Report C #2)
- **Text**: "used only for the mesh’s internal discovery and election bookkeeping … Holds no application data; not something you address or configure."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `core/frognet_tuples.py:336-338` `DEFAULT_DBHOST = "databasehost_control.frognet"`: "capability, presence, beacons and the Services table resolve here". `frognet_tuples.put` writes there by default.
- **Fix**: "The deterministic control host (highest .1). It holds the coordination plane: the SD: tuples (capability, presence, beacons) and the Services table. It holds no durable sensor data; that stays on databasehost.frognet."

**34. Where**: 39 Glossary, "echo" (still from 10-08 #38)
- **Text**: "fully-qualified name and the node's wired and wireless addresses"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: As in 10-08 #5: field 2 is always the node's .1; fields 3–4 are upstream addresses or 0.0.0.0.
- **Fix**: "the node name, its .1 identity, and up to two upstream addresses (0.0.0.0 when absent)."

**35. Where**: 39 Glossary, "GROUP_TOKEN" (still from 10-08 #39)
- **Text**: "A node's pond-membership credential, written to /etc/frognet/tunnel.conf when it redeems a passcode from the broker."
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**:
  - The v4 broker has no passcode or group-token routes.
  - `frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh` sends pond, pubkey, subnet and GUID.
  - `internet_tunnels_v3/install/install_client.sh:164` lists GROUP_TOKEN among the `v2_keys`.
- **Fix**: Delete the entry, or write "v2-era tunnel.conf field; unused by the v4 broker."

**36. Where**: 39 Glossary, "media host"
- **Text**: "The elected node that fans a live call’s frames out to every viewer, chosen by measured real-time video-encoding capability (a working ffmpeg + libvpx encoder is required). Role name mediahost."
- **Verdict**: STALE
- **Evidence**:
  - The Communicator's media server is `comms-media` (:8994). It runs where it is started: by hand (`TESTING.md` §2), or on an FD&F pond node by `fdf-comms.sh`, found through the pond's where cell. That is the settled ruling and `fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh`.
  - The elected `mediahost` role still exists (`core/sotf_handler.py:331-380`: LAN-only, ffmpeg+libvpx gate, CPU bench, hw encoder).
  - Its service runs a file that does not exist: `frognet-mediahost.service:33` `…/ribbit/examples/communicator/fnav.py --serve 0.0.0.0:9000`.
- **Fix**: "media server — comms-media (TCP 8994): it takes each participant's audio and video upload and serves each viewer at that viewer's own rate, passing through or transcoding down. It runs where it is started; on an FD&F pond node, fdf-comms.sh starts it and says where it is in the pond's where cell. (FrogNet's elected mediahost role, LAN-only and gated on ffmpeg+libvpx, still exists in core/sotf_handler.py; the service it starts points at a file no longer in the tree.)"

**37. Where**: 39 Glossary, "SSID projection" (still from 10-08 #40)
- **Text**: "when it has no wired uplink"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: `usr/local/bin/frognet-netstart` header: "If eth0 has no link: wlan0 gets the identity address … hostapd starts in AP mode". eth0 is downstream.
- **Fix**: "when its eth0 has no link."

**38. Where**: 39 Glossary, "FD&F" (still from 10-08 #41)
- **Text**: "(Part XVII)"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**: FD&F is Specification C (file 24); the book has no Part XVII.
- **Fix**: "(Specification C)"

**39. Where**: 39 Glossary, "pond" and the missing FD&F terms
- **Text**: "pond  —  A whole FrogNet network — the set of nodes that belong together, as tracked by the broker."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with Specification C and the 10-11 ruling)
- **Evidence**:
  - Under FD&F a pond is held in the region: the pond's where cell (`fdf-comms.sh`, `fdf-where`) and `docs/PLAN-PONDS.md` and `WORKLOG-PONDS.md`.
  - The glossary has no entry for RAM manager, hub, region (FD&F), where cell, bridge / fdf-tun, comms-ram, or comms-media. All of them are named in the ruling and in `MANIFEST-2026-10-11.md`.
- **Fix**:
  - pond: "Under FrogNet, the nodes that register together at a broker. Under FD&F, the members of one pond in a region, held in the region's memory."
  - Add:
    - "RAM manager — the FD&F memory every machine opens one outbound socket to; all data goes through it, never member to member."
    - "hub — the FD&F region's forwarding point on the droplet (StreamingFrog, 165.232.153.144:51888)."
    - "bridge (fdf-tun) — a phone or PC that joins as one TUN with 1:1 aliases in 100.64/10 and leaves via the hub."
    - "where cell — the pond tuple (pond, where, w:<guid>) that says where a node's services are."
    - "comms-ram — the Communicator's memory, TCP 8800."

**40. Where**: 39 Glossary, "scope"
- **Text**: "FrogNet's nesting levels of reach: Self, Lillypad, Chorus, Pond."
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED (in code)
- **Evidence**: `core/frognet_tuples.py:405-432`: the tuple scopes in code are `host:<ip>:<pid>`, `host:<ip>`, `host:<ip>:<role>` and `session:<id>`. No Self/Lillypad/Chorus/Pond scope exists in core, proxy or discovery (grep `lillypad`: 0 hits in core/proxy).
- **Fix**: Keep it as book vocabulary, but say so: "the book's names for reach. In code, a tuple's scope is host:<ip>[:pid|:role] or session:<id>, and chorus and pond are broker groupings."

# P1 — front matter, Part 0, Specifications A and B, Parts I to V, checked against /home/claude/tree1011 (2026-10-11)

Conversation tools were available and were used for history: a16e4802, a46794d6, 5bf4812e, 22c2753a, 38223476 and 90594a78.
