**Where**: 01_Part_0_The_Architecture_Contract.md, 0.5 FD&F: Farinacci, Day & Fawcett
**Text**: "the Map-Resolver's address is derived from the name (SHA-256 into a reserved prefix), never configured or looked up in DNS"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: opt/fdf/src/fdf-source/rinab/src/rina_region_client.hpp:59-62. `map_resolvers()` returns `RINA_MAP_RESOLVER` when that variable is set, and derives the address (198.18.0.0/15 from SHA-256, line 45) only when it is not. rina_node.cpp:228 (`RINA_AREA, RINA_MAP_RESOLVER`) and the rina-host comment at rina_region.cpp:998 also name a configured RINA_MAP_RESOLVER. No DNS lookup exists in the code, even though the comment at rina_region.cpp:1008 still says "or the area's SRV record".
**Fix**: "the Map-Resolver's address is derived from the name (SHA-256 into 198.18.0.0/15) unless RINA_MAP_RESOLVER names one; it is never looked up in DNS"

---

**Where**: 01_Part_0_The_Architecture_Contract.md, 0.6 What Is Demonstrated, and What Is Not
**Text**: "and encryption (the only cryptography is an HMAC on the mapping registration)"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: Section 0.5 of the same Part says FrogNet's 10/8 traffic on a region "rides WireGuard hub-and-spoke through the hub" (opt/fdf/bin/fdf-hub:70-86 `wg set ... listen-port`). The phone and Windows bridges run wireguard-go (opt/fdf/src/frognet-android/tun/engine.go:39-41, 375). The status of 2026-10-10 (5bf4812e: "Every connection to the host is plain TCP; the registration HMAC is the only cryptography") predates the hub. It is true only of the FNWP sessions to the RAM manager.
**Fix**: "and encryption of the sessions to the RAM manager (FNWP to the RAM manager is plain TCP; FD&F's own cryptography is the HMAC on mapping registrations; FrogNet traffic through the hub and the bridges rides WireGuard)"

---

**Where**: 04_Part_I_FrogNet_What_the_Sovereign_Transport_Does.md, 1 / "And it is invisible"
**Text**: "On a FrogNet node this holds."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: opt/fdf/bin/fdf-comms.sh:22,26,32. A node that hosts the Communicator runs `comms-ram --listen 0.0.0.0:8800` and `comms-media --listen 0.0.0.0:8994`. Part 0 0.3 and Part I 2 both say these servers run on your own node. Every node also serves its web stack on :80 (etc/setup_iptables:77-80) and its daemon listens on 0.0.0.0:9009 (opt/frognet_semantic/daemon/daemon_main.py:59). The FD&F exception in the next sentence applies for the same reason: a memory server is a listener.
**Fix**: "On a FrogNet node the listeners are the node's own machinery (its web server on :80, its daemon on :9009) and, on a node that hosts the Communicator, its memory and media servers (comms-ram :8800, comms-media :8994). An FD&F region is the same by design: its RAM manager is a reachable listener (the droplet's rina-host on TCP 51888), because every participant connects up to it."

---

**Where**: 08_Part_V_Discovery.md, 13 We Eat Our Own Cooking
**Text**: "The probes that measure paths — reflect, the :9009 ping-pong, the round-trip measure — run the same either way: the region says who exists, the probes say what is reachable."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: opt/frognet_semantic/discovery/orchestrate.py:66-70 ([NO_CRAWL_IN_REGION_V1]: "The walk, its probes and its promote/reap do not run"). live.py:661-675 reads `/var/lib/frognet/region_hosts` ("FD&F's plan; no walk"). internet_tunnels_v3/graph_apply.py:5 and 193-203 ([ALIVE_IS_THE_SESSION_V1]) admit every planned route with no echo probe, and hold hub routes only until the hub link's WireGuard handshake. The comment at live.py:500 ("Probes ... stay exactly as they are") is stale.
**Fix**: "On a node joined to an FD&F region the walk does not run. The region holds every member's facts, and fdf_graph turns them into a plan: who exists, which members share a LAN, and which go through the hub. The tunnel daemon installs that plan on each region event. It admits every planned route without a probe, and holds a hub route only until the hub link's WireGuard handshake. The merge then names only subnets that have an installed route. Reflect, the :9009 ping-pong and the round-trip measure belong to the walk, off a region."

---

**Where**: 08_Part_V_Discovery.md, 13, WORKED EXAMPLE — What getHosts hands back
**Text**: "{ "ip": "10.80.80.1", "name": "HomeBase", "echo": "..." },"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: var/www/html/getHosts.php:99-107 ([DEVICE_ROW_V1]). Every entry now carries `role` and `alive` as well: role is `''` and alive is `false` off a region, and on a region both come from region_hosts.json. On a region the list is the daemon's file, not makeHostJson.bash (lines 40-50).
**Fix**: Change each row to the form `{ "ip": "10.80.80.1", "name": "HomeBase", "echo": "...", "role": "", "alive": false }`, and add after the example: "On a region node the list comes from the region (role and alive filled in; a phone or PC reads role leaf), not from this node's walk."

---

**Where**: 06_Part_III_Hooking_Up_the_Network.md, 6 / Resolving names, and the default route
**Text**: "Discovery owns /etc/hosts. On every merge it writes the whole file from the walk's result"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: opt/fdf/src/fdf-source/docs/MERGE-r19-a15.md (LEAVES_NAMED_BY_EVERY_WRITER_V1, found on Seattle5 by John, 2026-10-11): "Two writers own /etc/hosts on a region node: the daemon's election (each region event...) and runMerge". discovery/elect.py `leaf_lines` and live.py name the leaves. On a region no walk runs (orchestrate.py:66-70), and routes come from graph_apply, not "the RTT-ranked winners and fallbacks from the walk".
**Fix**: "Discovery owns /etc/hosts. On every merge it writes the whole file: localhost, the FrogNetHost/FrogNetAdmin line for each node, and the .frognet role names. Off a region these come from the walk; on a region node they come from the region's facts. There the tunnel daemon's election also rewrites the file on each region event from the same facts, and both writers name the devices (phones, PCs). The write is idempotent and safe to repeat. Do not keep hand entries there."

---

Checked: 131 claims; 6 problems.

---

# P2 — Parts VI, VII, VII½, VIII, IX (files 09–13) against the 2026-10-11 tree

Conversation tools were available. I used them for the 44x measurement (chat 22881970, 2026-03). Everything else was checked against code.
The broker code: the loose `TREE/opt/frognet_semantic/broker/opt/frognet_broker_v4/frognet_broker_v4.py` is older than the shipped bundle. It lacks [DBADMIN_V1], so it has no /api/v4/admin/db/* routes. The bundle `broker/full_broker.tgz` (frognet_broker_20260813, with install_frognet_broker.sh, frognet-pond and broker v4) matches the book's installer and db commands, so I checked broker claims against the bundle. Below, BUNDLE = that extracted bundle.

---

**1. Where**: 09_Part_VI §16, "Reading and writing it — data on the URL"
**Text**: "That reader is real. frognet_monitor, the network’s info center, is a terminal dashboard that does exactly this"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with code and with Part XIII½ §42c "The two calls")
**Evidence**: The monitor never calls `entity=sensors&action=values`. It makes two calls: `entity=sensors&action=list&SensorName=…` for the id, then `entity=sensor_data&action=get&SensorID=…` (usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor_py/sensors.py:93-109). It also probes every node directly at `http://<ip>/frognet_echo.php` (probes.py:46), so it is not only a reader of the store.
**Fix**: "That reader is real. frognet_monitor, the network’s info center, is a terminal dashboard. It lists every node and shows each one's live telemetry: echo, round-trip time, cache hit rate and bytes saved. You can drill into a node to see its sensors and open a single reading in full. It reads by name in two calls, sensors/list for the id and then sensor_data/get for the payload, sent to databasehost.frognet through the proxy. It writes nothing to the store. It probes each node's frognet_echo.php itself."

**2. Where**: 09_Part_VI §16, tuple paragraph
**Text**: "A name that is its key — a sensor's full name, like SD:barn/temp; a type that says which service or kind it belongs to; and a value"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: The unique key is three columns, SensorName + SensorType + SensorAddress. All three always go into the INSERT so that ON DUPLICATE KEY can fire (var/www/html/api.php:538-547, [TUPLE_ADDRESS_V1]). The name alone is not the key.
**Fix**: "A tuple is addressed by three coordinates: a name (a sensor's full name, like SD:barn/temp), a type that says which service or kind it belongs to, and the address of the writer. Its value is a small blob of JSON."

**3. Where**: 09_Part_VI §16, worked example "A barn sensor writes"
**Text**: "{ SensorName:"SD:barn/temp", SensorType:"sensors",
          value:54.6, ts:1719... }"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: upsert_by_name reads the payload from `jsonData` (or `data_str`). Top-level `value` and `ts` are ignored and the stored jsonData becomes `{}` (api.php:502-513).
**Fix**: "{ SensorName:"SD:barn/temp", SensorType:"sensors",
          jsonData:{ value:54.6, ts:1719... } }"

**4. Where**: 09_Part_VI §17, "Absent is an answer"
**Text**: "A live .1 that has not published is still a candidate; its capability is unknown, so it ranks at the floor, and the highest .1 breaks the tie."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: An unpublished live .1 enters the pool as `{"lan_ip": ip, "_unpublished": True}` (core/frognet_service_hosts.py:226-235). DatabaseRoleHandler.score returns -1.0 when mysql_running/mysql is absent (core/database_handler.py:84-88), and evaluate drops any score below 0 (:196). So it never ranks against published candidates. It can hold the name only when no candidate qualifies: then the name falls to the highest live .1 (frognet_service_hosts.py:291-310).
**Fix**: "The election does not wait. It runs on every converged merge. A live .1 that has not published is still in the pool, but its capability is unknown, so each role's own gate decides. The database gate (mysql running) refuses it. If no candidate qualifies, the name falls to the highest live .1. Every node reads the same store and the same converged hosts block, so every node reaches the same answer from the same absence."

**5. Where**: 09_Part_VI §18, "Split, Merge, and Why It Is Safe"
**Text**: "What FrogNet runs is a per-process watcher on the name: when databasehost.frognet resolves somewhere new, it calls each module’s hostReset() (core/frognet_host_reset.py)."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: The only running HostResetWatcher is started by the frognet-daemon (daemon/daemon_main.py:86, core/role_publish.py:114-144). It reads databasehost_control.frognet, not databasehost.frognet, from /etc/hosts every 15 s (role_publish.py:100-112). Its only module is RolePublishReconcile, whose hostReset() re-publishes capability (:88-97). No application module is registered. In region mode publish_all returns at once (role_publish.py:63-65). The other HostResetWatcher user is a simulator check (discovery/sim/host_reset_check.py).
**Fix**: "What FrogNet runs is one watcher in the frognet-daemon. Every 15 s it reads databasehost_control.frognet from /etc/hosts. When that changes, it re-publishes this node's capability (core/role_publish.py, core/frognet_host_reset.py). In broker mode only. No application module is registered with it. An application detects the change itself, by write-then-read or by the store's generation counter. On a region the daemon writes the new databasehost into /var/lib/frognet/view.json within a second of the change."

**6. Where**: 10_Part_VII §19 "The Broker" (also §20 last section)
**Text**: "A pond whose broker goes dark loses only the ability to onboard a stranger or open a new cross-internet tunnel; everything already formed keeps running."
(and in §20: "A broker going dark costs you the ability to introduce a stranger or mint a new tunnel; everything already formed keeps running.")
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with the same Part: "Both tunnels terminate at the broker, which forwards between them"; §21 "every node peers with the broker")
**Evidence**: Every cross-site tunnel's peer is the broker's own WireGuard interface inside the pond namespace (BUNDLE broker_namespace_v4.py create_wg_interface :320, add_wg_peer :399, add_route :434). If the broker machine is down, no cross-site packet has anywhere to go. Only a stopped broker *service* (uvicorn) leaves the kernel forwarding in place.
**Fix**: §19: "If the broker service stops, tunnels already formed keep forwarding, because the forwarding is in the kernel; what is lost is onboarding and new tunnels. If the broker machine goes down, every cross-site tunnel goes with it, because they all terminate there. Each LAN keeps running on its own." §20: "If the broker service stops, you lose introductions and new tunnels and the formed tunnels keep forwarding. If the broker machine goes down, the cross-site links go with it, and every LAN keeps running on its own."

**7. Where**: 10_Part_VII §19 (also §20 "Clearing and resetting")
**Text**: "Nodes, tunnels and choruses are rebuilt by nodes checking in."
(and in §20: "nodes, tunnels and choruses are rebuilt by nodes checking in, and the ponds are not.")
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: On register a node auto-joins only entire_pond (BUNDLE frognet_broker_v4.py:1956-1966). Nothing in the node tree joins a named chorus on poll. CHORUSES in tunnel.conf is read by no daemon code (internet_tunnels_v3 has no reader; the only /api/v4/choruses/join caller is frognet_chorus.php:132). db clear-nodes deletes chorus_members, and db reset drops the choruses table (:3560-3620).
**Fix**: "Nodes and tunnels are rebuilt by nodes checking in, and each node rejoins entire_pond. Named choruses and their memberships are not: after a reset the operator recreates the ponds and choruses, and members rejoin through the chorus API."

**8. Where**: 10_Part_VII §20, "Running it day to day"
**Text**: "frognet-pond remove              remove a pond"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: `frognet-pond remove <pond> <chorus> <node>` calls DELETE /api/v4/chorus-member, which drops a node from a chorus (BUNDLE usr/local/bin/frognet-pond:26,164-168). The broker has no pond-delete route.
**Fix**: "frognet-pond remove <pond> <chorus> <node>   drop a node from a chorus"

**9. Where**: 10_Part_VII §21 "The Tunnel"
**Text**: "The broker programs iptables and netfilter moves the packets"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: Packets cross between tunnel interfaces by routes the broker installs inside the pond's network namespace (BUNDLE broker_namespace_v4.py add_route :434, create_wg_interface :320). iptables is used for the UDP port DNAT into the namespace and for masquerade (:56, :173-195).
**Fix**: "The broker installs routes between the tunnel interfaces inside the pond’s network namespace, plus iptables port forwards into it, and the kernel moves the packets"

**10. Where**: 10_Part_VII §21, end of "The tunnel daemon owns the lifecycle"
**Text**: "applied by the tunnel daemon, which admits LAN candidates with one probe each and gates hub routes on the WireGuard handshake."
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: [ALIVE_IS_THE_SESSION_V1] r18 removed the echo probe. "No echo probe (r5-r17 sent one per newly admitted route; RAM already says it)", and every planned route is admitted (internet_tunnels_v3/graph_apply.py:4-6, 193-203). Hub routes are still gated on the handshake (:6, :107-108).
**Fix**: "applied by the tunnel daemon. It installs a planned LAN route at once, because the member's live session and a lease pair written by both ends already say the link is there. It gates hub routes on the WireGuard handshake."

**11. Where**: 10_Part_VII §22 "One Overlay, Many Sites"
**Text**: "Routes to the forwarder's own /24 go straight out the tunnel; routes to subnets behind it go out the same tunnel but via the forwarder's .1."
(and the worked example line "10.133.145.0/24  via 10.133.144.1   (SEA-2, relayed)")
**Verdict**: WRONG (also INCONSISTENT with §21 "dest dev wgN — … with no gateway at all")
**Evidence**: [TUNNEL_DEV_ROUTE_V1]: tunnel routes are always `dest dev wgN` with no via, for the channel's own /24 and for every relayed /24. "This collapses the prior own-/24 vs relay-/24 distinction" (frognet_route/planner.py:278-314; internet_tunnels_v3/poll.py:224-233).
**Fix**: "Every /24 the forwarder carries, its own and the ones behind it, goes out the same tunnel as `dest dev wgN` with no gateway. The far kernel routes the inner packet onward." Example: "10.133.145.0/24  dev wg2            (SEA-2, relayed through SEA-1)"

**12. Where**: 12_Part_VIII §23 "The Sensor Platform", first paragraph
**Text**: "A role is not reserved to a node. Any machine on the pond can register for one — database or media, the two roles that exist. The election reads capability, not pedigree, so a well-provisioned box that is not itself a FrogNet node can host the historical FrogNet Region backing store or win the mediahost election."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: [POOL_IS_THE_LIVE_BLOCK_V1]: only live .1 FrogNetHost entries in the converged hosts block are candidates. A capability row whose writer is not in the block is dropped (core/frognet_service_hosts.py:236-246, _alive_ones :119-129). On a region [ELECT_NODES_ONLY_V1] excludes everything but nodes (discovery/elect.py:96-106).
**Fix**: "Roles are held by nodes. Every live FrogNetHost (.1) is a candidate for both roles that exist, database and media, and the election picks among them on capability. A box that is not a FrogNet node cannot hold a role. (The Communicator’s media server, comms-media, is not elected: it runs where it is started.)"

**13. Where**: 13_Part_IX §26, opening and "How a template is learned"
**Text**: "The first exchange of a structure goes raw — the whole HTTP request and answer — and both sides learn its template from it"
(and "That template is learned from the raw exchange a moment ago; nobody sends a template. From then on the structure is known on both sides")
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: Only the proxy stores templates (proxy/templates.py:440 is the only caller of store_templates). The peer's daemon answers REQ_RAW by running the request and returning RESP_RAW, and learns nothing (daemon/engine/session.py:1658-1717). The daemon looks templates up by opcode in its own node's local store (daemon/engine/execution.py:175-178; core/store.py:216, DB_CONFIG at 127.0.0.1). When that store has no template it answers "no templates for opcode", and the proxy re-bootstraps RAW.
**Fix**: "The first exchange of a structure goes raw — the whole HTTP request and answer — and the sending proxy learns its template from it. The far daemon uses the template its own node's proxy learned for the same key. Neither side sends a template. If the far side does not have one yet, it says so and the exchange goes raw again."

Checked: 118 claims; 13 problems.

---

# P3 — Parts X, XI, XII, XII½ (files 14–17) against the 2026-10-11 tree

Conversation tools available; used for history (chats 1f49d502, 304f955c, 22c2753a, 8f937a39).

---

**1. Where**: 14_Part_X intro paragraph, and §28 "The flagship extensions" (tic-tac-toe paragraph)
**Text**: "an early /game origin and a boardgame role were built and were removed on 25 September 2026"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: proxy/proxy_main.py:272 `[GAME_ORIGIN_REMOVED_V1] John, 2026-09-25` (the /game origin); discovery/sim/role_registry_lxmlfree_check.py:59 and simulation/role_registry_lxmlfree_check.py:59 `boardgame went with the games, 2026-09-29` (the role). The same sentence appears twice (line 3 and line 58).
**Fix**: "an early /game origin and a boardgame role were built; the origin was removed on 25 September 2026 and the role on 29 September 2026"

**2. Where**: 14_Part_X §28 "The flagship extensions" (SotF paragraph)
**Text**: "an adaptive ladder that steps quality down from full video toward audio and then text as the link degrades"
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: §33 of the same Part: "the running code has the video sizes and audio only". communicator/src/media/transcode.cpp:17-21 (SIZES, 1080p to 160x120 gray), server.hpp Viewer.target `SIZES.size() = audio only`. Below that there is no rung. No text rung exists.
**Fix**: "an adaptive ladder that steps the picture down, size by size, to audio alone as the link degrades"

**3. Where**: 14_Part_X §29 "Re-establishing Memory After a Split", first paragraph
**Text**: "Every role-bearing handler on every node in the island runs its lifecycle reconcile — hostReset — and each re-advertises its capability into the new space."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: core/role_publish.py:86-96. The watcher's module is RolePublishReconcile. Its hostReset() calls publish_all(), which calls each role handler's advertise() (role_publish.py:76). The handlers' own hostReset() is not on that path. §30 says this correctly: "The thing that republishes every role's capability on a reconcile is not a handler."
**Fix**: "On every node in the island the reconcile re-publishes every role's capability: one probe, then each role handler's advertise() into the new space."

**4. Where**: 14_Part_X §30 "The trigger is an observation, not a message"
**Text**: "The daemon runs a watcher that, every 15 seconds, resolves databasehost_control.frognet from /etc/hosts and compares the answer to the one it got last time. If the address is different, it reconciles."
**Verdict**: STALE (incomplete for the current deployment)
**Evidence**: core/role_publish.py:59-66 `[CAPABILITY_TUPLES_OFF_IN_REGION_V1]`: in region mode, publish_all() returns before probing or writing. discovery/live.py:812-817 `[REGION_MERGE_WRITES_NO_STORE_V1]`. On an FD&F region (the current deployment, r19-a15) the reconcile writes nothing. The election reads facts.capability instead (core/region_capability.py).
**Fix**: append "On an FD&F region the watcher still ticks, but the reconcile writes nothing: capability is the tunnel daemon's fact in the region, and the elections read it from there."

**5. Where**: 14_Part_X §31 "The same problem, wearing different clothes"
**Text**: "Discovery does it. The games do it. The floating database does it."
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: The Part's own intro says "FrogNet ships no games". proxy_main.py:272 (/game removed 2026-09-25); role_registry.py has only mediahost and databasehost.
**Fix**: "Discovery does it. Presence does it. The floating database does it."

**6. Where**: 14_Part_X §31 "The same problem" (paragraph beginning "The second is the ability")
**Text**: "one carrying our frames out to the mediahost, one carrying the call back"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: communicator/src/media/server.hpp:8-10 and server.cpp:55-73. The two connections are one audio plane and one video plane, declared by their first frame. Each connection both uploads (pump) and receives the fan (Conn is "this connection as a video viewer" and "as a video sender"). §33 of the same Part says "one for audio and one for video".
**Fix**: "one carrying audio and one carrying video, each both ways, between us and the media server"

**7. Where**: 14_Part_X §31 ("The first is the FrogNet-hosted Ribbit Region...") and §33 "Two planes, one handler"
**Text**: "The control plane is shared state in the FrogNet-hosted Ribbit Region, historically FrogNet Memory."
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: communicator/src/ram/comms_ram.cpp:4-17. The Communicator's control state (presence, chat, the five call-state tuples, media-host connection info) lives in comms-ram, the Communicator's own Ribbit RamHost (`vendor_id "comms"`, answers /comms-api, default listen 0.0.0.0:8800). It is found through the pond's where cell (fdf-comms.sh:8-9, 36). It is not the FrogNet default Region. The §31 sentence "The first is the FrogNet-hosted Ribbit Region, historically called FrogNet Memory, reached through the same shared-state API..." has the same fault.
**Fix** (§33): "The control plane is shared state in comms-ram, the Communicator's own Ribbit memory (port 8800, found through the pond's where cell)." (§31): "The first is the Communicator's shared memory, comms-ram — a Ribbit RAM host — reached through the same shared-state API as the rest of the application's control state."

**8. Where**: 14_Part_X §33 "Two planes, one handler", last paragraph
**Text**: "if it cannot, it drops it and reaches for the next, newer frame. The send never blocks because it is never called when it would."
**Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
**Evidence**: communicator/src/fnav/plane.hpp:6-8, 42-44. Room is checked before the first byte, and a frame that cannot start is dropped. But audio is retried twice, 8 ms apart, and a keyframe three times; other video is never retried. A part-written frame is finished within 250 ms or completed with abort padding. The same over-statement is in §31 "The data path": "if the socket is not ready this instant, the frame is dropped".
**Fix**: "if it cannot, an ordinary video frame is dropped and the next, newer one takes its place; audio gets two more tries 8 ms apart and a keyframe three, and a frame already started is finished within 250 ms or closed with padding. Nothing ever waits longer than that."

**9. Where**: 14_Part_X §33 "What the ladder became"
**Text**: "holds the newest keyframe until it can be sent, the newer overwriting the older."
**Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
**Evidence**: communicator/src/media/server.hpp:12-14 and :82 `HELD_KEY_MAX_S = 3.0`. A held keyframe waits at most 3 s. An unanchored viewer with nothing held asks the sender for a keyframe, at most once per 3 s per sender.
**Fix**: "holds the newest keyframe, for at most three seconds, until it can be sent, the newer overwriting the older; a viewer left without one asks the sender for a fresh keyframe."

**10. Where**: 15_Part_XI §35f "Concurrency does not go away"
**Text**: "a tuple written with own=True belongs to the writing process and is removed when that process exits, so a departed writer leaves nothing behind."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: core/frognet_tuples.py:341-345 and 537-545. Cleanup runs from atexit and "fires on normal exit / graceful signals, NOT on SIGKILL, power loss, or a crash". It is best-effort, and failures are recorded in _CLEANUP_FAILED. The guarantee the comment names is the reaper. But etc/systemd/system/frognet-reaper.service:34 runs /opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator/frognet_reaper.py, which is not in the tree. What remains is age-out by ts.
**Fix**: "a tuple written with own=True belongs to the writing process and is deleted when that process exits normally; a writer that crashes or is killed leaves its tuple until readers age it out by its timestamp."

**11. Where**: 15_Part_XI §35f "Concurrency does not go away"
**Text**: "The rest of that list is where the memory model is going, and section 61 carries it with the other honest edges."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: 23_Part_XVI §61 names held reads and per-Region policy. It does not name versioning, leases, transactions, optimistic updates or single-writer semantics. It also says the later Ribbit work "demonstrated ... truth ownership, governed acceptance, held views, ... expiration and durable records". That conflicts with "design intent rather than ... what is shipped today".
**Fix**: "The rest of that list is where the memory model is going; section 61 records what the later Ribbit work has already shown (held reads, ownership, expiration) and what remains open."

**12. Where**: 16_Part_XII intro, first paragraph
**Text**: "Each vendor's memory is one listener, its RAM host, on one path (/<vendor>-api)"
**Verdict**: WRONG (overstated)
**Evidence**: ribbit/cpp/include/ram_host.hpp:39-46: `/<vendor_id>-api` is the default, and a vendor may override api_path(). The FD&F RAM manager named in the same paragraph answers `/FawcettInnovations.RINA.ram_interface.php` (etc/systemd/system/fdf-ram-frognet.service:9; fdf-hub API constant).
**Fix**: "Each vendor's memory is one listener, its RAM host, on one path (/<vendor>-api by default; the FD&F RAM manager's is /FawcettInnovations.RINA.ram_interface.php)"

**13. Where**: 16_Part_XII §36 "The one surface, named honestly"
**Text**: "Two things on the droplet face the internet."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: Later in the same chapter, the scan shows Apache on 80/443 on that host. fdf/bin/fdf-services.sh:5-14, 33 runs on the droplet and opens fdf 8840, kart 8799 and comms 8800/8994 on the public address. Part X says comms-media "runs on the droplet".
**Fix**: "Two FrogNet things on the droplet always face the internet." After the FD&F sentence, add: "When fdf-services.sh runs there, the services it starts face it too: the Communicator's comms-ram on 8800 and comms-media on 8994, and the kart and fdf memories on 8799 and 8840. Apache on 80 and 443 serves the website."

**14. Where**: 16_Part_XII §36 opening, and the "worked example" paragraph on enrollment
**Text**: "it opens no tunnel port: it dials the broker." / "Its tunnel daemon then polls my-channels."
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: internet_tunnels_v3/region.py:1-25. In region mode (the current deployment) the tunnel daemon answers the broker's paths from the FD&F region. It brings up one channel per live hub, which is fdf-hub, "Replaces frognet_broker_v4 for a region" (fdf-hub:6-9). It is driven by fdf-events lines with "No timer".
**Fix**: "it opens no tunnel port: it dials the broker, or on an FD&F region the region's hub." / "Its tunnel daemon then polls my-channels (on an FD&F region it reads the hub from the region instead, on each region event)."

**15. Where**: 16_Part_XII §36 "What a scan actually returns"
**Text**: "it is not a registered service and nothing about it is guessable."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: broker installer install_frognet_broker.sh:246 `PORT=18257` is the default when --port is not given. deploy_broker_v4.sh, frognet_setup_v4_helper.bash:89 and frognet_setup_v4_api.php:120 also default to 18257. The book prints the number.
**Fix**: "it is not a registered service; 18257 is the installer's default, and an operator who wants it unguessable passes another --port."

**16. Where**: 16_Part_XII §36 "Take the broker. Now show me the pivot."
**Text**: "Its tunnel private keys live only in its kernel interfaces and are never written to disk."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: broker/opt/frognet_broker_v4/broker_namespace_v4.py:381-392. Each private key is written to a tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile (delete=False), passed to `wg set ... private-key <file>`, then unlinked. The database *_privkey columns are written empty (frognet_broker_v4.py:1360).
**Fix**: "Its tunnel private keys are kept in its kernel interfaces; each touches a temporary file only for the moment `wg set` reads it, and the database stores none."

**17. Where**: 16_Part_XII §36 "Take the broker...", paragraph ending the kernel-forwarding argument
**Text**: "The broker has nothing to surrender because it kept nothing."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: The droplet that runs the broker also runs the FD&F RAM manager: etc/systemd/system/fdf-ram-frognet.service, rina-host on 0.0.0.0:51888. The settled ruling says all FD&F data goes through the RAM manager. Root on that host therefore holds the region's live memory. The book's own line "If the same host carries an FD&F RAM manager, its region file goes with it" concedes the store exists, and ORACLE 2 does not account for it.
**Fix**: "The broker process has nothing to surrender because it kept nothing. The FD&F RAM manager is another matter: on a host that carries one, root on the host holds the region's memory, and that is an endpoint compromise in the terms of ORACLE 2."

**18. Where**: 16_Part_XII §39 "Encryption Is a Dial You Hold", third paragraph
**Text**: "Between sites, each site's hop to the broker is WireGuard. The broker decrypts, forwards in the kernel and re-encrypts"
**Verdict**: STALE
**Evidence**: In region mode the inter-site WireGuard concentrator is fdf-hub, one wg interface per region with one peer per live member (fdf-hub:6-20). internet_tunnels_v3/region.py, my-channels -> the hub. The same decrypt/forward/re-encrypt seam sits at the hub.
**Fix**: "Between sites, each site's hop to the broker — or, on an FD&F region, to the region's hub — is WireGuard. That machine decrypts, forwards in the kernel and re-encrypts"

**19. Where**: 17_Part_XII½ "The rules, as one table", intro paragraph
**Text**: "and the rest are recorded in docs/FNAV-SPEC.md"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: I grepped ribbit/examples/communicator src/, python/ and docs/ for each of the table's tags. Sixteen of them are in none of those, and the only file anywhere in TREE that contains them is the book builder (opt/frognet_semantic/docs/magnum_croakus/build_magnum.js). The sixteen are THE_KEY_IS_THE_NAME_NOT_THE_DECORATION, A_MEMBER_IS_PROOF_THE_CALL_EXISTS, LEAVE_MEANS_FORGET, RESIDENT_ONCE_NEEDS_A_RESIDENT_VALUE, A_WRITER_CHECKS_ITS_OWN_OUTPUT, A_CHANNEL_BELONGS_TO_ONE_CODEX, A_CEILING_MUST_BE_ABLE_TO_LIFT, A_SENDER_BOUND_ALONE_CANNOT_CLIMB, SAY_WHY_THE_LIST_IS_EMPTY, DIAGNOSTICS_GO_WHERE_THEY_CAN_BE_READ, A_SILENT_EXIT_IS_A_BUG_REPORT, SAY_WHICH_BYTES_ARE_RUNNING, A_FINGERPRINT_IS_OF_THE_CODE_NOT_THE_BYTES, THE_BUILD_SAYS_WHAT_IT_PACKAGED, A_VERIFIER_THAT_CANNOT_RUN_IS_WORSE_THAN_NONE and SELF_CALLS_MUST_EXIST. The FNAV-SPEC.md path is under ribbit/examples/communicator/docs/.
**Fix**: "a few of the tags survive in the source (FOUR_TUPLES, ABSENCE_IS_NOT_A_MEASUREMENT, READ_A_COUNTER_NOBODY_ELSE_DRAINS), about half are recorded in the Communicator's docs/FNAV-SPEC.md, and the rest survive only here."

# P4 — Parts XIII, XIII½, XIV, XV (files 18–21), independent check against tree1011

Conversation tools were available. Code under /home/claude/tree1011 is the reference throughout. `CDIR` below means opt/frognet_semantic/ribbit/examples/communicator.

---

**Where**: 21_Part_XV_The_Communicator.md, §46 The pieces
**Text**: "It shares nothing with the C++ but the rows in comms-ram, the wire to comms-media and the rules"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: CDIR/python/README.md:3-5: the Python version "reaches the memory through `libcomms_tuples` (the platform's client library as a C ABI), which the C++ build produces". The library is built from CDIR/src/pyabi/comms_tuples.cpp, and build-windows.ps1:52 builds the `comms_tuples` target.
**Fix**: "It shares with the C++ only the platform's client library (libcomms_tuples, built by the C++ build), the rows in comms-ram, the wire to comms-media and the rules."

**Where**: 21, §46 Building and running it
**Text**: "Every program says what it is first: comms-app prints [app] build comms-app-2026-10-10-r3 and carries it in the window title; the phone logs comms-android-2026-10-11-r5."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: comms-media prints only "[media] listening" and has no build tag (CDIR/src/media/server.cpp:32, src/media/main.cpp). comms-feed prints "[feed] build ..." (src/feed/main.cpp:83). comms-ram logs "comms-ram communicator 2.2 (...)" (ribbit/cpp/src/ram_host.cpp:404, src/ram/comms_ram.cpp COMMS_RAM_VERSION).
**Fix**: "comms-app and comms-feed print their build first ([app] build comms-app-2026-10-10-r3, [feed] build ...), and the window title carries it. comms-ram logs its version (communicator 2.2). comms-media prints no build. The phone logs comms-android-2026-10-11-r5."

**Where**: 21, §46 Building and running it
**Text**: "The libraries are FFmpeg (libavcodec, libswscale, libavdevice), libvpx, libopus, PortAudio and Qt 6."
**Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
**Evidence**: CMakeLists.txt:62-66 also requires libavutil, libavformat, liblz4 and libcrypto (OpenSSL). build.sh:13-16 installs all of them.
**Fix**: "The libraries are FFmpeg (libavcodec, libavformat, libavutil, libswscale, libavdevice), libvpx, libopus, PortAudio and Qt 6, plus LZ4 and OpenSSL's libcrypto for the Ribbit platform."

**Where**: 21, §46 Presence, the lobby and chat
**Text**: "There is no logout and no delete: stale is gone."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: CDIR/src/comms/room.cpp:151 writes presence with own=true. ribbit/defaults/api/tuples.hpp:29-33 defines own=true as "this client removes the tuple when it closes". room.cpp:58 calls `c_.cleanup_owned()` on shutdown, and tuples.cpp:23 calls it in the destructor.
**Fix**: "There is no logout. A participant that closes removes its own presence row. One that vanishes stops rewriting it, and the row is stale after 15 seconds."

**Where**: 21, §46 Presence, the lobby and chat
**Text**: "an idle room makes a handful of requests a minute."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: CDIR/STATUS.md:35 (test_room_watch on a real comms-ram): "an idle room 8-20 requests over 21 s". That is about 23 to 57 a minute. The presence heartbeat alone is 12 writes a minute (room.hpp:37 HEARTBEAT_S=5).
**Fix**: "an idle room makes 8 to 20 requests in 21 seconds (it made 4 reads a second per window when it polled)."

**Where**: 21, §46 What the media server does
**Text**: "Audio is never what is given up while video is still going to that viewer."
**Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED (no code enforces it)
**Evidence**: CDIR/src/media/server.cpp:47 puts every accepted connection, audio planes included, in debt mode. fan_audio (server.cpp) sends with Plane::send(SendKind::Audio). With no room after 3 tries, or with debt outstanding, that returns Dropped (fnav/plane.cpp:101,103,114). No code compares this with video delivery. The claim appears only in a header comment (server.hpp:10-11).
**Fix**: "Audio is never shed by choice: the server's sizing and shedding run only on video planes. An audio block that a viewer's socket will not take after three tries is dropped, like any other frame."

**Where**: 21, §47 Why the keyframe is broken up, and what rides in the gaps
**Text**: "Between the segments go the deltas, which keep the picture current, and the audio, which keeps the conversation alive."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: fnav/plane.cpp:177-199 (send_video) sends every VSEG piece of a frame in one loop before it returns. The next delta is encoded only after that, on the same thread (comms/call.cpp video_tx). A new frame from the same source while pieces are held makes the reassembler discard them (fnav/segment.hpp:12-13). Audio has its own socket. FNAV-SPEC.md:20-21: "so the shared uplink drains between pieces and audio is serviced in the gaps".
**Fix**: "Between the segments the link drains, and the audio, on its own socket, is serviced in the gaps. The deltas follow the keyframe."
Also change the figure line `interleaved    [kf][a][d][kf][a][d][kf][a][kf] -> display` to `segmented      [kf][a][kf][a][kf][a][kf] -> display   then [d][d]...`.

**Where**: 21, §51 What the media path cost / Reading shared state, closing paragraph
**Text**: "which is what the simulator in the next part is for."
**Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
**Evidence**: The next part is Part XV½ (22_Part_XV_Memory_Where_Messages_Were_Expected.md, distributed training). The simulator is Part XIII (18_Part_XIII_Proving_It_The_Simulator.md).
**Fix**: "which is what the simulator in Part XIII is for."

**Where**: 21, §51 How the Ladder Actually Decides (the stamp paragraph)
**Text**: "Each sender burns its frame count and its wall clock at capture into the bottom-left of the picture"
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: CDIR/STATUS.md (r3 section): "Each desktop sender burns `#<frame> HH:MM:SS.mmm` ... The phone's own sender does not stamp (its picture is GL-rendered into the encoder's surface)." The stamping code is src/comms/stamp.hpp and is used only in call.cpp video_tx.
**Fix**: "Each desktop sender (comms-app, comms-feed) burns its frame count and its wall clock at capture into the bottom-left of the picture; the phone's sender does not"

**Where**: 18_Part_XIII_Proving_It_The_Simulator.md, §41 Where it stops (NOT DEMONSTRATED)
**Text**: "The wire has no loss, no latency and no jitter."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: simulation/transport_sim_tier.py:28-36,191-213 models latency, jitter, bandwidth and outage per direction (WireMedium, NetworkParams). simulation/AI_README.md: "Loss, jitter, bandwidth and outage exist in the transport tier over a single link. You cannot attach 600 kbps to the New York leg of a five-site pond."
**Fix**: "No topology edge carries loss, latency or jitter. The transport tier models latency, jitter, bandwidth and outage over a single link only, and the two are not yet joined."

**Where**: 19_Part_XIII_Telemetry_Write_Then_Read.md, §42c What to take from it
**Text**: "The Monitor is perhaps two thousand lines, and most of it is drawing."
**Verdict**: WRONG
**Evidence**: `wc -l` on usr/local/bin/frognet_monitor_py/*.py without the two test files gives 2,532 lines (2,896 with them).
**Fix**: "The Monitor is about two and a half thousand lines, and most of it is drawing."

## 24_Specification_C_FD_F_Farinacci_Day_Fawcett.md

**1.**
- **Where**: §63 The FD&F Participant Model, second paragraph
- **Text**: "framed FNW1 and compressed with BLDC-1 against the primed template store (fdf.prime)"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (for the phone and the Windows PC) / INCONSISTENT (with the BLDC-1 ruling)
- **Evidence**: TREE/opt/fdf/src/frognet-android/member/src/main/kotlin/com/fawcettinnovations/fdf/member/Wire.kt:4-7 — "[FNWP_RAW_MEMBER_V1] ... A member only ever sends REQ_RAW; the RAM manager's ServerEngine answers every REQ_RAW with RESP_RAW ... No templates, no SAME cache, no differencing". The Kotlin member is what the phone and the Windows bridge run (windows/fdf-bridge.cmd:32 runs com.fawcettinnovations.fdf.cli.MainKt). Only the C++ fdf-client (frogram Session, platform/ribbit_cpp/fnwp_engine.hpp:119 RIBBIT_TEMPLATES) uses BLDC-1. Also: fdf.prime primes the templates (tools/apply_primed_templates.py: "the first request of a primed shape goes REQ_FULL/REQ_DIFF, not REQ_RAW"); the reference state for FULL/DIFF/SAME is each end's semantic cache, not the template store (settled ruling).
- **Fix**: "framed FNW1. The C++ fdf-client compresses with BLDC-1, its templates primed from fdf.prime and its reference state each end's semantic cache; the Kotlin member on the phone and the Windows PC sends REQ_RAW, uncompressed."

**2.**
- **Where**: §66 The Region, second paragraph
- **Text**: "Each region event is one reconcile; nothing polls."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: rinab/src/fdf_client.cpp:384-388 `[MAPS_ASSERTED_V1]` — a thread sleeps FDF_ASSERT_SEC (default 30) and re-reads map_list, re-registering what the region lacks; fdf/bin/fdf-facts:150,160-172 — `_uplink_prober` pings out of the uplink every FDF_UPLINK_PROBE_SEC (3 s). frognet/opt/frognet_semantic/internet_tunnels_v3/poll.py:967 `[HANDSHAKE_POLL_100MS_V1]` polls the handshake at bring-up.
- **Fix**: "Each region event is one reconcile. Two timers remain: fdf-client checks its mappings every 30 s and re-registers any the region lacks, and fdf-facts probes the uplink every 3 s."

# P6 — Appendices A–M, Afterword, Glossary (files 26_–39_) against tree1011

I had the conversation tools. I checked the code at /home/claude/tree1011. I unpacked the broker bundle (opt/frognet_semantic/broker/full_broker.tgz) and read it.

---

**1. Where**: 26 Appendix A, "Measured every time"
- **Text**: "Capability rows do not age."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with the code)
- **Evidence**:
  - The service election does not age rows (core/frognet_role_elect.py:118-128, 246-249).
  - The merge's floor election does. discovery/hosts.py:253-277 (`_capability_index`, called from `select_database_host`, which orchestrate.py:179 runs on every merge) drops any row older than FROGNET_BALLOT_MAX_AGE_S (default 1800 s, hosts.py:259) when it is off a region.
- **Fix**: "The service election never ages a capability row. The merge's highest-.1 floor (discovery/hosts.py) still ignores a row older than FROGNET_BALLOT_MAX_AGE_S, 1800 s by default."

**2. Where**: 26 Appendix A, "What it returns", IDENTITY
- **Text**: "lan_ip              this node's .1 (from getFrogNet.bash)"
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
- **Evidence**: usr/local/bin/frognet_capability_probe.sh:310-331. If getFrogNet.bash gives no 10.x address, lan_ip becomes the highest 10.x .1 on any non-wg interface, then any 10.x address, and finally 127.0.0.1.
- **Fix**: "lan_ip              this node's .1, from getFrogNet.bash; if that gives none, the highest 10.x .1 on a non-wg interface, else 127.0.0.1"

**3. Where**: 26 Appendix A, worked example
- **Text**: "These are static properties only; nothing that moves with load is scored."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (true of the handler, not of the floor)
- **Evidence**:
  - core/database_handler.py:59-174 is static, as the book says.
  - The merge floor that runs before it, discovery/hosts.py:456-491 (`_rank`), scores disk_write_mbps, disk_fsync_ms and cpu_bench_total. It falls back to mem_available_kb when mem_total_kb is missing.
- **Fix**: Add: "(The merge's highest-.1 floor, which only guarantees that a databasehost line exists, still ranks on measured disk and CPU; the service election replaces its choice whenever capability rows exist.)"

**4. Where**: 27 Appendix B, worked example paragraph (the same claim is in 28 Appendix C, Probes row)
- **Text**: "computed once and served from cache in well under a millisecond"
- **Verdict**: UNSUPPORTED
- **Evidence**:
  - var/www/html/frognet_echo.php runs a shell (backtick) of getFrogNet.bash on every call; the script reads /etc/sentinels/getFrogNet.cache.
  - No measurement exists in the code or in John's chats. The figure was written by Claude in chat fd26b870 (2026-06-30) and never measured.
- **Fix**: "computed once and served from a cache file, each sub-probe bounded by a two-second timeout." In Appendix C, replace "in well under a millisecond from cache" with "from a cache file".

**5. Where**: 28 Appendix C, "Broker, tunnels & membership", frognet-pond row
- **Text**: "Also list, status, members, allocations, remove, bootstrap."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - Those are the verbs of frognet-pond-v4 (broker/opt/frognet_broker_v4/frognet-pond-v4:266-273).
  - The shipped /usr/local/bin/frognet-pond in full_broker.tgz (payload/usr/local/bin/frognet-pond:3-8 and 19-35) says it replaces frognet-pond-v4. Its verbs are ping, list, status, create, nodes, remove <pond> <chorus> <node>, forget, block, unblock, db, result. There is no members, allocations or bootstrap. Appendix J already uses `frognet-pond nodes`.
- **Fix**: "Also ping, list, status, nodes, remove, forget <pubkey>, block/unblock, db, result."

**6. Where**: 31 Appendix F, "Twenty-one bytes", paragraph after the table
- **Text**: "The identifier is a short hash over the destination, the operation, and the reference values it was measured against"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (the same appendix, "How SAME is proven", defines "the identifier" as the 16-byte HMAC same_id)
- **Evidence**: The text describes req_hash (proxy/transport_semantic.py:662-668: target, path, opcode, reference, truncated to 16 bytes). same_id is the HMAC (core/semcache_id.py).
- **Fix**: "The request hash a REPEAT carries is sixteen bytes over the destination, the operation, and the reference values it was measured against"

**7. Where**: 35 Appendix J, Step 1, "If it does not."
- **Text**: "the first phase is the only one that wants the internet"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: usr/local/bin/installer/frognet_install.sh:791-821. Phase C rebuilds the venv with pip from the network whenever the venv's architecture does not match the machine, or when --rebuild-venv is given.
- **Fix**: "the first phase wants the internet, and so does the venv rebuild in phase C when the release was built on another architecture"

**8. Where**: 35 Appendix J, Step 1
- **Text**: "It asks for the database password (the same on every node in the pond) and whether this node is a gateway."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (incomplete)
- **Evidence**: frognet_install.sh:1553-1643. With a wireless interface and no --no-ssid-projection, it also asks whether to project an SSID, which AP interface to use when there is more than one, and the Wi-Fi passphrase. It then asks for confirmation.
- **Fix**: "It asks for the database password (the same on every node in the pond), whether to project an access point and its passphrase (unless --no-ssid-projection or --wifi-psk is given), and whether this node is a gateway."

**9. Where**: 35 Appendix J, Step 5
- **Text**: "With no camera, comms-app sends a synthetic picture (--synthetic); --no-audio runs without sound."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (flag form)
- **Evidence**: ribbit/examples/communicator/src/app/main.cpp:8, 365 and 823. `--synthetic` takes a size argument (WxH, default 1280x720). A bare `--synthetic` swallows the next argument.
- **Fix**: "With no camera, comms-app sends a synthetic picture (--synthetic WxH, 1280x720 by default); --no-audio runs without sound."

**10. Where**: 35 Appendix J, Step 6, "The two numbers, and why both matter." (and "On the numbers.")
- **Text**: "it is a real measurement from a run across a 900 MHz radio with a one-megabit ceiling"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT
- **Evidence**:
  - The 987 → 637 → 450 → 850 (17.5 fps) walk is the "Bandwidth Experiments" session: Part XV, file 21, calls it "the link starved by hand", August 2026.
  - Chat 1f49d502: "22 fps is the baseline first run — 1280×720 bidirectional over 900 MHz … 17.5 fps is the ladder walk … Label them baseline run and ladder walk."
  - Chat 304f955c identifies it as the live two-way starve test with the slider.
- **Fix**: "it is a real measurement from the ladder walk, the link starved by hand with the bandwidth slider, not a bench estimate." In "On the numbers.", change "one recorded run on one radio" to "one recorded run".

**11. Where**: 36 Appendix K, "What is not a FrogNet limit", paragraph after the table
- **Text**: "a default of four in an older daemon, a default of four in the passcode helper, and a value of five thousand written into each node's configuration at install"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - The first four is in the retired setup script, not a daemon: usr/local/bin/frognet-tunnel-setup.sh:88 and 100.
  - The passcode helper is frognet-create-passcode.sh:37.
  - The 5000 is written by frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh:270 when it creates tunnel.conf at registration, and by frognet-config-v4:111. frognet_install.sh writes no MAX_TUNNELS.
  - Nothing reads it: no Python hit, and no hit in either broker copy.
- **Fix**: "a default of four in the retired setup script (frognet-tunnel-setup.sh), a default of four in the passcode helper, and a value of five thousand that frognet-tunnel-setup-v3.sh writes into tunnel.conf when it creates it"

**12. Where**: 38 Appendix L, body
- **Text**: "Not generated in this build. No source tree was found"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (still unfixed; it was item 35 of the 10-08 verify)
- **Evidence**: The appendix promises a generated list and prints the builder's failure stub instead. The tree exists at /home/claude/tree1011.
- **Fix**: Rebuild with FROGNET_SRC=/home/claude/tree1011, or drop the appendix.

**13. Where**: 39 Glossary, "broker"
- **Text**: "It holds no private keys"
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**: broker/opt/frognet_broker_v4/frognet_broker_v4.py:198, 204 and 1307-1356. Every tunnel row stores host_privkey and join_privkey, which the broker generates for its own tunnel ends (`ns._wg_genkey()`).
- **Fix**: "It holds no node's private key; the private keys of its own tunnel ends are in broker.db."

**14. Where**: 39 Glossary, "broker"
- **Text**: "so it sees inter-site traffic in the clear. Reachable by IP from every node. It DOES carry the inter-site data path — cross-site traffic transits it — and inter-site traffic is in the clear at that seam."
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (the same claim is stated twice in one entry)
- **Fix**: "so it carries the inter-site data path and sees that traffic in the clear. Reachable by IP from every node."

**15. Where**: 39 Glossary, "hub"
- **Text**: "The FD&F region's forwarding point on the droplet (StreamingFrog, 165.232.153.144:51888)."
- **Verdict**: WRONG
- **Evidence**:
  - TCP 51888 is the RAM manager, rina-host (deliverables/etc-droplet/systemd/system/fdf-ram-frognet.service:2 and 9; fdf-region-join:9).
  - The hub is fdf-hub, the region's WireGuard concentrator. It runs as its own service with its own UDP --listen port (fdf-hub.service:10; opt/fdf/bin/fdf-hub docstring). It has one wg interface per region and is addressed 10.253.253.254/16.
- **Fix**: "hub — fdf-hub, the region's WireGuard concentrator on the droplet (StreamingFrog, 165.232.153.144): one wg interface for the region, one peer per live member. The RAM manager on the same droplet is TCP 51888."

**16. Where**: 39 Glossary, "WireGuard"
- **Text**: "its AllowedIPs is always 10.0.0.0/8."
- **Verdict**: WRONG (on an FD&F region)
- **Evidence**:
  - The node side is 10.0.0.0/8 (internet_tunnels_v3/region.py:44).
  - The FD&F hub gives each member peer only the /24s that member's verified mappings and facts claim, and keeps its own prefix-to-peer table (opt/fdf/bin/fdf-hub docstring, "prefixes", "ownership").
- **Fix**: "its AllowedIPs on a node is 10.0.0.0/8; on an FD&F hub each member peer carries only that member's own /24s."

**17. Where**: 39 Glossary, "capability probe" and "advertise"
- **Text**: "each role's advertiser publishes it as its <role>/capability tuple, which the election scores"
- **Verdict**: INCONSISTENT (with Appendix A and the code)
- **Evidence**:
  - core/role_publish.py:58-65 and discovery/live.py:815-818 do not publish in region mode.
  - internet_tunnels_v3/poll.py:2372-2400 writes the blob as facts.capability, and core/region_capability.py feeds the elections from those facts.
- **Fix**: In "capability probe": "…off a region, each role's advertiser publishes it as its <role>/capability tuple; on an FD&F region the tunnel daemon writes it as the member's capability fact. Either is what the election scores (Appendix A)." In "advertise", add: "Off a region only."

Checked: 196 claims; 17 problems.
